lease_until: <driver sets this>

## State of attack

**Formulation.** Hard-constraint block RG for SU(2) Wilson on a periodic 4-torus, with the quaternion-mean average with waypoints and a field-chosen base point (Text 1, 5.1, 5.4). This map is defined on the whole compact field space, it pushes Haar exactly to Haar, it gives a Markov kernel mu_W on the fields, and its linearisation is exactly the paired-contour Q of (R4). The induction is I(k) of Text 3.3, **level-only**: r_k is one function of the current field, equal to a small-field form on the regular region Om(V) times a large-field factor G_k(V) <= exp(-c_F eps_0 Y_k(V) / g_k^2), with Y_k the l^1 norm of the rough plaquettes. There are no frozen fine variables and no histories. Rough cubes where the next field is regular go into the R sector of Text 4.7, a hard-core gas in the fluctuation integral, which is never evaluated at a complex coarse field. The vacuum is subtracted exactly by rho_k(trivial coarse field); with a source this is the counterterm E of UV-CT (Text 6).

**The organising principle, found this round.** Two kinds of constant must be kept apart (Text 3.1, 4.5). *Linear* constants, of the abelian map at the trivial background, are exact or absolute, and free of L and k: ||Q_str||_{l^4} = 1, ||Q_str||_{l^2} <= 1 / L, Lemma 7.1, Lemma 10.1, (5.4), the Morrey and Agmon rates. Only these enter a margin or a contraction. *Nonlinear* constants, the radius and vertices of the map and of F_k beyond second order, may depend on L, and must not depend on k: Theorem G' gives that with a soft Taylor bound at each level and a recursion that cannot compound. Since eps_0 and gamma are chosen after L, nonlinear corrections lose every comparison. This removed most of what I had been calling hard.

**Order of constants.** Text 3.1: c_F absolute; L large; M polylogarithmic in L; R_t = A L log L, entering only nonlinear constants; then the nonlinear constants C(L, R_t); then eps_0 small against them; alpha = c_2 eps_0; p(g); the source radius rho; then gamma. (FLOW) is no longer a hypothesis on L: Proposition 8.4.

**What is derived and what is believed.**
- Derived line by line: Lemma 3.2a and the tiling (T1) to (T5), new at this stop; Lemma 1.1, Corollary 1.2, identity (1.2), Lemma 2.1, the linearisation in 5.1, identity (5.1), Theorem 5.5, Theorem 4.2, Lemmas 7.1, 7.2, Theorem 7.3, the star removal for q_k, the symbol bound QQ^* >= 1/3, (8.1), b, Lemma 10.1, **Lemmas 4.5a, 4.5b, 4.5c, Proposition 8.4, the identity (4.2) of the excess, the exact split and Jacobi's minor in (CUT) item 1 and Lemma 4.4b**, and the library proofs of mine (ultralocal cluster expansion, path expansion, lattice Sobolev and box gauge, lattice HLS and Gehring).
- Derived at the level of each step, given the items named there: the local centre V^# with rough sets and the density step in 4.7 (new at this stop, and the rough sets are read by nobody); Theorem G' on a simply connected patch, Lemmas 4.5f, 4.5g, 4.5h (abelian), Proposition 4.5i, Lemma 8.5, Proposition 8.6, Theorem 4.3, (4.3), Lemma 4.4b, Lemmas 4.7a, 4.7b, 4.7c and the recursion in 4.7, Proposition 11.1, the Wilson term 8.7, the chart of (CUT) item 1 with (REL), (WOOD), (STAB), (TAIL), stages (i), (ii) of 4.7, (OL) in 8.3, the source induction 6.1, the step in order 4.8, **and, new in this round and read by nobody yet, Lemma 4.5k (TREE), (EI) in tree form, the closure of the polymer norm, and (TAIL) in tree form**.
- Derived abelian: Lemma 4.5j on good patterns (the lift contracts amplitudes in a gauge adapted bond by bond). Lemma 4.5f and library Lemma 7 have new proofs by charts at the vertices of the cell lattice, on good patterns. Library: Lemma 8 (pointwise gauge under a Morrey bound), and the cluster expansion with activities in an anchored Banach algebra.
- That labels what is written, and it does not say that a checker has read it. Three more readers of the same kind read this file at this stop; see below. A first hostile reader (same model, fresh, no memory of the derivation, so not independent evidence in the sense of a gate) read Text 3, 4.4, 4.6, 4.7, 4.8 and reported twelve defects. Two were at the level of a route: **(EI), and with it the far hole sets and the far expansion of R polymers, had decay in the farthest distance or in |X|, and the cluster library needs a tree.** So my statement of the last stop, that no item is at the level of a route, was wrong.
- **Theorem I is a target. It is not proved.**
- Numerics. (i) `checks/hard-core--independent_defect_gain.py`, earliest line-averaged map, flat background: PASS-SHEET with max S = 1.000 at L = 3, 5, 7; single links at L = 3, 5 only, since L = 7 was stopped, so PASS-GENERIC is not evaluated at L = 7. **Those runs did not hold the numeric lock:** another push held it, my acquire failed, and I had piped its output through tail, which hid the failure. (ii) `checks/hard-core--independent_lift_form.py`, under a confirmed lock: see Lemma 10.1. (iii) `checks/hard-core--independent_field_ineq.py`, map D3 with the older rule threshold 0.5, under a confirmed lock, at L = 3, 5: PASS-55, max Ratio 1.000 and 0.999 against bounds 12.6 and 15.2, in the SHEET family; CHARGE exact to 2e-16; RULE-EFFECT below. These families can falsify (5.5). They do not confirm it on all fields. On the final rule: see the end of Text 5.
- (xi) `checks/hard-core--independent_bricks.py`, under a confirmed lock, exact: PASS-A, PASS-B, PASS-C at d = 2, 3, 4, on all 7, 46, 485 partitions and all unions and subcubes. Construction test on small tori: FAIL-D as registered on the first run, by an error of the test, which cut the torus as an interval with a seam; after the fix PASS-D, on two levels at d = 2, L = 5, 7, and d = 3, L = 3, with no non-guillotine vertex, at most d + 1 tiles at a vertex, and exact nesting. That is a bookkeeping test on small sizes.
- (x) `checks/hard-core--independent_shelling.py`, under a confirmed lock, exact: every component of every good pattern has a shelling, after adding one class to the bad ones (FAIL-SHELL as registered, on that class only). `checks/hard-core--independent_fill_trap.py`: NO-TRAP, as logged below.
- (ix) `checks/hard-core--independent_patterns.py`, `_patterns_fast.py`, `_patterns_rel.py`, under a confirmed lock: exact table of local patterns, PASS-Q3, PASS-PI1. The fill: PASS-RATIO, PASS-D (distance at most 2), PASS-R (ratio at most 24.7, with no growth with the side, at sides 6, 8, 10), and for small cells next to a large good set, PASS-G (a refined good pattern stays good, in every run), PASS-DREL (distance at most 2). Hill climbs of a few thousand moves on tori of side at most 10: these can only fail to break (FILL), and they do not prove it.
- (viii) `checks/hard-core--independent_lift_l1.py`, under a confirmed lock: sup-l^1 amplitude of the lift in the Feynman gauge of the chart, P_L = 0.94 to 1.11 at L = 9, P_L L = 8.5 to 10 and still rising; **NEITHER-PL**, against criteria that turned out to ask for more than is needed (Lemma 4.5j).
- (vii) `checks/hard-core--independent_lift_scan.py`, under a confirmed lock: all 217 vertex patterns (of 402 classes under the group of order 384) that carry a coarse plaquette, L = 5, 7, random coarse data: **PASS-SCAN**, s_est >= 1.113. Random data give lower estimates of phi, so this is weak evidence at each pattern. Second stage, the linear program on the six lowest, L = 5, 7, 9, 2000 plaquettes: **PASS-LP**, s_lp = 0.79, 1.03, 0.91, 1.22, 1.08, 0.90. The lowest is a removed snake of five orthants, 1000-0000-0001-0011-0111, with phi = 0.71, 0.55, 0.44. So the true exponent on some pattern is below 1, and phi L is still rising there at L = 9. The linear program was not run on all 217, so the worst pattern for it may be another.
- (vi) `checks/hard-core--independent_wilson_b.py`, under a confirmed lock: FAIL-WARD against a threshold I had not computed, with the deficit exactly the zero-mode term; PASS-B and PASS-B2, ratio 0.974 at N = 72 (Text 8.7).
- (v) `checks/hard-core--independent_lift_sup.py`, under a confirmed lock: WEAK-SIGMA, then, with a wider scan of plaquettes, WEAK2-SIGMA, s2 = 0.81 to 1.64, then with 4000 plaquettes at the worst pattern PASS3, s3 = 1.02: phi = 3.5 / L at L <= 11 (Text 4.5h).
- (iv) `checks/hard-core--independent_coer_g.py` and `_chart_limits.py`, under a confirmed lock: PASS-COER; FAIL-WOOD and FAIL-COND against thresholds I had not computed, then PASS-FORMULA and PASS-LIMIT, with limits about 0.013 and 200 (Text 4.6, CUT item 1).
- RULE-EFFECT, TUNED2 on noise: with base fixed at y, min |m| falls to 0.005 to 0.012 at L = 3 and to 0.001 to 0.009 at L = 5; with the rule it stays near 0.56 and 0.73. |W - 1| is larger with fixed base by a factor of 8 to 9 at L = 3. At L = 5 the three seeds give factors 0.6, 21 and 1.7, so the first seed goes the other way. The rule does not lower |W - 1| on every sample; what it reliably does is keep |m| away from zero, which is the hypothesis of 5.3. All ratios are far below the bound here, since the noise inflates the right side. Three seeds per L are a small sample.

**The large-field answer so far.** Theorem 4.2 and Theorem 4.3: a large-field region regenerates the large-field hypothesis at the next step with the same constant, in one line from the field inequality (5.5), because the l^1 norm of rough curvature contracts by L^{-2} under averaging. Every rough coarse plaquette is paid either by fresh regular energy at the finer level, or by rough plaquettes at the finer level with a margin L^2 / C_OS. That margin pays the entropy. Rough cubes under a regular field are a dilute gas in the fluctuation integral, with the excess (4.3) as the cost. The divergent count LF-count is the vacuum part of that gas, cancelled by the named subtraction, and the rest is irrelevant by Lemma 4.7c, because the next Gaussian smooths whatever it integrates. I do not claim this is Balaban's R operation: his bodies could not be read (Project Euclid and Deep Blue both served bot checks, and I stopped there).

**(FILL) is gone at this stop.** It was the hardest item at the last stop: that the closure of the rough cells under a fill of bad local patterns stays within a bounded distance of them. **A route not tried: do not fill; take regions to be unions of tiles of a fixed brick tiling, with at most d + 1 tiles at a face of codimension d, nested across levels (Text 3.2).** Then no union of tiles has a bad pattern, by a one-line induction with van Kampen (Lemma 3.2a, checked by exhaustion), the nesting is by construction (T2), and two walls in one coordinate at a vertex are excluded by a colouring and one deletion rule (T3). |Lambda| <= (2 P)^4 |Lambda_0|, P absolute. An intersection with a box, and a mirror image, stay good, so patches need no clause. [A union with a box need not be good, as a reader showed, and nothing uses one.] The price is absolute constants, which L beats, and a tiling that is not covariant, which nothing consumes (3.3(d)). The reader's reason for all this stands: two rough cells in contact along an edge, offset (1, 1, 1, 0), make a ring with no plaquette across it, and the critical Sobolev bound fails there by n^{1/4}. *Routes tried for (FILL) before this, and why they failed.* The maximal seed set: the rule is not monotone. A monotone majorant: it eats a box from its corners. A potential: the perimeter increases. Morphological closing: it does not fill a ring. Hulls in windows: contacts recur at the next scale. Fatter seeds: contacts occur at any offset. A trap in the faces that fire (`checks/hard-core--independent_fill_trap.py`, exact): NO-TRAP at radius 2, 3, 4, and with the rule at codimension 3 only. A monotone rule: it fills re-entrant corners, an orthogonal hull. Numerics on the fill stay as a record: D = 2, ratio at most 30.

**Hardest remaining inequality, within UV-CT, is now not a single named inequality.** No gap in Obligations is left at the level of a route, apart from one on a numerical search of (5.5). The labels at birth are closed, next paragraph. At my last stop but one I wrote that no item was at the level of a route, and a reader then found two, and at this stop two readers found one more route-level error, in my first treatment of old polymers, so this sentence has little weight until someone reads Lemma 4.5k, (TAIL), and the rough sets. For UV-OS the hardest is the relative bound at frozen far data at the end of Text 4.9.

**Found at this stop, unwrapped patches: a finite simply connected piece of a universal cover containing a given ball is not automatic, and Lemma 4.5d asked for one.** Library Lemma 7 does not need it. It gives a'' with periods on any finite good union, and the periods are fixed exactly by the level-k data, since a loop is a chain of bonds. The abelian bound is in Text 4.5, and Theorem G' is to be read with it.

**The labels at birth, the gap of the last stop, are closed by a route not tried: centre the chart at a strictly local field V^#(W), not at the exact minimiser (at (TAIL) in 4.7).** V^# on a cube depends on W only within r_2 / 2 chart cubes, by a pullback, and it is exact to g^C, with a small source term. So at fixed noise V does not move with far W, and no sharp function does: the conflict between noise cuts and field indicators is gone rather than solved. Non-constant field indicators sit at depth r_2 in their polymer, so the W that moves them is real near data already. An old polymer inside a new one is exchanged to the variable v, where the centre is only in the density, so its rough set shrinks by L per level, and the exchange error is one ball of radius R_c per failing cube, paid by exp(-p^2 / 3), which needs p_0 > 2. *Routes tried first, and why they failed.* Entire labels chi_s + chi_m + chi_f = 1: they end at the edge of the chart, with a jump or a non-analytic gluing, of tiny size but with far dependence. One sharp function per term, from 1(far) = 1(no rough) - 1(|xi| <= p_2), on one cube: true there, but links near a face read the noise of two cubes, so the V variable on one cube moves the cut of the other. Mobius into free sets: all indicators become noise cuts, and the conflict moves to old polymers at the interface. Labels relative to a local centre with a global chart: the operators still move with far W. An early version of Lemma 4.7c had rejected a zone of size log(1 / g) because it gave it to every polymer: it is harmless when the polymer has a tiny factor to pay for it, and all polymers with a zone do.

**Tree decay, the hardest of the first half of this stop: (TREE), Lemma 4.5k.** The first reader showed that this is what the gas needs, and that nothing I had gave it. **Route, new: an algebra of anchored majorant series, with the recursion split by the length of the tree.** A long tree at level k is exponentially longer than its image at level k + 1, so the old coefficient comes with exp(-c_tau tau_0 / 2), which beats L^4 and needs nothing of the lift but ||a_l|| <= rho_T. A short tree is bounded by Cauchy from the sup bound, which is one-shot. So there is no compounding and no cancellation is asked of anything, the same mechanism as (4.4). What is new in the input is Lemma 4.5j: in a gauge adapted bond by bond the lift has amplitude at most C L^{-sigma}, by a Riesz potential on the Morrey bound of Lemma 4.5h, and that is below 1 for L large, whatever sigma > 0 is. It also needed a separation of radii: rho_T is a fixed negative power of L times the analytic radius epsilon_1, and eps_0 is far below it, so far sites are small perturbations outside their own blocks. A reader attacked the short and long split, the recursion, the first-order source, the order of constants, Step 5, and the Banach variant of the library, and could not break them given Lemma 4.5j; that is what sent me to bad patterns. (EI) is now a true input for the library: a hull groups sparse sets, and no change of the library is needed, except that it runs in the algebra, so far dependence of H^# on W is also in tree form.

*The first reader's twelve defects, and where they went.* (1) (EI) has no tree: Lemma 4.5k. (2) Far hole sets, region tails: same, with sites as variables. (3) "Analytic in b on small and mid cubes" was false with B_k, C_k in f: they are Mayer factors now, f has EE^an only, and near a chosen old polymer the product cut is replaced as in 4.7c. (4) The measure was not a product at a mixed bond, and a reset left the fiber: the state is T_u = a_u u b_u; and interface indicators read two cubes: neighbours of direct cubes are in Bad. (5) At large xi', the vacuum left Dom: the vacuum of such a site is b_hat = 0. (6), (7) The entry bound for Delta had no derivation below the range of P: replaced by Hadamard's three lines at slope 1 / (2 rho_P), which costs a factor (log L)^3 in the rate of G_1 only, carried by M of order (log L)^5; kappa is no longer c M, and only "large" was ever consumed. (8) r_* had no proof with a factor 2: r_* = 2 C_2(L) C_R(L) eps_0. (9) Y_k(enlarged hull): one definition now, in the proof of (4.1). (10) |Y| in t was false for a thin loop: Vol(Y) := D(Y)^4 and the sup on the ball. (11) bb^new matched, with tube polymers in the recursion. (12) Names: rho_g, alpha_Y(L), C_R(L), H_tau. The reader could not break: the chart algebra, (COER-G), (WOOD), the condition bound, the Gaussian identities, Lemma 4.7b, Lemma 4.7a, the excess arithmetic, the budget, the recursion count, (TAIL) at birth for large Y, the order of constants other than (7), (8).

*A fourth reader, on the repairs of the third.* (a) The fill was order dependent [the fill itself went at the next stop]. (b) **The Whitney charts of my second try gave a false bound on the constants, by Stokes:** tangential flux accumulates. Replaced by charts at the vertices of the cell lattice, at scale n only, built by a shelling with a folding map, by exhaustion of all good patterns, in which one class had to be added to the bad ones. Then there are no small charts and no constants to accumulate. (c) Cuts on xi at fixed Bad stood in the way of the shift of stage (ii), and a first repair was circular: the effective noise xi_eff := xi + 2^{1/2} P^+ B' / g, for which b_hat = 2^{-1/2} g P xi_eff exactly, removes the spectator from the local system altogether. (d) 3(b) of stage (i) had two errors, for a mid collar cube, and a sum over a large site. (e) **Between the interior bound of 9.4 and the boundary polymer there was a gap: analytic polymers now stay at distance L from free boundaries, and (DENS) is gone.** (f) A far chart cube had no crossing case: added. (g) Good patterns at the cut boundary of a patch: by the fill. (h) Smaller: a facet clause in the definition, l^2 boundedness of a o d, the size of beta f on the class, the form of a nonlinear correction, Lambda' with the fill. It could not break the shelling and folding by hand, partition Step 2, the constraint constants in 4.5f, the new 4.5j, refinement of good patterns, Mayer factorisation, beta(f) = 1, the identification in G', or 9.4 in the interior.

*The other three readers, and where their findings went.* A reader of the classical sector had failed twice by exceeding the output limit, with nothing saved. Narrower ones, told to save notes per section, finished. **Large-field sector (Text 1, 2, 4.1-4.3, 5).** (a) n_0^{C n_0} from the partitions of Lemma 4.7a was false for large polymers, not uniform in the volume: now each component of Y_0 is one site, and there is no partition. (b) The polymer regeneration line was false as written, at a vertex and in the collar: Y_0 := Bad + collar, and the first layer is charged to the core. (c) G_{k+1} <= exp(...) contradicted the R polymers: corrected. (d) "Exactly F_{k+1,Om'}" needs V regular off H: (4.3') is named, and "no excess lemma" is withdrawn. (e) Fibers are analytic only at fixed interior links: stated so. (f) (4.1) in local form. (g) (5.2) is stale for the final map, and unused: withdrawn from the list. Others: small. It could not break Lemma 1.1, J >= L^{-9/2}, (1.2), Lemma 2.1, the linearisation, superadditivity, Theorem 4.2, Steps 1 to 5 of Theorem 5.5, (5.4), or the entropy count. **Lift, flow, irrelevance (4.5a-c, G', 7 to 11).** (a) **11.1 Step 6 was false at xi = 1 / L:** F^* is L^{-2} K * F', not L^{-2} F', so the marginal part of a polymer family is not a multiple of A(W): R = 6.45 at P = (pi, pi, pi, pi). It is now extracted as a multiple of F_j(V_j) = F_{j+1}(W), exactly, by subtracting beta f_j at level j before evaluating. The same in the R recursion. (b) The identification of G' with the tropical functional used a shrinking polydisc: it now goes up level by level, by convexity and the envelope theorem. (c) Hypothesis of 11.1 on the curvature class. (d) The Fourier argument in 9.4 was wrong, and a Coulomb gauge is not exp local: replaced by 4.5g and a local elliptic bound. (e) 11.4(b): truncation by region tails. (f) Stationarity along all link variations in G': a Neumann series. (g) Y in 8.8(a) is not local: the conclusion stands by amplitude counting. It could not break 4.5a, 4.5c, G' constants, 7.1, star removal, the symbol, 7.3, the Legendre and Schur determinants, b, (T1)-(T5), 8.5, 8.6, 8.7, 10.1, Steps 2 to 5 of 11.1. **Tree norm (4.5j, 4.5k, EI, TAIL, library variants).** (a) **Lemma 4.5j was false on a region with a ring, and the partition of unity of 4.5f and library Lemma 7 was false at every codimension-2 corner, even on the full torus.** These sent me to the fill and to Whitney charts. (b) c_1 absolute failed in consumption: c_1 = kappa_G / (2 M L). (c) tau_k with a set anchor: links off A, so c_3 carries M^{-4}. (d) Margin per site in Step 1, C_b not absolute, (1 + tau_c)^4, N_0^3, and a power of a that was false for polymers: bookkeeping, fixed.

**For an older form of UV-OS only, and no longer asked by the upper chain's fourth form (Text 20 there): (G1'), for coarse flux f' above c c_F eps_0 L^sigma / C_M: that under E_W, for every W, children with flux above max(eps_0, C_s L^{-kappa} f') are rare.** It is consumed only by the field tier of UV-OS (upper-chain Proposition 14.8). Three routes fail. *Route 1: (4.3') at such W.* The first-order gain phi_* f_p of a hole next to a lift of curvature phi_* is above the cost c_F eps_0 f_p as soon as phi_* > c_F eps_0, so nothing in I(k) excludes rough children. *Route 2: go down h levels,* with a lower bound on the denominator by trial on the h-step lift, and an upper bound on the numerator by the level k - h form. It gives a cost quadratic in f, but with constant c_F / C_OS^h, against the energy of the lift, with constant of order 1/2, so the ratio is not small. *Route 3: conditional Jensen in L^p(mu),* to bring the rare set down by moments: the exponents grow per level, and an exponential Orlicz norm does not contract on rare sets. **What is missing is an upper bound on r_k at a rough V that is sharp to a factor close to 1 in the exponent; I(k)(c) is linear in f_p and sharp only as a bound.**

*Standing warning, which the first reader confirmed.* Most of the errors found have been in places I was reading to check something else. Earlier candidates for hardest and where they went: Lemma 4.5f on regions (every face-adjacent pair of cells has its bond), (STAB) (order of constants, Bernstein), the moment bound for growth, the reverse Holder inequality at a vertex (adapted balls), polymers wrapping a loop (they vanish at no flat field, and the scale pays), the complex class (defined by curvature), C_0 free of L (not required, Proposition 8.4), (COV) (bypassed, Proposition 4.5i), (SPECT) (superseded), (COER-G) (from Lemma 4.5f, with numerics), (MOM) (analyticity in W, strictly local shift), (DENS) (a Gehring layer bound, and now not needed), (RH) (Caccioppoli with library Lemma 7), (J) (direct cubes), large flux (the dichotomy), (OL) (Schur form of the density), hole determinants (Jacobi), the gauge normalisation (exact constant), (REL) (three lines), Wilson term (split of the loop integral), term (3) (interpolation), crossing bonds (exact Schur formula), factorisation of Theorem 4.3 (Lemma 4.7a). **Theorem G's error near corners, three errors in 5.4-5.5, the extension a_tilde, the range of the root, the gapless root C_rem^{1/2}, the box, the eta-dependent chart, the spurious gain of a partial sum at adjacent direct cubes, the tree bound on kernels, bonds coupled through bumps, strict support of R polymers, and the twelve above were all found by re-reading steps I had labelled as derived, and further errors of that kind are likely. Treat the labels below with that in mind.**

**Routes tried and why they failed** (Text 1.5, 2, 5.4, 7, 10): Cauchy in product form on telescoped densities for (EI) (no tree: the distance from a cube of a patch to the patch is zero, so the sum over sets in a patch diverges at radius g^{-1/3}; replaced by Lemma 4.5k); Cauchy in product form on a tube for R polymers (the tube is capped at alpha, so beyond the cap there is no decay in the distance, and the sum over sets of far cubes diverges in the volume; replaced by (TAIL) in tree form); strict truncation of the centre and the densities at a range A log(1/g) (it would remove sparse sets inside a patch, since a N_r is then small, but the operators of the chart still have tails, and the old polymers inherit them, so a recursion for polymers is needed anyway, and it covers the densities too; dropped); requiring the amplitude bound in the Feynman gauge of the chart (not needed, and it would have asked for a De Giorgi estimate on scalars near corners; replaced by a gauge per bond); a Schur norm for Delta from an entry bound (no such bound below the range of P; replaced by three lines); strict support for R polymers (false, the centre V^*(W) is global; patch telescoping gives pieces of size exp(-c r) on supports of order r^4, which exclusion cannot afford; replaced by (TAIL), a tube of analyticity, expanded in the next white noise); a kernel bound exp(-delta_0 tree) for Taylor coefficients of order m, by Cauchy (does not follow; now proved by its own induction, Lemma 4.5k, in summed form); spreading the second-order constraint correction by bumps (couples bonds, gamma gets tails; replaced by crossing links, which are private); sup-norm balls cut by the region in Gehring (thin tentacles at re-entrant corners; replaced by adapted balls); single cubes as the sites of the gas near adjacent direct cubes (a partial sum resets a neighbour, and the Haar gauge freedom makes the interface rough, with a spurious gain; replaced by the components of Bad as sites, with the hole read from direct links); bounding the excess through the minimum over the fiber of F_{k,Far} (loses the first-order term: the links in a hole can carry part of the constraint); a fine-lattice extension into a hole (false for a hole with circulation); an enlarged hole by an analysis-only gauge, a weak-type count and a co-area level, with a difference form of the field inequality (all unnecessary after the dichotomy of strong blocks, and the difference form was not derived); a polynomial in the projected form (a step of range L, so range L^2 for degree L; replaced by a massive local root, a Gaussian conditioning, and the strictly local U = 1 - l Q); C = (1 - e) P P^T + C_rem with the symmetric root of C_rem (no spectral gap, so no decay that I can prove; replaced by the exact split with a factor 1/2); a chart depending on the direct set (indicators lose strict locality at fixed xi'; replaced by one reference Gaussian and Jacobi's minor); no box at all on the chart (the gauge part of b is unbounded; replaced by Dom and an exact constant normalisation with FP); interlacing and Duhamel for hole determinants (not needed after Jacobi); a Gaussian on part of a tube with frozen links (needs a lower bound on a mean of partly rotated paths; replaced by direct cubes, where only the exact probability measure of Lemma 1.1 is used); a Lipschitz bound by the measure of a thin shell for the spectator (decay only in the farthest distance, does not sum over sets; ## Text

### 1. The hard constraint, on the whole compact field space [DERIVED here; hostile check in 1.6]

Credit: taking a coarse link to be an average of paths through a pair of blocks is Balaban's construction (CMP 98; CMP 109
(0.4) as transcribed in the program's NOTES section 9.2, LANE-READ, body not re-read by me). The paired-contour linearisation
is RENEW section 2. The quaternion mean, the exact Haar pushforward and the rest of this section are derived here and are
specific to this push; I have not checked whether the mean appears in his papers. It is a choice of map: nothing of his
is imported on the strength of "many definitions serve".

**1.1 Geometry.** L odd. T_k = level-k torus, spacing 1 in level-k units; T_{k+1} = centres of L-blocks B(y). For a coarse
bond c' = (y, y + L e_mu), let I(c') be the set of L^3 level-k links crossing the face between B(y) and B(y + L e_mu).
Links of I(c') occur in no family for any other coarse bond, because every other family stays inside its own two blocks
and crosses its own face. "Interior links" are those inside a block.

**1.2 Quaternion mean.** Identify SU(2) with the unit quaternions in R^4. For a finite family with common endpoints and
fixed nonnegative weights summing to one, put m = sum_i w_i g_i in R^4, and Mean{g_i} = m / |m| whenever m != 0. Then:

- Mean(a g_i b) = a Mean(g_i) b, for a, b in SU(2); no element is singled out and the weights are permutation covariant.
- If g_i = exp(i A_i), then Mean = exp(i sum_i w_i A_i + O(|A|^3)): the second-order term is absorbed by the normalisation.
- If m != 0 and h_i is another family with sum over i of w_i |g_i - h_i| = delta and delta <= |m|/2, then |Mean{g} - Mean{h}| <= (4/|m|) delta.
- In SL(2,C) the same formula m / det(m)^{1/2} is holomorphic off det m = 0. Near a real m, det m is close to |m|^2, so the
  analyticity radius is proportional to |m|. For a complex m a lower bound on |m| is not enough: m = 1 - sigma_1 has det 0 and |m| = sqrt 2.

For u in I(c') with transverse position t, let line(u) = {x in B(y) with transverse position t}. Put
a_u = Mean over x in line(u) of [P(y,x) * straight(x -> u^-)], and b_u = Mean over x in line(u) of
[straight(u^+ -> x + L e_mu) * P(y + L e_mu, x + L e_mu)^{-1}], with P the Mean over axial orders of axial paths. Both a_u, b_u are
functions of interior links only. Let T_u = a_u u b_u and define

    m(V, c') = L^{-3} sum_{u in I(c')} T_u(V),      M(V, c') = m(V, c') / |m(V, c')|.                                (1.1)

Where an inner mean has m = 0, take the first element in a fixed order: this happens on a null set and only makes the map
Borel, which is all that 1.3 needs. M is gauge covariant, with the block centres carrying the coarse gauge transformation.

*Linearisation.* With V = exp(iA) at first order,

    (QA)(c') = |B|^{-1} sum_{x in B(y)} A(straight(x -> x + L e_mu)) + h(y) - h(y + L e_mu),   h(y) = |B|^{-1} sum_x [avg axial A from y to x].

The h term is one function of the block for all directions, so it is pure gauge, and the coarse curvature is exactly the
average (R4) of RENEW section 2: F'(X) = L^{-4} sum_{y in B(X)} sum_{s,t < L} F(y + s e_mu + t e_nu). RENEW's (R5) and (R7), with their sharp
constants, apply to this linearisation. A family using a different x set for each direction would leave a residual
g_mu(y) - g_nu(y), a star through the block centre that gives weight of order 1 to plaquettes next to y; so the x set is the
full block, and the line average in a_u, b_u is what keeps each crossing link in exactly one element of the family.

**1.3 Lemma 1.1 (exact Haar pushforward).** Under product Haar measure on the level-k links, M(V) is Haar distributed on the
coarse links, and the coarse links are independent. Conditionally on the interior links the same is true, and the
crossing-link sets I(c') are independent. Hence D(W) := int dV delta(W^{-1} M(V)) = 1 for every W.

*Proof.* Condition on the interior links. Then {a_u, b_u} are fixed, and the sets I(c') are independent Haar families, with M(., c')
depending on I(c') only. For fixed c' and g in SU(2), the substitution u -> u b_u g b_u^{-1} on every u in I(c') preserves the product
Haar measure, and it sends T_u to T_u g for every u at once. So m -> m g and M -> M g on the full-measure set m != 0. Therefore the
conditional law of M(., c') is right-invariant, so it is Haar.  []

**Corollary 1.2 (Markov kernel, fibers, Jacobian).** Off the closed null set {m = 0}, M is real-analytic in the crossing links and
a submersion. Its Jacobian per coarse bond obeys J >= L^{-9/2}: the simultaneous right translation in the proof moves
L^3 links at unit speed each and moves M at unit speed, so dM dM^* >= L^{-3} on su(2). **At fixed interior links,** {crossing links : M = W} is a
real-analytic manifold, non-compact only towards {m = 0}, and for bounded Borel f, with the integral over crossing links at fixed interior links, then over the interior links,

    int dV delta(W^{-1} M(V)) f(V) = int_{Fib(W)} f dsigma / J,      total mass 1 for every W.

So delta(W^{-1} M(V)) dV is, for each W, a probability measure mu_W on the level-k fields, and rho -> int mu_W(dV) rho(V) is a Markov operator.

**1.4 Exact recursion and the named subtraction.** rho_0 = exp(-beta A), rho_{k+1}(W) = int mu_W(dV) rho_k(V). All rho_k are
positive and gauge invariant, and int dW rho_k(W) = Z for every k. With 1 the trivial coarse field and r_k = rho_k / rho_k(1),

    r_{k+1}(W) = [ int mu_W(dV) r_k(V) ] / [ int mu_1(dV) r_k(V) ],      Z(beta) = rho_K(1; beta) int dW r_K(W; beta).        (1.2)

**So the explicit subtraction is log rho_K(1; beta), the constrained partition function with the coarse field trivial.** It is exact at every beta, and it carries every vacuum term, including the vacuum part of every large-field gas. With a source it becomes the counterterm of UV-CT (Text 6.2).

**1.5 Routes that failed.**
- *One-link solve.* M = X_1(others) V(b_0) X_2(others) gives a global graph and exact Haar. It gives b_0 weight 1, so the composite
  constraint routes each level through a single link, and in the linearised model the variance of the level-k coarse link is
  a sum of k terms of order one. That destroys the uniform bound on Q_k G Q_k^*. Scaling count, enough to drop the route.
- *Entire average around a reference path,* M = exp(i avg psi(T_u S^{-1})) S with psi entire and psi(T) = 0 at T = -1. It is holomorphic and
  a submersion, but psi differs from the logarithm at large angle, so a rough S keeps full weight once every loop through it is
  rough. A flux tube around S costs of order 30/L in level-k action and makes the coarse link rough by a large angle: the
  fine action no longer dominates the coarse action at large curvature, and a rough link on a composite straight line persists at no
  further cost. The quaternion mean has neither defect: each path has weight L^{-3} in m at every field.

**1.6 Hostile check of Section 1.** (a) A first proof of submersion for the reference-path average used loops indexed by x in B(y),
so a crossing link entered L loops, and the annihilation step only gave that a sum over a line vanishes. The line
averages a_u, b_u fix that here too: each crossing link is in one T_u. (b) Lemma 1.1 needs the crossing link to appear only once
in T_u and in no other coarse family. True by construction, provided a_u, b_u are built from interior links, and the straight
legs are interior except for u itself. (c) M is discontinuous on {m = 0}, and in the interior links it is only Borel: an inner mean m_z can be small while the outer |m| is near 1 (a reader's case: a link at the midpoint of the line of u, rotated by about pi, is used by half of the x, so m_{u^-} = O(1 / L)), and the rule of 5.4 can jump. So the whole fiber is not a manifold, and it is not stated to be. Upper bounds are unaffected, because mu_W is a probability
measure whatever happens there. Analyticity is used only on the chart, where every plaquette of the two blocks is small, so all inner means are near 1, and the rule picks x_0 = y; the direct bonds use only the exact measure at fixed interior links. (d) This M is SU(2)-specific; for SU(N) the polar part has the same right-equivariance, up to a det
phase, which is a later target. (e) Nonlinear corrections in M have not yet been compared with any bound on Q_k G Q_k^*: a
small-field obligation, listed.

### 2. Fiber flow and the two ways the coarse field enters [DERIVED]

**Lemma 2.1 (fiber flow).** For g in SU(2) acting on one coarse bond c', define phi_g on level-k fields by u -> u b_u g b_u^{-1} for every
u in I(c') and the identity on all other links. Then M(phi_g V, c') = M(V, c') g, the other coarse links do not move, phi_g preserves
Haar measure and (phi_g)_# mu_W = mu_{W g}. So for bounded Borel F,

    int mu_{W g}(dV) F(V) = int mu_W(dV) F(phi_g V).                                                                      (2.1)

*Proof.* The substitution in Lemma 1.1, done on one bond.  []

Formula (2.1) is holomorphic in g for fixed interior links whenever F is holomorphic in the crossing links. In exchange it moves
L^3 links by the full angle of g. In a flat frame phi_g is a rotation of one face, so it leaves plaquettes inside the face
unchanged at first order and puts curvature of size |g - 1| on the order of L^2 plaquettes on the edges of the face. The action
cost is of order L^2 |g-1|^2, against |g-1|^2 for the smooth lift. Consequently:

- **Never iterate (2.1) to bound rho_k at a complex coarse field.** Using it at each level multiplies the constant in
  |r_k(e^{iy} V)| <= r_k(V) exp(C |y|^2 / g_k^2) by L^2 per step. [Scaling count.] The small-field part gets its complex bound from the smooth
  lift, and a frozen island only sees the coarse field through regular exterior data (Section 4).
- Formula (2.1) remains a useful exact identity for bounds at real W, for the Lipschitz estimate
  |log r_{k+1}(W g) - log r_{k+1}(W)| <= sup_V |log r_k(phi_g V) - log r_k(V)|, and for checks.

### 3. Order of constants and the induction statement (level-only scheme)

Everything is stated on the current level's lattice. r_k is one function of the level-k field V; nothing finer is ever carried, and there is no sum over histories. Credit: the split of an effective density into a small-field form and a large-field factor, and the extraction of a cost from the action, are the organisation of Balaban (titles and reviews only; bodies unread) as exposed for d = 3 scalar by Dimock II, III, which I read. Iterating polymers with a contraction per step, rather than re-evaluating them at a composite background, is Dimock's Lemma 11 in part I. CMP 109 (LANE-READ) re-evaluates instead. The Lyapunov inequality below is derived here, and **I make no claim about how this relates to Balaban's R operation.**

**3.1 Order.** See the principle in 4.5: linear constants are free of L and k, and they alone enter margins and contractions; nonlinear constants may depend on L and are beaten by the choice of eps_0.
1. The rates delta_0, c, sigma of 4.5, 7.3: absolute.
2. L odd and large, for the margins: Theorem 4.2 (L^2 >= 8 C_OS O (1 + C_M)), the excess (4.3), and the contraction by (M / L)^a. Not for (FLOW): Proposition 8.4.
3. Patch radius R_t = A L log L (4.5i), entering only nonlinear constants. M = M(L), a power of log L, with L >> M, and M >= 8 P for the tiles of 3.2, which holds since L is large. C_M is absolute, since a cell is a unit cell (3.2). The range of the strictly local chart in (CUT) item 1 is rho_P := C t t'^2 M_1 L, with t, t', M_1 multiples of log L, and it has to be below a quarter of the side M L of a cube. **The kernel of G_1 has rate s_P := 1 / (2 rho_P) per level-k link, slower than c / L by a power of log L (CUT item 1), so per cube of side M L its rate is kappa_G := M L s_P = M / (2 C t t'^2 M_1).** I take M of the order of (log L)^5, so that kappa_G >= 4 log L, and M >= 4 C t t'^2 M_1. kappa := kappa_G / 4. Wherever the kernel of B' is used below, its rate per cube is kappa_G, and the kernels of A^{-1}, C_1^{1/2}, G_2 keep c / L. [Until this round the file had rate c / L for G_1, so kappa = c M. A reader found that the entry bound behind it had no derivation below the range of P. What is consumed is only that the rate per cube is large: it runs the cluster library, and it beats c_4 = c L^{-2} in the tails of KK_eta, which is what the log L is for.] c_3 := kappa_G / (C M^4), with C absolute, is the rate of the anchored norm of polymers, per link of their own level, and L >> M^4 leaves it above kappa_G / (M L), so that per cube of side M L its rate is at least kappa_G. c_1 := kappa_G / (2 M L) is the growth rate of the radius function of (TAIL).
4. C_OS absolute (5.5). c_F := c_A / (8 C_OS^2 O), absolute, O the overlap number of hulls.
5. Nonlinear constants C(L, R): C_Y, alpha_Y(L), rho_*, the vertex bounds of Theorem G', the constants C_0, C_2 of Proposition 8.4. **The analytic radius of the functional, epsilon_1 := c / (C(L, R_t) L^2): Theorem G' and Proposition 4.5i hold on the class with this in place of eps_0, for F_k only. The tree radius rho_T := L^{-A_T} epsilon_1 and the base radius epsilon_2 := L^{-A_T'} rho_T of Lemma 4.5k, with A_T, A_T' absolute and large.**
6. eps_0 small: at most c_1 L^{-2} for fiber stability, at most epsilon_2, and small against C(L, R)^{-1} and against any power of 1/L that a margin requests. No lower bound on eps_0 is used anywhere.
7. alpha := c_2 eps_0, complex radius, with c_2^2 <= c_F / (C M^3). r_* := 2 C_2(L) C_R(L) eps_0, the chart domain, with C_R(L) = C L^2 from (REL) and C_2(L) polynomial in L, the sup norm of the solution of the gauge condition; rho_g := C_R(L) eps_0, the box on rho; theta := C(L, R_t) r_* is small by the choice of eps_0.
8. p(g) = (log 1/g)^{p_0}, p_0 > 2: white-noise cut. [2 p_0 > 1 was enough until this round. The larger value is used once, in 4.7, so that exp(-p^2 / 12) beats exp(-kappa C r_2^4). Nothing below minds a larger p, since p(g)^3 costs a power of log only.]
9. rho, the source radius, may be chosen here, so it may depend on L (6.4).
10. gamma, last and small against everything above.

Compare 5.6 of an earlier version of this file, where M > L forced a grace period: with L >> M a cell is already small against a block, and that whole mechanism is gone.

**3.2 Regions and functionals.** A *cell* at level k is a unit cell of T_k, that is, a level-(k - 1) block. [Cells had side M in earlier versions, which made C_M polylogarithmic in L. I checked each consumer in this round, and nothing forces it: patterns at the next level down are unions of whole blocks either way, and polymers are still unions of cubes of side M. So C_M is absolute, and the margins of Theorem 4.2 and of (4.3) ask only that L be large against absolute constants.] For V on T_k: f_p = |V_p - 1|. Rough plaquettes: f_p > eps_0. Y_k(V) := sum over rough p of f_p. Lambda_0(V) := the cells meeting a rough plaquette, that is, containing one of its sites; Lambda(V) := the union of the tiles below that contain a cell of Lambda_0(V); Om(V) := its complement. Near: plaquettes that are not rough and have a site in Lambda(V). E_k(V) := sum over plaquettes with all four sites in Om(V) of f_p^2. [Near was within distance 2 of Lambda until this round. That was only caution, and it made the budget of 4.8 spend part of Y_k twice: a near plaquette of a hull inside H could be charged to a rough cell outside H. With this definition, a near plaquette of a hull inside H has its rough cell inside H, since all its sites are. And a level-k plaquette with all four sites in the region has its whole hull in the region, so Lemma 7.1 holds for it exactly, with no margin at the boundary.] There are at most n_near, an absolute constant, near plaquettes per cell of Lambda, each with f <= eps_0, at most 4 Y_k / eps_0 cells in Lambda_0, and at most (2 P)^4 times that in Lambda, so the sum of f over near plaquettes is at most C_M Y_k, with C_M := 4 (2 P)^4 n_near.

*Good patterns [from a reader's counterexample].* Around a face of the cell complex of codimension d there are 2^d cells, the vertices of a cube Q_d. For the set S of kept cells, K(S) has vertices S, an edge for each pair of face-adjacent kept cells, and a square for each 2-face of Q_d with all four kept: a link needs two face-adjacent cells, and a plaquette the four cells around a face of codimension 2. A pattern at an edge is **good** if every component of K(S) is simply connected. A pattern at a vertex is **good** if every component of K(S) is simply connected and it is not an antipodal pair removed with nothing else. A union of cells is good if all its patterns at edges and at vertices are. By exhaustion, exact (`checks/hard-core--independent_patterns.py`): for d = 2 all are good; for d = 3 the bad ones are exactly those with an antipodal pair removed and nothing else (PASS-Q3, as expected by hand on the octahedron); for d = 4, 8652 of 65536, in 60 classes, the smallest removed set having 3 cells; and H_1 = 0 implies simply connected in every case (PASS-PI1). For a removed set inside a facet of Q_d, K(S) is simply connected [DERIVED]: each kept cell of that facet is joined to its twin on the opposite facet, and a kept edge there bounds a square, so K(S) retracts onto the opposite facet, whose 2-skeleton is simply connected. So a pattern with K(S) not simply connected, or an antipodal pair, has removed cells on both sides of every transverse coordinate. [The clause on the antipodal pair comes from the shelling of library Lemma 7, and from nothing else.]

**Why bad patterns have to go.** Two removed cells in contact along an edge, with offset (1, 1, 1, 0), are enough, and two rough plaquettes can do it. The six other cells around the edge form a ring with no plaquette across it. A loop around the ring has length 6 in lattice units, at every point of the edge, at every depth n, and it can be contracted only through a ribbon along the edge. A field h(t) omega, with omega closed around the ring, |omega| of order 1 / r, has energy of order h^2 / T, for an edge of length T n, and any gauge has |a| >= h / 6 on some link of each such loop. So ||a||^4_{l^4} >= c h^4 T n in every gauge: **the critical Sobolev bound fails by a factor n^{1/4}, on a simply connected patch**, and the one-shot Theorem G' with it. And a coarse bond of the ring has lift amplitude at least 1 / 6 per link, summing to at least 1 over the ring in any gauge, against the contraction of Lemma 4.5j. At a bad vertex the period at scale r can vary with log r, by the same count. All of this is about a set of very small probability, and it would still have spoiled the uniformity in n. I had stated Lemma 4.5d and library Lemma 7 for any finite simply connected pattern. That was false.

*Tiles [new in this round; they replace a fill of bad patterns and the gap (FILL) it left].* Bad patterns must not occur in any region, and the question is how to remove them at a bounded cost. **Take regions to be unions of tiles of a fixed brick tiling, in which at most d + 1 tiles meet at a face of codimension d.** Then no union of tiles has a bad pattern, for a reason that is one induction. Credit: tilings by bricks with at most d + 1 at a point are Lebesgue's, from dimension theory, as I recall it; everything I use is proved below.

A partition of Q_d into subcubes is a **guillotine partition with distinct coordinates** if it is the trivial one, or if some coordinate i splits Q_d into two facets, each carrying such a partition, the coordinates used on the two facets being disjoint from each other, and different from i. It has at most d + 1 parts.

**Lemma 3.2a (local) [DERIVED; exhaustion, exact, at d <= 4: `checks/hard-core--independent_bricks.py`].** For such a partition let S be a union of parts, and Q' a subcube of Q_d. Then S n Q' is empty, or K(S n Q') is connected and simply connected. And S is not Q_d minus an antipodal pair, for d >= 2. So every union of parts, and its intersection with a subcube, is a good pattern, with one component.

*Proof.* The restriction of such a partition to a subcube is one of the same kind, so take Q' = Q_d, and induct on d. Let X(S) be the cubical complex of the subcubes of Q_d with all vertices in S. K(S) is its 2-skeleton, so they have the same pi_0 and pi_1. For a trivial partition S is empty or Q_d. Otherwise let i split it, with facets A, B, and coordinates C_A, C_B. Then S n A = T x Q_{rest}, with T in Q_{C_A}, and S n B = T' x Q_{rest'}, with T' in Q_{C_B}. If one of them is empty, use the induction on the other. If not, their projections along i meet in T x T' x Q_{rest''}, which is not empty since C_A, C_B are disjoint, and X(S) = X(S n A) u ( X(T x T' x Q_{rest''}) x [0, 1] ) u X(S n B). By induction T and T' have connected, simply connected complexes. So has the product, and van Kampen gives the union. *Antipodal pair.* The removed set would consist of two single-vertex parts, and the path from the root to such a leaf uses all d coordinates. Past the node where the two paths part, by a coordinate j, they use disjoint sets of coordinates, each of which must be all that is left. So nothing is left, and the two vertices differ in j only.  []

*The tiling.* Let P be an absolute integer, fixed below, with 2 P < L. For each level k we build a partition TT_k of the cell lattice of T_k into boxes of cells, **tiles**, of sides at most 2 P, from the top down. At the top, the parent is T_K, whose side we take to be a multiple of P, at least 2 P, with its cuts closing up on the circle. At level k < K the parents are the tiles of TT_{k+1} refined to level-k cells, boxes with all sides at least L. A parent is cut in four rounds, in the coordinates 4, 3, 2, 1. In the round of coordinate j each current box n gets a residue rho(n) mod P, different from the residue of every box of that round and that level whose closure touches the closure of n; it is cut by the hyperplanes x_j = c strictly inside n with c = rho(n) mod P, **except** for a position c at which some tile of TT_{k+1} touching n has a wall orthogonal to e_j. A **wall** is the part of such a hyperplane in the closure of the box it cuts; the faces of a parent are walls of a coarser level.

(T1) *Sides are at most 2 P.* Coarser wall positions in a coordinate are multiples of L apart, in level-k cells, and P < L, so no two consecutive candidates are both deleted.

(T2) *Nesting.* Each tile of TT_{k+1}, refined, is a union of tiles of TT_k, by construction.

(T3) *At each vertex x of the cell lattice of T_k, and for each j, at most one wall orthogonal to e_j, of level k or coarser, contains x.* By descending induction on k. Let two such walls contain x; both have x_j = c. If both are of level k, their boxes touch at x and carry different residues mod P, so they cannot have the same c, unless they are one box and one wall. If one is of level k and the other coarser, the second is on the boundary of a coarser tile T'' with x in its closure, and T'' touches the box n of the first, or T'' contains n, when c is an end of the j-interval of n and is not a cut. In the first case c was deleted. If both are coarser, they are closed boxes with corners in the lattice of level k + 1, so their intersection contains a vertex of that lattice, against the induction.

(T4) *Around any face of the cell lattice, the tiles give a guillotine partition of Q_d with distinct coordinates.* Restrict the tree of cuts to Q_d. A wall that separates two cells of Q_d contains their common facet, so it contains the face. So the local tree consists of walls through one vertex, and (T3) makes their coordinates distinct.

(T5) *P can be chosen absolute.* The residues are a proper colouring of the touching graph of the boxes of one round, so P larger than its degree is enough. That degree does not depend on P or L. A tile of TT_{k+1} has neighbours in at most 16 grandparents, since grandparents have sides at least L > 2 P in level-(k+1) cells, so a box of side at most 2 P meets at most two of them in each coordinate of a level-(k + 2) cell. In one grandparent at most 4 slabs meet an interval of length 2 P, since the cuts of one box are at least P apart, then at most 4 rows in a slab, and so on. With closures, read 5 for 4. So a tile touches at most 16 * 5^4 tiles, a box of a round lies in one parent, and it touches at most 5^3 boxes of the round in each parent touching that parent, so at most (16 * 5^4 + 1) * 5^3 boxes. The value P = 2^21 is enough. [It enters only through absolute constants, below which L is taken, so its size is of no consequence. A smarter construction would be smaller.]

**So: Lambda(V) := the union of the tiles of TT_k containing a cell of Lambda_0(V), and regions are unions of tiles.** By (T4) and Lemma 3.2a, all their local patterns are good, also after intersecting with a box, and each local pattern has one component. By (T2) the refinement of a region is a union of tiles of the next level down, so a level-k region and the refinement of a level-(k + 1) region have good union and good differences, with no relative statement to prove. |Lambda(V)| <= (2 P)^4 |Lambda_0(V)|, and Lambda(V) lies within D_T := 2 P cells of Lambda_0(V). A coarse set S of blocks is replaced by the tiles of TT_{k+1} meeting it, at the cost of a factor (2 P)^4 in |S|.

*What this costs.* Only absolute constants. C_M grows by the factor (2 P)^4; L must be large against P^2 through Theorem 4.2, and against P through locality; and the threshold C' of the dichotomy in 4.4 grows by (2 P)^4, which is harmless since eps_0 is chosen after it. No margin compares C_M with anything but a power of L. *What this gives up.* The map V -> Lambda(V) is not covariant under the lattice symmetries, since the tiling is not. r_k is, being one exact function. See 3.3(d). F_{k,Om} and EE^an_{k,Om} are defined for every Om that is a union of tiles of an image of the tiling under the lattice symmetries, as a family covariant in (Om, V), and only the choice of Om as a function of V uses the tiling. ["Every good union" was false here, as a reader showed: an intersection of a good union with a box can be bad, if it is not a union of tiles. On T, a box is a union of parts of every tiling, so the balls of Text 11 are covariant.]

*Cubes are tiles too [found by a reader].* The polymers are unions of cubes, and the chart region R of 4.7 is the complement of the direct cubes. On a grid two direct cubes can touch at offset (1, 1, 1, 0), so R has a bad pattern, and (COER-G), which rests on Lemma 4.5f, has no proof there. So **cubes are the tiles of a tiling of the same kind, one level at a time, with no nesting: bricks of side M in level-(k + 1) cells, in brick-wall position, M a multiple of 2^21, and the side of T_{k+1} a multiple of M.** Then all unions of cubes have good patterns, and counts of neighbours only improve: at most 5 cubes at a vertex in place of 16. Everything else about a cube of side M, or M L at the level below, is unchanged.

*Hostile pass on the tiles.* I looked for a bad pattern at a place where two levels meet: a coarse tile with a corner at a vertex and a fine tile with the opposite corner there would make an antipodal pair, and without the deletion rule of (T3) this does occur. With it, those are two walls in each of three coordinates through one vertex, and they are excluded. I looked for a thin tile: a truncated box can have side 1, and nothing uses a lower bound on a side of a tile. I looked for a tiling problem at the top: the side of T_K must be a multiple of P, so Tor has at least P last cells, an absolute number, and the trivial tile is never used. I looked for a lost budget: the near plaquettes of a filled tile have no rough plaquette of their own within a cell, but within D_T cells, which is why the l^1 charge reaches further, and it is still at most C C_M L^{-2} of a coarse weight, since 2 P << L. **Not attacked: the local constants of Lemma 4.5f and library Lemma 7 are functions of the local pattern and the nerve, and the local patterns of unions of tiles are a subset of the good ones, so nothing is new there, but Lemma 7 has only been read by the readers named in the state of attack.** The earlier routes to bounding a fill are in the state of attack, and `checks/hard-core--independent_patterns_fast.py`, `_patterns_rel.py`, `_fill_trap.py` are kept as the record of them.

For a union of cells Om and V regular on Om, F_{k,Om}(V) is the **classical functional with free boundary**, defined tropically: F_{0,Om} = A on Om, and F_{k+1,Om'}(W) := min of F_{k,Om}(V) over the regular slice of the fiber, with constraints only on bonds with both blocks in Om'. Directly from the definition:

    Adding a constraint or frozen boundary data can only raise the minimum: F_{k+1,Om'}(W; frozen) >= F_{k+1,Om'}(W).                          (3.1)

**3.3 Statement I(k).** For every real V on T_k,

    r_k(V) = exp( -g_k^{-2} F_{k,Om}(V) + EE_{k,Om}(V) ) G_k(V),     Om = Om(V),                                                                (3.2)

which *defines* G_k > 0, such that:

(a) *Classical.* On regular V, F_{k,Om} = qcov_n + N_k, with n = L^k, qcov_n the gauge-covariant form of the exact quadratic perfect action of Text 7.1, 10.1, and N_k gauge invariant of third order in the curvature, with density bound |N_k density| <= C_N (local |F|)^3 uniformly in k (Theorem P, 4.5, from Theorem G'). In particular F_{k,Om}(V) >= c_A E_k(V on Om) with c_A >= 1/4.

(b) *Analytic sector, indexed by regions.* The complex class C(Om; eps_0, alpha) is the set of complex fields V on links of Om, with F_p := log V_p in su(2) tensor C, such that |Re F_p| <= eps_0 and |Im F_p| <= alpha on plaquettes of Om. [The file used this class without defining it until this round.] It is gauge invariant and local, which is all that polymers need, and no global gauge is asked for: a first definition, with a global z, would have needed a sup bound on a lift in one gauge, which I do not have near a corner. On a patch of radius R, radial transport gives a gauge with |log V_l| <= C R (eps_0 + alpha) in a simply connected piece, which is below rho_* by the choice of eps_0, so the class lies in the polydisc of Theorem G' in a patch gauge. It contracts under the lift: |Re F^*| <= C L^{-sigma} eps_0 and |Im F^*| <= C L^{-sigma} alpha, by Lemma 4.5h applied to the linear data, with nonlinear corrections at most C(L, R) (eps_0 + alpha)^2, negligible by the choice of eps_0. A perturbation V(b) with |b| <= r_0 := c alpha stays in the class. **Polymers of the analytic sector stay away from free boundaries [changed in this round].** For every region Om, EE^an_{k,Om} := sum over Y at distance at least L, in level-k units, from the complement of Om, of e_k(Y; V), with each e_k(Y) gauge invariant, analytic on C(Om; eps_0, alpha), e_k(Y; 1) = 0 at the flat field on the whole torus, with sensitivity to V decaying exponentially away from Y at rate kappa / M, |e_k(Y)| <= E_0 exp(-kappa d(Y)), and sum_Y |e_k(Y)| <= C_E |Om|. It is defined by the closed recursion N_good[Om'] of 4.7. It does not know about Lambda(V) except through the region, and its region dependence has tails: if Om_1, Om_2 differ only at distance d from Y, then |e^{Om_1}_k(Y; V) - e^{Om_2}_k(Y; V)| <= E_0 exp(-kappa d(Y) - (kappa / M) d). **This and the sensitivity to V hold in tree form: e_k(Y; exp(i v) V), as a series in v and in the states of far sites, has anchored norm ||.||_Y <= C(L, M) E_0 exp(-kappa d(Y)), at radius rho_e := c alpha and rate c_3 ("The tree norm", 4.5, and Closure in 4.6), where a site within a bounded multiple of (1 / c) log(C(L) / c_2) of Y is held fixed, with a factor 2.** One difference alone does not sum over sets of far sites. *Why the collar.* The interior bound of 9.4 and its Holder modulus hold at distance at least L / C from a free boundary, and Proposition 11.1 needs them. Until this round the file kept polymers next to a free boundary, with a separate contraction by the energy of a layer, (DENS), and a reader found a gap between the two zones. They are not needed: the cubes within max(r_2, L / M) cubes of a hole are in the mandatory polymer P of the gas anyway, where the entropy is paid per cube, so nothing is lost by having no analytic expansion there.

(c) *R sector and large field.* The factor G_k in (3.2) has the gas form of 4.7, with |z_k(P; V)| <= exp(-c_F eps_0 Y_k(V; P) / g_k^2 - kappa |P \\ Col_k(P)|), where Col_k(P) is the set of cubes of P within max(r_2(g_k), L / M) cubes of the cells of Lambda in P, and R-polymers B_k, C_k. Nothing here is evaluated at complex V. In particular, for w = 0, G_k(V) <= exp(-c_F eps_0 Y_k(V) / g_k^2 + small).

(d) *Symmetry.* All objects are gauge invariant. r_k is covariant under the lattice symmetries of T_k, and so is F_{k,Om} + EE^an_{k,Om} as a family in (Om, V). The map V -> Om(V) uses the tiling of 3.2, so the decomposition (3.2) at a V with holes need not be covariant, and nothing consumes that. **What 4.7c needs, at any W, is that the block sum of the marginal forms beta(e) over the polymers in one coarse block does not depend on the block, up to the Holder modulus [a reader showed that my first line here, an average of the family over the group, breaks the identity at V with holes].** At W regular on T, the sum of E_W[e(Y; V)] over Y is E_W[log G_k], which is an exact covariant function of W, since G_k = r_k exp(g^{-2} F_{k,T} - EE^an_{k,T}) on regular V. So its second-order form in the curvature at flat W is translation invariant, and testing it on coarse curvature that is smooth and localised gives the claim. At W with holes, a polymer Y at distance d from the holes differs from its value at the same W with no holes by a tail exp(-(kappa / M) d), and this defect goes to the mandatory polymers, with the size of (TAIL). So the same family works at all W, with no averaging.

**Theorem I (target).** If g_k <= gamma for all k <= K, then I(k) holds for all k <= K, with constants independent of k, K and the volume. The flow is defined by extracting the marginal part from EE_{k+1} (11.1), and Proposition 8.4 gives what the step needs from it, jointly with I(k), by induction on k.

### 4. One step k -> k+1: what regenerates (the crux)

**4.1 Field input.** (5.5): for any real V and any split of the hull plaquettes of a coarse plaquette P into irr and reg, |W(dP) - 1| <= C_OS ( L^{-2} ||f||_{l^1(irr)} + ||f||_{l^2(reg)} ).

**Theorem 4.2 (Lyapunov inequality) [DERIVED from (5.5)].** For every real V, with W = M(V) and L^2 >= 8 C_OS O (1 + C_M),

    c_F eps_0 Y_{k+1}(W) <= (1/2) [ c_F eps_0 Y_k(V; H) + c_A E_k(V; H) ],                                                                            (4.1)

with H any union of cells containing the hull of every rough coarse plaquette, Y_k(V; H) over rough plaquettes within D_T cells of H, and E_k(V; H) over plaquettes of H. [The form with global Y_k(V), E_k(V) follows. A reader noted that the consumer needs this local form.]

*Proof.* Take a coarse plaquette with phi := |W(dP) - 1| > eps_0. Put irr = rough and near plaquettes of the hull. Write Y_k(hull P) for the sum of f over rough plaquettes within D_T cells of the hull, D_T the reach of a tile; a near plaquette of the hull has its seed of Lambda_0 there, so ||f||_{l^1(irr)} <= (1 + C_M) Y_k(hull P). [This read Y_k(enlarged hull P), and then Y_k(hull P), until a reader asked which.] This can reach D_T cells outside H, and that fraction, at most C C_M L^{-2} of the weight of such a plaquette, is charged to its budget in 4.8, item 6. Then (5.5) gives phi <= 2 C_OS L^{-2} (1 + C_M) Y_k(hull P), or phi <= 2 C_OS E_k(hull P)^{1/2}. In the second case, phi <= phi^2 / eps_0 <= 4 C_OS^2 E_k(hull P) / eps_0. In either case

    phi <= 2 C_OS (1 + C_M) L^{-2} Y_k(hull P) + 4 C_OS^2 E_k(hull P) / eps_0.

Sum over such P, with each level-k plaquette within D_T cells of at most O hulls, O absolute since D_T << L, and multiply by c_F eps_0.  []

This is why near plaquettes are put on the l^1 side: their energy is not in F_{k,Om} at all, as they have a site in Lambda, but their l^1 weight is at most C_M Y_k, and L large makes L^{-2} C_M small.

*Why a cost linear in f.* A cost of order f^2 per rough plaquette looks natural, and I tried it first. It has no margin: a smooth W with curvature phi has children of curvature phi / L^2 with total f^2 equal to phi^2 by (R5), which is sharp. The entropy paid at each level then has to come out of the coefficient, c_F^{(k+1)} = c_F^{(k)} (1 - C g_k^2 log(1/g_k) + beta g_k^2), and along the flow the sum of g_k^2 log(1/g_k) over levels is about (log K)^2 / (2 beta log L), so c_F decays with K. **That fails.** With a cost linear in f, the l^1 term contracts by L^{-2}, because of (R7) and not of the action; the margin is there at every level, and no history is needed. Any cost f^a with 1 <= a < 2 has margin of order L^{4 - 2a}.

**Theorem 4.3 (large-field regeneration) [DERIVED from (4.1) and I(k)(a), (c); factorisation by Lemma 4.7a; the budget is written out in 4.8].** For W with rough set Lambda', and frozen variables on the refinement H of Lambda', which contains the hull of each rough plaquette, use mu_W as a probability measure on V|_H. For the classical part use superadditivity, F_{k, Om_1 u Om_2} >= F_{k, Om_1} + F_{k, Om_2} for disjoint regions, which holds at level 0 since A is a sum, and passes through the minimum. This decouples H from the exterior in the direction of an upper bound. If V is regular off H, the exterior minimum is exactly F_{k+1, Om'}(W). **If V is rough off H, the exterior has holes and it can lie below that minimum: this is what (4.3') is for, and the budget of 4.8, item 6, spends part of Y_k on it.** Off H the quantum part is compared with the vacuum using polymer bounds. Then, with Y_k(V) in the exponent net of the part spent in 4.8,

    G_{k+1}(W) <= sup_V exp( -[c_F eps_0 Y_k(V) + c_A E_k(V; H)] / g_k^2 ) * (C / g_k)^{n_f |H|} * C(L, M)^{|Y_0 \\ H|},

where (C / g_k)^{n_f |H|} is the comparison of Haar with the vacuum Gaussian, n_f the number of level-k variables per level-k cell, and Y_0 := H + collar of r_2 cubes, in which no fine expansion is available. By (4.1) the exponent is at most -2 c_F eps_0 Y_{k+1}(W) / g_k^2, and |H| <= C L^4 Y_{k+1}(W) / eps_0 since each rough coarse plaquette has phi > eps_0. Also |Y_0 \\ H| <= C r_2^4 Y_{k+1}(W) / eps_0, with r_2 of the order of log(1 / g). So the entropy is at most C(L, M) eps_0^{-1} (log(C / g_k) + r_2^4) Y_{k+1}(W), which is below (1/4) c_F eps_0 Y_{k+1}(W) / g_k^2 when g_k^2 (log(1/g_k))^4 << c_F eps_0^2 / C(L, M), true for g <= gamma. So the exponent is at most -(3/2) c_F eps_0 Y_{k+1}(W) / g_k^2, after the part of Y_k spent on strong blocks next to H (4.8, item 6), and g_{k+1}^{-2} <= (3/2) g_k^{-2} by (T2) of Proposition 8.4. **Monotonicity of the flow is not used.** Hence

    prod over P' of | z_{k+1}(P'; W) | <= exp( -c_F eps_0 Y_{k+1}(W) / g_{k+1}^2 ),

and G_{k+1}(W) is this, times the sum over families, times exp(EE^R_{k+1}(W)), whose exponent has either sign, with |EE^R| at most tiny per cube. [I wrote G_{k+1}(W) <= exp(...) here, which for regular W reads G <= 1. A reader noted that this contradicts the R polymers. I(k)(c) has the correct form, up to small.]

*Factorisation.* The hull H is a mandatory direct set in the local system of 4.7, with the reference Gaussian now of the form A on the refinement of Om', for this fixed W. Lemma 4.7a keeps exp f_Z as an upper bound only, so plaquettes straddling H may be dropped there by non-negativity, and superadditivity is used in the bound and not in the integrand. The activity of the mandatory polymer is relative to N_good[Om'](W), so r_{k+1} = exp(-g^{-2} F_{k+1,Om'} + EE^an_{k+1,Om'}) * (N_good[Om'](1) / N_good[T](1)) * (N / N_good[Om'])(W) / (N / N_good)(1). The vacuum ratio N_good[Om'](1) / N_good[T](1) is the comparison (C / g_k)^{n_f |H|} of Haar with the Gaussian, by Lemma 4.4b, times det(H^S_R A_{Om'}^{-1})^{1/2} <= exp(C L^2 Tr (H^S_R - A_{Om'}) / 2) <= exp(C(L) |d H|), since H^S_R >= A_{Om'} >= c L^{-2}, and the trace is local. This too is entropy per cube of H, so it is absorbed in the same margin: no boundary term B_0 survives, and I removed it from I(k)(c). *The polymer bound regenerates [corrected in this round].* Here Y_0 := Bad + collar of 4.7, which contains H and all rough cells of V, and so Col_k(P) for every old P, since r_2 cubes of side M L are wider than r_2 cubes of side M. An old P enters a new P' through the level-(k+1) cubes it touches. A connected tail of P outside Y_0 that touches N level-(k+1) cubes has at least c L (N / 16 - 1) level-k cubes, since it must cross all but the boundedly many that meet at one vertex; so exp(-kappa |P \\ Col_k(P)|) <= exp(-c kappa L (|P' \\ Y_0| / 16 - 1)). The first bounded layer, which that does not pay (one old cube at a vertex touches 16 new cubes, against exp(-kappa) only, and over L^3 positions of the tail), is charged to the core: it is at most C kappa + C log L per component of Y_0, against c_6 / g^2 per cube of Bad, or against the margin of 4.8 per rough plaquette of W. This pays the sum over old P inside P', and the new rate kappa, with room. Beyond the collar a good cube has f small, so its integral against the normalised Gaussian is at most 1 + small, with no factor C(L, M).

**No constant has degraded.** This is the answer to the charter's question, for the bound: a large-field region regenerates hypothesis (c) at the next step, with the same c_F, from (5.5) and the excess (4.3'), with no history and no tree count. The frozen variables are on H, for one step only. Hypotheses (a), (b) are in 4.5, 4.6.

**4.4 Recycling: rough cubes where W is regular.** Let W be regular on Om', and let V in the fiber have rough plaquettes. There are four types of cube at level k: small (white noise within the cut p(g)); mid (white noise above the cut and within p_2 of (CUT) item 3, no rough plaquette), weight at most exp(-p^2 / 3) with no entropy, since the Gaussian is normalised; far (white noise above p_2); direct (meeting Lambda(V) or S(V), that is a tile with a rough plaquette, or a tile with a strong block), integrated by Haar. A direct cube has activity relative to the regular replacement given by Lemmas 4.4a and 4.4b. Assembly: 4.7.

*Excess (Lemma 4.4a) [abelian identity DERIVED line by line; nonabelian form by Taylor at V^*, derived at the level of each term].* Claim: for W regular on Om' and V real in its fiber,

    F_{k,Om(V)}(V) >= F_{k+1,Om'}(W) + (c / 2) ||d_k B||^2_{Far} - (c_F / 2) eps_0 Y_k(V),                                     (4.3)

with Far and B defined below. So relative to the regular replacement **the net cost is at least (c_F / 2) eps_0 Y_k / g_k^2, with no restriction on Y_k.**

*The dichotomy.* Let C_S be the constant in F_{k+1,Om'}(W) <= F_{k+1,Om' \\ S}(W) + C_S eps_0^2 |S| for a union S of tiles of TT_{k+1} and W regular near S. *Proof, abelian, then by the choice of eps_0 [DERIVED; a reader found that my first proof was gauge dependent: the energy of a bump lift is C_up sum |v_c|^2, and at constant curvature on S of diameter D the flux forces |v| of order eps_0 D, which gives C_S of order D^2].* Let a_S be the minimal lift on Om' \\ S. Let a_up be the field that in block frames has all interior links trivial and every crossing link of a bond c equal to the coarse link of c. It has M = W exactly, a plaquette of it has holonomy 1 unless it sits at a hinge where four blocks meet, and there its holonomy is that of the coarse plaquette. So ||d a_up||^2 <= C L^2 eps_0^2 per coarse plaquette, gauge invariantly, in the nonabelian case too. v := a_S - a_up has Q_str v = 0 on bonds of Om' \\ S. Take the gauge e' of Lemma 4.5f, ||e'||_x <= C_LP n ||d v||_{N(x)}, and let chi be a cutoff at block scale, equal to 1 off a collar of S, and 0 on S. Then a_tr := a_up + chi e' - l([Q_str, chi] e') is a competitor on Om', with the energy of a_S off the collar, and on S and the collar at most C (||d a_up||^2 + ||f_S||^2) there, where the energy of f_S near S is at most C eps_0^2 per block by Lemma 4.5g. Nonlinear corrections are at most C(L, R) eps_0^3 per block. So C_S <= C L^2. It may depend on L, and no margin compares it with a constant that does not: it enters only through C', and what uses C' is below eps_0. Put C' := 32 C_S O (2 P)^4 / c_F. **Call a block strong for V if it lies in the hull of a coarse plaquette with Y_k(hull) >= C' eps_0.** Let S(V) be the union of the tiles of TT_{k+1} containing a strong block. There are at most O Y_k / (C' eps_0) strong hulls, of four blocks each, so |S(V)| <= 4 O (2 P)^4 Y_k / (C' eps_0), and

    C_S eps_0^2 |S(V)| <= (c_F / 8) eps_0 Y_k(V):

**the whole coarse energy of a strong block is paid by the rough cost, with a margin, however large the flux in it.** Off S(V), each hull has Y_k < C' eps_0, so every rough plaquette there has f_p < C' eps_0, and there are at most C' of them per hull. So V is near flat on all links there, including near links. All plaquettes are at most C' eps_0, which is far inside the analytic domain, since rho_* >> C' eps_0 by the choice of eps_0. The rule picks x_0 = y there, since s(y) <= C' eps_0 <= s_rule. And relative to V^* := V^*(W; Om' \\ S), V = exp(iB) V^* with B small and single valued, in a gauge by blocks with the map's frames, as on the regular slice with eps_0 replaced by C' eps_0. **There is no large flux outside S(V).** Let Omega_0 be the refinement of Om' \\ S(V), a union of tiles of TT_k by (T2), and Far := Omega_0 minus the tiles of TT_k meeting Lambda_0(V). So all its patterns are good, and it differs from Omega_0 minus Lambda_0(V) by at most (2 P)^4 cells per cell of Lambda_0(V). By superadditivity F_{k,Om(V)}(V) >= F_{k,Far}(V), and M(V) = W on every bond of Omega_0, with the same analytic branch of M as at V^*.

*Abelian identity.* With q_0, q_Far the forms on Omega_0 and Far, Delta := q_0 - q_Far >= 0, B^* the lift of W, so q_0 B^* = Q^* mu_* and (1/2) q_0(B^*) = F_{k+1}(W), and B with Q B = 0:

    (1/2) q_Far(B^* + B) = F_{k+1}(W) - (1/2) <B^*, Delta B^*> - <B, Delta B^*> + (1/2) q_Far(B),                                 (4.2)

using <B, q_0 B^*> = <mu_*, Q B> = 0. **This is an identity.** All forms depend only on d_k, and Delta has an exp-local kernel in plaquette variables about the holes, with absolute constants. With phi_* := sup |f_p(V^*)| near the holes, phi_* <= C L^{-sigma} eps_0 by Lemma 4.5h, (1/2) <B^*, Delta B^*> <= C phi_*^2 C_M (number of rough cells), and |<B, Delta B^*>| <= C phi_* sum_p exp(-delta d(p, Lambda)) |(d_k B)_p| <= C phi_* C_M Y_k, since |(d_k B)_p| <= f_p + phi_* and every near plaquette has weight at most C_M Y_k in l^1.

*Nonabelian.* Expand at V^*, which is regular, with Theorem G' and 4.5i: F_{k,Far}(exp(iB) V^*) = F_{k,Far}(V^*) + <grad F_{k,Far}(V^*), B> + (1/2) <B, Hess_Far B> + O(C(L, R_t) |B| ||B||^2). (i) F_{k,Far}(V^*) = F_{k,Omega_0}(V^*) - D_F(V^*), with D_F >= 0 analytic, second order at flat, so D_F(V^*) <= C phi_*^2 C_M (number of rough cells) up to a relative C(L, R_t) phi_*. (ii) <grad F_{k,Omega_0}(V^*), B> = <mu_*, Q^{V^*} B>, and exactness M(exp(iB) V^*) = W gives <mu_*, Q^{V^*} B> = -(1/2) mu_* . Hess M(B, B) + ...: **no coarse defect appears.** <grad D_F(V^*), B> <= C phi_* sum_p exp(-delta d) |(D B)_p| as in the abelian case. (iii) Hess_Far - mu_* . Hess M is at least c ||d_k B||^2_Far - C(L, R_t) eps_0 ||B||^2, and the cubic term is at most C(L, R_t) eps_0 ||B||^2 as well, with ||B||^2 over all links of Omega_0, near links included, since Hess M lives on tubes that pass through holes. *Bound on ||B|| [written out in this round; it replaces a remark about circulation].* Omega_0 is a union of whole blocks, and Q B = 0 holds on its bonds, up to second order. So Lemma 4.5f on Omega_0, not on Far, gives an analysis-only gauge, with F invariant, in which ||B||^2 <= C L^2 ||d_k B||^2_{Omega_0} = C L^2 ( ||d_k B||^2_Far + sum over plaquettes in holes of |(d_k B)_p|^2 ). In a hole |(d_k B)_p| <= f_p + phi_*, with f_p <= C' eps_0 off S(V), so the last sum is at most C (1 + C_M) eps_0 Y_k. A hole with a loop in Far needs no separate treatment, since its plaquettes are in the sum. So all these corrections are at most C(L, M, R_t) eps_0 ( ||d_k B||^2_Far + eps_0 Y_k ), and the second is far below c_F eps_0 Y_k by the choice of eps_0. *The term in D_F.* Gauge invariance of D_F gives <grad D_F(V^*), D lambda> = 0, so the linear term factors through the curvature, as <J, D B>, with J local to the holes, |J_p| <= C phi_* exp(-delta d(p, Lambda)), by the abelian kernel, up to C(L, R_t) eps_0^2. On non-rough plaquettes |(D B)_p| <= C' eps_0, so the sum is at most C phi_* (1 + C_M) Y_k. So the total loss off S(V) is at most C C_M L^{-sigma} eps_0 Y_k, below (c_F / 4) eps_0 Y_k for L large, with c_F absolute by Theorem 5.5. With the dichotomy this is (4.3).

*A shortcut that fails.* F_{k,Far}(V) >= min over the fiber of F_{k,Far} suggests a loss of second order in phi_*, with no term in f_p. **The first-order term is real:** a tube passes through a hole, so the hole's links can carry part of the constraint, and a rough plaquette with f_p carries coarse flux up to L^{-2} f_p by (5.5). So the energy drops by phi_* f_p, to first order. Without the regularity of the plaquettes in a hole, the hole would release the constraint of a whole bond, at a loss of order eps_0^2 per hole.

*What this replaced, and why.* (a) An earlier version treated a hole by a fine-lattice extension a_tilde with ||d a_tilde||_{hole} <= C ||da||_{collar}. **That is false for a hole with circulation:** if a flux tube passes through the hole and the collar is flat, then da = 0 on the collar, and any extension has non-zero flux in the hole. (b) An earlier version enlarged the hole to the set where B is large, with an analysis-only gauge whose kernel decays by spreading, a weak-type count of that set, and a co-area choice of level, and it needed a difference form of the field inequality for the coarse defect, which I had not derived. The dichotomy makes all of this unnecessary. I had dismissed dropping a whole coarse cell because one barely rough plaquette only pays c_F eps_0^2, below the coarse energy C eps_0^2. The threshold C' eps_0, absolute, separates the two cases: above it dropping is paid, and below it there is no large flux at all. (c) First versions bounded the shift by the fraction of paths through the rough set, which keeps a term f^2 and loses for dense rough sets, and treated F_{k+1}(W) as (1/2) |F'|^2, which misses its coarse-momentum dependence (R goes up to 12 at P = (pi, pi, pi, pi)). (d) For the center: the chart of (CUT) is centred at V^*(W; Om'), and (4.3) at V^*(W; Om' \\ S). They differ by an amount with ||d_k delta||^2 <= C eps_0^2 |S|, with tails at rate c / L, which enlarges C_S by an absolute factor and is the reason the collar is measured at that rate.

*Normalisation with direct cubes (Lemma 4.4b) [DERIVED at the level of each step].* Each cube of side M L is either **direct**, with all its links real Haar variables, or **chart**, with its links in the Gaussian chart. A bond is a chart bond only if both its blocks are in chart cubes; the crossing links of every other bond are direct. Conditionally on the interior links, a direct bond's crossing links are integrated against the exact probability measure of Lemma 1.1, whatever the interior links are. So **a chart variable never sees a constraint with frozen links**, and no lower bound on a mean is needed: mixed bonds impose nothing on interior links. Let R be the chart links, and X_R the restrictions to R of elements of X. Since the crossing links of a non-chart bond are not in R, X_R is exactly the space tangent to the constraints of chart bonds.
