**The reference Gaussian of (CUT) item 1 is the same for every direct set.** Under it, b_hat on R has covariance g^2 (A^{-1})_{RR}, whose inverse is H^S_R / g^2, with H^S_R the Schur complement: H^S_R (b) = min of <b', H_tau b'> over b' in X extending b from R. So the density of b_hat|_R with respect to Lebesgue on X_R is exp(-<b, H^S_R b> / (2 g^2)) / Norm_R, and a configuration with direct set eta has, relative to the configuration with no direct cube, the prefactor

    c(eta) := ( Norm_R / N_0(R) ) (coarse Gram)_R / ( ( Norm / N_0 ) (coarse Gram) ),     Norm_R = (2 pi g^2)^{dim X_R / 2} det( (A^{-1})_{RR} )^{1/2}.

*Jacobi's complementary minor.* Let D := { b in X : b|_R = 0 }, and r the restriction from the orthogonal complement of D onto X_R. Then det (A^{-1})_{RR} = det(r r^T) det(A^{-1}) det(A|_D) (=). r is a contraction, so det(r r^T) <= 1, and det(A|_D) <= ||A||^{dim D} <= C^{dim D}. **So no determinant of a form with a hole has to be estimated: Norm_R / Norm <= (C / g)^{dim D}, and the reference form is all that enters.** The gauge volumes give back (g / C)^{dim Lie(G_0) - dim Lie(G_0(R))}, and FP_R / FP and the coarse Gram matrices, which are banded, contribute C(L) per bond or site near eta. So

    c(eta) <= (C / g)^{n_rem} C(L)^{|N(eta)|},

with n_rem the physical variables removed, for which the factor 1 / g is the comparison of Haar with the Gaussian. An earlier version compared determinants by interlacing and Duhamel, with a chart that depended on eta; that chart spoiled strict locality at fixed xi' (below), and the estimate is not needed.

*Stiffness.* On X_R, ||B|| <= C L (||d_k B|| + ||Pi_0 d_k^* B||), with C absolute, by the bound in (CUT) item 1 on the chart region, which holds on any region, and with no constraint needed on a block that has no chart bond in some direction. The plaquettes between a chart cube and a direct cube are not rough, since a rough plaquette puts its cubes in eta, so their Hessian is regular. In a direct cube, the regular directions are not free: (4.3) gives a Gaussian lower bound on them, modulo the orbit, which has volume one, so they contribute (C g)^{dim} C(L, M) per cube, cancelling the factors 1 / g. Only near links of rough plaquettes and links of strong blocks, where the action is dropped, carry 1 / g. Call these H. With |H| <= C (C_M + L^4) Y_k / eps_0, the relative activity of a direct cube is at most exp(-c_3 |H| / g_k^2) (C / g_k)^{n |H|} C(L, M), with c_3 := c_F eps_0^2 / (C (C_M + L^4)).

An earlier version kept a Gaussian on part of a tube with frozen links, which needed a Jacobian bound on a mean of partly rotated paths, a threshold of one quarter on the frozen fraction, and a separate treatment of blocked tubes. **All of that is gone.** There, a family a_u with |m_a| near zero would have made M non-smooth in the exterior, and that would have been a real problem for a chart.

*The divergent count.* Per level-(k+1) cell the recycled gas has activity about L^4 M^{-4} exp(-c / g_k^2), tiny, at every level. Summed over all cells of T_{k+1} and over levels it is of order |T_phys| L^{4(K-k)} exp(-c / g_k^2), which diverges if c beta < 4. This is Proposition LF-count of the program [arithmetic re-derived]. In (1.2) its value at W = 1 goes into the named subtraction and cancels exactly. Its W-dependence is irrelevant after extraction, by Lemma 4.7c, with marginal shift of order exp(-c / g_k^2), summable in k. **That is where the 4D price goes.** Nothing in this scheme bounds the count, and no condition on c beta arises.

*Summation.* See 4.7.

**4.5 Theorem P (classical functional): a fixed point in the critical norm.**

*Why the earlier proof was replaced.* The first version of this section solved the nested constrained problem on the fine lattice in the norm sup |a| / eta + sup |grad a| / eta^2. That proof is correct on a box, where the Neumann Green's function has the free-space bound by reflection, but the gluing step used it on every patch of Om, and a patch next to a corner of Lambda is not a box. At a re-entrant edge of a free boundary the minimal field is singular: take the cross-section with an L-shaped domain; a curl-free, divergence-free field with natural boundary condition is the gradient of a function that is constant on the boundary, and that function behaves like r^{2/3} sin(2 theta / 3), so |F| ~ r^{-1/3}. In level-k units the fine curvature is then larger than eta^2 eps by a factor n^{1/3} at the edge, so the sup norm is not uniform in k. **This is not a counterexample to any conclusion, since r^{-1/3} is well inside l^2 and l^4, but the old proof did not cover it, and I had not noticed.** The route below uses no sup norm on the fine lattice and no derivative of a. It also makes the star harmless.

*The principle.* There are two kinds of constant. **Linear constants** belong to the abelian map at the trivial background: Lemma 4.5a, Lemma 7.1, Lemma 10.1, (5.4), the rates in Lemmas 4.5f to 4.5h. They are exact or absolute, free of L and of k, and they are the only ones allowed to enter a contraction or a margin. **Nonlinear constants** are the radius and the vertices of the map and of F_k beyond second order. They may depend on L and on R, and they must not depend on k. Since eps_0 is chosen after L, every nonlinear correction is smaller than any fixed power of 1/L that a margin asks for.

**Lemma 4.5a (the linear map on l^p) [DERIVED, exact].** Q_str acts componentwise. A bond c' along mu has weights q(c', b) = L^{-3} tri(b) on the mu-links of its two blocks, with row sum sum_b q(c', b) = L, by 5.1. For a fixed mu-link b, the two bonds that use it have tri values summing to 1, so the column sum is sum_{c'} q(c', b) = L^{-3}. By Holder,

    ||Q_str B||_{l^4(T_{i+1})} <= ||B||_{l^4(T_i)},      ||Q_str B||_{l^2(T_{i+1})} <= L^{-1} ||B||_{l^2(T_i)}.

Indeed |Q B(c')|^p <= L^{p-1} sum_b q(c', b) |B_b|^p, and summing over c' gives the factor L^{p-1} L^{-3}, which is 1 at p = 4 and L^{-2} at p = 2. **So l^4 is exactly critical, with norm one at every step, and l^2 contracts by 1/L.** Compositions Q_str^i have the same bounds with no constant.

**Lemma 4.5b (one step) [DERIVED].** For a level-i field, let h(y)(B) := avg_x B(path(y -> x)), the star term of 5.1, Xi(y) := exp(i h(y)(B)), and for a coarse bond c' = (y, y') put B'(c') := log[ Xi(y)^{-1} M(exp(iB))(c') Xi(y') ]. On the polydisc |B_b| < alpha_Y(L), with alpha_Y(L) <= c s_rule L^{-2}, plaquettes can exceed eps_0, since eps_0 is much smaller than alpha_Y(L), but s(y) <= C alpha_Y(L) L^2 <= s_rule, so the rule of 5.4 picks x_0 = y, and the means are non-zero, so B' is an analytic function of the finitely many variables B|_{hull c'}. Its linear part is Q_str B(c') by 5.1. So

    B' = Q_str B + Y(B),      |Y(B)(c')| <= C_Y(L) sup_{hull c'} |B|^2 <= C_Y(L) ||B||^2_{l^4(hull c')},

on the polydisc, also for complex B, since a sup norm is at most an l^4 norm. C_Y and alpha depend on L only, **and the same function Y occurs at every level**. By covariance of M, the gauge factors Xi at level-(i+1) sites only conjugate at the next level, so B' is gauge equivalent to M(exp(iB)) for all gauge-invariant purposes. [This is a finite-dimensional Taylor bound, and that is the point: at fixed L nothing about the star, about reflection cancellations, or about fine-lattice regularity is needed here.]

**Lemma 4.5c (composite map, no compounding through levels) [DERIVED, line by line].** Let a be a field on a region of T_0, A := ||a||_{l^4}, B_0 := a, B_{i+1} := (B_i)'. Let O_L be the overlap number of hulls and C' := C_Y(L) O_L^{1/2}. If 6 C' A <= 1 and 4 A < alpha_Y(L), then for every i,

    B_i = Q_str^i a + D_i,      ||D_i||_{l^2(T_i)} <= 6 C' A^2,      sup |B_i| <= 2 A.

*Proof.* D_{i+1} = Q_str D_i + Y(B_i), D_0 = 0. By Lemma 4.5b, ||Y(B)||_{l^2(T_{i+1})}^2 <= C_Y^2 sum_{c'} ||B||_{l^4(hull c')}^4 <= C'^2 ||B||^4_{l^4(T_i)}. By Lemma 4.5a, and since l^2 norms dominate l^4 norms on a lattice, ||B_i||_{l^4} <= A + x_i, with x_i := ||D_i||_{l^2}. So x_{i+1} <= L^{-1} x_i + C' (A + x_i)^2. If x_i <= 6 C' A^2, which is at most A, then x_{i+1} <= (6 / L + 4) C' A^2 <= 6 C' A^2 for L >= 3. The sup bound follows from sup <= l^4.  []

*Local form.* B_k(c) depends on a only in the two level-k blocks of c. Setting a to zero elsewhere gives |D_k(c)| <= 6 C' ||a||^2_{l^4(blocks of c)}. *Analyticity.* B_i and D_i are analytic in a, as compositions of analytic maps with uniform bounds on an l^4 ball, and also for complex a. So Cauchy gives ||dD_k(a)||_{l^4 -> l^2} <= C(L) ||a||_{l^4}, uniformly in k.

**Lemma 4.5d (gauge with the critical bound) [PROVED in `library/lattice_sobolev_box_gauge.md`].** On a box of side N, every link field has a gauge a' with ||a'||_{l^4} <= C_2 ||da||_{l^2} and ||a'||_{l^2} <= C_2 N ||da||_{l^2}, C_2 independent of N, and the same on a finite union of boxes with box overlaps. Only reflection and Fourier on a torus are used, so a re-entrant corner of the union does no harm. *Regions: no fattening.* A region is a union of level-k cells, with the crossing links between face-adjacent cells of the region. Two cells that are only diagonal neighbours share no site and no link, so there is no pinch on the lattice. Within radius R_t there are finitely many patterns of cells, all with good local patterns (3.2). A pattern need not be simply connected, and V can have large holonomy around a hole, so no small gauge need exist on it. **So patches are unwrapped:** let the patch at x be a finite simply connected piece of the universal cover of the local pattern, containing the intrinsic ball of radius R_t around x. V lifts to it, and has a small gauge there, since every loop bounds a surface of bounded area. There are finitely many unwrapped patches, up to symmetry, for a given R_t. **[Found at this stop: a finite simply connected piece of a universal cover containing a given ball is not automatic, and I have no proof that one exists for every pattern.** A simply connected open manifold need not be exhausted by compact simply connected pieces, as the Whitehead manifold shows, though that is not a cover of a finite pattern. [I compared this with Davis's manifolds too, and a reader pointed out, from memory, that those covers are unions of compact contractible pieces.] So I no longer ask for one. *The repair, abelian form derived here [nonabelian by the same Neumann series as Theorem G', at the level of this sentence].* Library Lemma 7 does not need simple connectivity. On a finite union P of lifted tiles it gives a'' with d a'' = da and a - a'' closed, possibly with periods pi(gamma) on a finite basis of loops. Take gamma a chain of bonds c_1, ..., c_m. A pair of adjacent cells is a box, so a closed form has the same integral over every contour of a bond, so Q_str of it on c_i is that integral, and pi(gamma) = sum_i ( v(c_i) - Q_str a''(c_i) ) exactly, with v := Q_str a. Since ||Q_str||_{l^4} = 1, |pi| <= C ( ||v||_{l^4} + ||da||_2 ), C depending on the pattern. Choose for each basis loop a closed form at cell scale with period one, amplitude at most C / n per link. So a is gauge equivalent to a'' + omega, with ||a'' + omega||_{l^4} <= C ( ||da||_2 + ||v||_{l^4} ), and ||a'' + omega||_{l^2} <= C n ( ||da||_2 + ||v||_{l^4} ). So patches need not be simply connected if the level-k data enter the bound, and in Theorem G' they are small by hypothesis, |v| < rho_*. Every loop of P bounds a surface in the cover, of area bounded by the pattern, so V has a small gauge on P, as before. Run Theorem G' with a(f) + omega(pi(v, f)) in place of a(f). A reader checked the abelian saddle in (f, pi) on paper, and it is invertible by the period formula, but **three steps of the proof of Theorem G' as written use simple connectivity: the right inverse of QQ, q = (QQ QQ^*)^{-1}, and the existence of Lambda. On a patch with loops they have to be redone with pi, and I have not done that, so "line by line" below holds for simply connected patches only.** Named in Obligations as an implication. *Where the cover is:* of the finite local pattern Om n box, not of Om.] *What the library has to supply on an unwrapped patch:* the gauge of Lemma 4.5d, by charts at the vertices of the cell lattice, library Lemma 7, for a finite simply connected pattern with good local patterns. [It said: with no condition except finite and simply connected. That is false (3.2).]

**Lemma 4.5e (lift) [DERIVED].** Let Omega be an unwrapped patch, and v level-k data on its bonds. There is a fine field with Q_str^{(k)} a = v and ||da||_2^2 <= C_up ||v||_2^2, with C_up absolute and independent of n and of the pattern. For a bond c, let b_c be a smooth bump on the mu-links of the central part of its tube, with Q_str b_c(c) = 1, |b_c| <= C n^{-1}, |grad b_c| <= C n^{-2}, supported in the blocks of c, so ||d b_c||_2^2 <= C. Transversally, bumps of different tubes do not overlap, and along mu, Q_str b_c(c') for the neighbouring bonds c' is small if the bump is near the crossing. So the level-k matrix Q_str b is diagonally dominant, and it is invertible with a local inverse, on any pattern. Take a = sum_c ((Q_str b)^{-1} v)(c) b_c. Conversely ||Q_2^{(k)} f||_2 <= ||f||_2 by Lemma 7.1, where Q_2^{(k)} is the direct average on plaquettes. On the whole torus the minimal lift obeys the sharper Lemma 10.1, in terms of ||d_k v||.

**Theorem G' (patch) [DERIVED, line by line given library Lemma 7].** Let Omega be an unwrapped patch of radius R: Om n box when that is simply connected, which is the covariant choice, and otherwise a finite union of lifted tiles n box, so in either case with good local patterns by Lemma 3.2a, since each local pattern of a region has one component and so lifts unchanged. It contains the intrinsic ball of radius R - D_T about its centre. There are rho_*, C depending on L, R, and not on k, such that for level-k data |v| < rho_* in the level-k slice S_k of Omega, the constrained stationary problem for the Wilson action on Omega_n with constraint M^k(exp(ia)) = exp(iv) modulo gauge has a unique small solution, analytic in v, and F_{k,Omega}(exp(iv)) := A(exp(ia(v))) is analytic on that polydisc, with

    F_{k,Omega}(exp(iv)) = (1/2) q_{n,Omega}(v) + sum_{m >= 3} T_m(v, ..., v),     |T_m(v, ..., v)| <= C^m |v|^m,

where q_{n,Omega} is the abelian form on Omega, with ||d_k v||^2 on plaquettes of Omega <= q_{n,Omega}(v) <= C_up ||v||^2.

*Proof.* Hilbert space: h := {da} on Omega_n with the l^2 norm. For f in h let a(f) be the linear gauge of Lemma 4.5d, so ||a(f)||_{l^4} <= C_2 ||f||_2. *Energy.* (exp(ia))_p = exp(i F_p(a)) with F_p(a) = (da)_p + N_p(a), |N_p(a)| <= C (sum_{b in p} |a_b|)^2, so ||N(a)||_2 <= C ||a||^2_{l^4}, and A_p = (1/2) |F_p|^2 + O(|F_p|^4), with sum_p |F_p|^4 <= C ||a||^4_{l^4}. So E(f) = (1/2) ||f||^2 + EE(f), with EE analytic on a ball of h and |EE(f)| <= C_1 ||f||_2^3. *Constraint.* Theta(B) := the small gauge transform of a level-k field into S_k, analytic with Theta(B) = Pi_k B + O(|B|^2), where Pi_k is the linear projection on S_k along gradients; this is a finite-dimensional inverse function statement, at fixed R. Then G(f) := Theta(B_k(a(f))) = QQ f + NN(f), with QQ f := Pi_k Q_str^k a(f), which depends on f only since gradients are killed, ||QQ|| <= C(R) by Lemma 7.1, and QQ has a right inverse bounded by Lemma 4.5e. By Lemma 4.5c, ||NN(f)||_2 <= C(L, R) ||f||_2^2. *Stationarity.* At v = 0 the linearisation of (f, mu) -> (f + dEE(f) - (QQ + dNN(f))^* mu, G(f)) is [[1, -QQ^*], [QQ, 0]], whose inverse is bounded by C(C_up), with no Poincare inequality needed, since on h the energy is exactly the identity form. The quantitative inverse function theorem in Hilbert space, with second derivatives bounded by C(L, R) through Cauchy, gives the solution and its analyticity. The second-order term is (1/2) <v, (QQ QQ^*)^{-1} v> = (1/2) q_{n,Omega}(v). Every constant is uniform in n because C_2, the bound ||QQ||, the right inverse, and Lemma 4.5c all are. *All link variations [a reader asked for this; the identification below needs it].* The fixed point is stationary along the slice {a(f)}. E and the constraint are gauge covariant, so it is stationary along gauge orbits too, and it remains to see that these span. Let Lambda be the linear map with d Lambda e = e - a(d e), which exists on the patch since e - a(d e) is closed; e -> a(d e) is bounded on l^2, uniformly in n, by library Lemma 7. An arbitrary link field e equals a(f) + d chi + [a, chi] as soon as chi + Lambda [a, chi] = Lambda e, up to constants, and with ||d Lambda [a, chi]||_2 <= C ||[a, chi]||_2 <= C ||a||_{l^4} ||chi||_{l^4} <= C ||a||_{l^4} ||d chi||_2, by Sobolev for chi of zero mean, this is solved by a Neumann series for ||a||_{l^4} small.  []

*Identification with the tropical F_{k,Omega} [rewritten in this round].* **My first version argued by induction on k at fixed R, that every regular slice at level k - 1 lies in the polydisc of Theorem G'. A reader pointed out that this is false:** a patch of radius R at level k has radius L^m R at level k - m, and rho_*(L, L^m R) shrinks with m, while a regular slice at level k - m has curvature eps_0 at unit scale, so it is not small in the critical norm of that patch. So the identification goes up, level by level, with convexity from Proposition 4.5i and not from Theorem G'. Let a be the solution of Theorem G' for data v at level k, and a_j := M^j(a) its image at level j <= k. Its curvature is at most E(hull)^{1/2} <= C (n_j / n)^sigma times the local sup of the level-k curvature, by Lemma 4.5h, plus a nonlinear term negligible by the choice of eps_0, so a_j is in the regular class at every level. (My first version cited sup |B_i| <= 2 A here, which is not enough, since A is of order R^3 eps and not below eps_0.) Claim: for j <= k, a_j is stationary for F_{j,Omega} under the constraint M^{k-j} = v, and F_{j,Omega}(a_j) = A(a). For j = 0 this is the definition of a. Let it hold at j - 1. Variations of a_{j-1} inside the fiber of a_j keep the level-k constraint, so a_{j-1} is stationary in that fiber. By 4.5i at level j - 1, F_{j-1,Omega} is strictly convex in the chart coordinate b of (CUT) item 1 on the regular part of a fiber, and any two regular points of a fiber are at |b| <= C L^2 eps_0 of each other by (REL), inside the class of radius epsilon_1. So a_{j-1} is the slice minimum: V^*_{j-1}(a_j) = a_{j-1}, and F_{j,Omega}(a_j) = F_{j-1,Omega}(a_{j-1}). The gradient of F_j at a_j is the multiplier of the lower problem, by the envelope theorem, and a variation of a_j under the level-k constraint lifts to a variation of a_{j-1} under the same constraint, so a_j is stationary. This proves the claim, jointly with the induction on k: 4.5i at level j - 1 needs Theorem G' and this identification on patches of radius R_t at level j - 1 only. **No lattice Uhlenbeck gauge is needed:** that was what stopped the energy-only class in the earlier 7.5.

*Consequences.* (i) *Hessian.* In a patch gauge, Hess F_{k,Omega}(V) = q_{n,Omega} + O(C(L, R) |v|), uniformly in k. With Lemma 7.1 and the Poincare bound of 4.6 on the one-step kernel, this is coercive on the slice for eps_0 small against C(L, R)^{-1} L^{-2}. So (COER) is immediate. (ii) *N_k.* The density is (exact second order) + O(C(L, R) (local |F|)^3), in a frame at the site. Lower bound: ||phi^{(k)}||_2 <= ||f||_2 (1 + C(L, R) sup_p E(hull p)^{1/2}) by Lemma 7.1 and Lemma 4.5c, so F_{k,Omega} >= c_A E_k, with c_A -> 1/2 as eps_0 -> 0. (iii) *Lift.* V^*(W) is the same solution at level k + 1 read at level k. Its level-k curvature is bounded by Lemma 4.5h below in general, and by the linear bound C_s L^{-2} sup |F'| of Text 9.4 in the interior of Om, up to corrections C(L, R) eps_0 sup |F'|, which are negligible by the choice of eps_0.

**Lemma 4.5f (local Poincare gauge for zero coarse data) [DERIVED, abelian; the proof was rewritten in this round].** Every pair of face-adjacent cells of a region has a bond, with its constraint, and a block with no neighbour in a direction has no bond there, and no need for one. Let the region have good local patterns. Let e be a fine link field on Omega_n with Q_str^{(k)} e = 0 on all bonds of Omega_k. There is a global gauge e' = e + d lambda with Q_str^{(k)} e' = 0 and ||e'||_{l^2(x)} <= C_LP n ||de||_{l^2(N(x))} for every cell x, N(x) its neighbours, with C_LP absolute. No unwrapping is needed here, even if the region has holes.

*Proof.* **The first proof here was false, as a reader showed.** It used a partition of unity at scale n in which, on a cell z, only z and its face neighbours occur. Where four cells x, z, u, v meet at a face of codimension 2, h_x vanishes on v, h_z on u, h_u on z, h_v on x, so at the corner every h is at most C / n, and they cannot sum to one. Library Lemma 7 had the same fault. **The charts must be indexed by vertices of the cell lattice, not by cells.** My second version used Whitney charts down to the lattice scale, and a reader showed that its bound on the constants was false, since fluxes accumulate in the tangential directions. No small charts are needed, as follows.

*Charts at scale n.* For a vertex v of the cell lattice and a component sigma of K(S_v), let U_{v,sigma} be the union over cells z of sigma of the box of side 5 n / 8 in z with corner at v, with the crossing links between face-adjacent cells of sigma. By library Lemma 7, Step 1, it has a gauge e_{v,sigma} = e - d lambda_{v,sigma} with ||e_{v,sigma}||_{l^2} <= C n ||de||_{l^2(U_{v,sigma})}, and sigma ranges over a finite list. *Partition.* On a cell z with corners v, let w_v(q) := prod_i psi(|q_i - v_i| / n), with psi = 1 on [0, 3/8] and 0 beyond 5/8, and let h_{v,sigma} := w_v / sum over corners of z of w, with sigma the component containing z. Across a crossing link from z to z', a corner of z off their common face has w_v = 0 there, since its normal distance is above 5 n / 8, and a corner on the common face is a corner of z' with the same sigma. So |d h| <= C / n. **At a corner where four kept cells meet they are all in one chart.** Put lambda := - sum h_j lambda_j. On chart i, up to the shift of the lattice product rule,

    e + d lambda = sum_j h_j e_j - sum_j (lambda_j - lambda_i) d h_j,

using sum_j d h_j = 0. Two charts that meet in a cell z overlap there in a box, so lambda_j - lambda_i = c^z_ij + (a function at most C n^2 ||de||_{local} in l^2), with c^z_ij a constant.

*Constants, from the constraint.* Let lambda^z be the box gauge on z, of block average 0, and lambda^{z z'} the box gauge on the box z u z', with the crossing links. Q_str(d lambda) is the coarse gradient of the block averages (from Q_root(d Lambda) = Lambda(y') - Lambda(y), and the star term), |Q_str e^{z z'}(c)| <= n^{-1} ||e^{z z'}||_2 <= C ||de||_{z u z'}, by the l^2 bound of 4.5a, and Q_str e = 0. So on z, lambda^{z z'} = lambda^z + const_z, and const_{z'} - const_z is at most C ||de||_{z u z'}. So the block-average gauges of face-adjacent cells agree up to C ||de||_{local}, as functions on the box z u z', up to the local Poincare term. Normalise lambda_{v,sigma} against lambda^{z_0} on one cell z_0 of sigma. Comparing inside the chart, with face-adjacent cells of sigma, it agrees with lambda^z on every cell z of sigma up to C ||de||_{local}. So |c^z_ij| <= C ||de||_{local} for all pairs. **The constants are controlled by the constraint, at scale n, with no accumulation, whether or not the region has loops at scale n:** this is why no Cech obstruction appears, while for non-zero coarse curvature it would. So ||e + d lambda||_{l^2(x)} <= C n ||de||_{N(x)}, with N(x) the cells that share a chart with x, and C absolute, as the patterns are a finite list.

*Q_str of the result* is the coarse gradient of the block averages of lambda, at most C ||de||_N. Remove it: let tau := sum_y lambdabar(y) psi_y + sum_y r(y) chi_y, with psi_y := sum over charts of h_{v,sigma} / |sigma| times 1[y in sigma], a partition of unity at scale n, Lipschitz along links, chi_y a bump inside block y of average one, and r(y) chosen to make the block average of tau exactly lambdabar(y). r(y) involves differences of lambdabar in a chart only, so ||d tau||_{l^2(x)} <= C n ||de||_N. With e' := e + d(lambda - tau) we get Q_str e' = 0 and the bound.  []

**Lemma 4.5g (exponential locality of the minimal lift) [DERIVED, abelian].** Let f^* = da^* be the minimal fine curvature on Omega_n for level-k data whose curvature vanishes outside a set S. Then for delta <= delta_0(C_LP), with rho the level-k distance from S,

    ||exp(delta rho) f^*||^2_{l^2(far from S)} <= C ||f^*||^2_{l^2(near S)},

with C, delta_0 independent of n and of the pattern of Omega. Likewise the multiplier obeys |mu(c)| <= C ||f^*||_{N(c)}.

*Proof.* Away from S the data are flat, so subtract a flat lift, which has zero energy, and get e with de = f^* and Q_str e = 0 there. Take e' from Lemma 4.5f. Let phi be Lipschitz at scale n, with slope delta / n relative to phi, equal to zero near S. Let l be a bounded local right inverse of Q_str, ||d l(c)||_2 <= C |c|. Test the Euler-Lagrange equation <f^*, d e''> = <mu, Q_str e''> with e'' := phi^2 e' - l([Q_str, phi^2] e'), for which Q_str e'' = 0:

    ||phi f^*||^2 = - <f^*, d(phi^2) ^ e'> + <f^*, d l([Q_str, phi^2] e')>.

The first term is at most C delta sum_x ||phi f^*||_x n^{-1} ||phi e'||_{l^2(x)} <= C delta C_LP ||phi f^*||^2, up to a bounded weight ratio over N(x) and up to the bounded region where phi is cut off. |[Q_str, phi^2] e'(c)| <= C delta phi^2(c) n^{-1} ||e'||_{N(c)} by 4.5a, which is at most C delta C_LP phi^2 ||f^*||_N, so the second term has the same bound. Absorb for delta small. For mu, test with l(delta_c).  []

So locality comes from a gauge-invariant energy estimate, and it survives re-entrant corners. At the trivial background and on the whole torus it agrees with the analytic symbol of 9.4.

**Lemma 4.5h (Morrey decay at depth) [DERIVED, abelian, absolute constants, with `library/lattice_gehring_hls.md`].** With f^* as above on an unwrapped patch, E(x; rho) the fine energy in the ball of radius rho in level-k units, for 1 / n <= rho <= 1 and data of size at most eps,

    E(x; rho) <= C rho^{2 sigma} ( E(x; 1) + eps^2 ),      sigma > 0 depending on the patterns only.

*Proof (RH) [DERIVED].* Let N be the side of a coarse cell in fine units, B = B(x, r) with 1 <= r <= N, the adapted ball of `library/lattice_gehring_hls.md`, Section 3: a union of boundedly many cubes of side r / 4, aligned with the nearest faces of the region, and comparable to the intrinsic ball. (A sup-norm ball cut by the region would not do: at a re-entrant corner it can have a thin tentacle, of aspect ratio r / (small), with a bad Poincare constant. I had this wrong until this round.) Take a_loc with d a_loc = f^* on 2B and ||a_loc||_{l^2(2B)} <= C ||f^*||_{l^{4/3}(2B)}, from library Lemma 7 in its l^{4/3} -> l^2 form, built from a' = K * f on each box, with |K(w)| <= C (1 + |w|)^{-3}, and Lemma 2 of `library/lattice_gehring_hls.md`. **It need not be gauge equivalent to a^* when 2B meets a loop of the pattern:** a_loc - a^* is then a closed form with periods, and that does no harm, because the test field below is any single-valued link field. Test the Euler-Lagrange equation <f^*, d e> = <mu, Q e>, valid for every link field e on the region, with e = chi^2 a_loc, chi a cutoff at B in 2B. Then ||chi f^*||^2 <= 2 ||chi f^*|| (C / r) ||a_loc||_{l^2(2B)} + |<mu, Q chi^2 a_loc>|. The weights of Q are at most N^{-3} with column sum N^{-3}, so with sup |mu| <= C eps, the last term is at most C eps N^{-3} ||a_loc||_{l^1(2B)} <= C eps N^{-3} r^2 ||a_loc||_{l^2(2B)}. So

    avg_B |f^*|^2 <= C ( avg_{2B} |f^*|^{4/3} )^{3/2} + C eps^2 N^{-4} (r / N)^2.

This is the reverse Holder inequality, with b absolute and h = C eps / N^2, the natural density, **at every scale from the lattice to the coarse cell, with no condition at block faces, since only the sup of the weights of Q enters**, and up to the free boundary, since the Euler-Lagrange equation has natural boundary conditions built in. Only the coarse pattern matters for the abelian minimal problem, because Q is a direct average at scale N; the finer block structure does not enter. **Gehring's lemma, proved on the lattice in `library/lattice_gehring_hls.md`, then gives f^* in l^{2 + eta} with eta > 0 and uniform constants,** and Holder gives the decay with 2 sigma = 4 eta / (2 + eta), and also a fraction C rho^a of the energy in any layer of thickness rho, which is what the boundary polymers in I(k)(b) use. A hole-filling proof, which is shorter, would need H^1 of the local annulus to vanish, and that fails at a corner where the local pattern has a loop. Nonlinear corrections to f^* are at most C(L, R_t) eps^2 in l^2, negligible by the choice of eps_0.  []

By Lemma 7.1 at level k within a level-(k+1) block, |f_p(V^*)|^2 <= E(hull p), and rho = 1 / L. So **|f_p(V^*)| <= C L^{-sigma} sup |F'|** everywhere on Om, corners included. Regularity of V^* needs only this, and so does the excess of 4.4, which compares with c_F, an absolute constant.

*What sigma is [separation of variables; not used in any bound].* Near a vertex with kept cone C(Sigma), Sigma a union of the 16 spherical tetrahedra of S^3, a closed and co-closed f with natural boundary conditions has the form d(r^s eta), with eta a co-closed eigenform of the Hodge Laplacian on Sigma, of eigenvalue lambda, with absolute boundary conditions. Since Maxwell in four dimensions has s^2 = lambda, and the energy is finite only for s > 0, the variational solution has |f| of the order of r^{s - 2}, with s = sqrt(lambda_1(Sigma)). So at r = 1 in a block of side L, |f_p| is of the order of L^{-2} (L / r)^{2 - s} = L^{-s}. **So sigma_true = min over patterns of sqrt(lambda_1(Sigma)).** For a straight re-entrant edge this gives s = 5/3, the r^{-1/3} I had in mind throughout. **Vertices and 3-fold corners are worse than edges:** in a 3-fold corner f is dual to the gradient of a Dirichlet solution, as at Fichera's corner, so the singularity is stronger than r^{-1/3}. The r^{-1/3} in this file is an example of why sup-norm bounds fail, and it is not the worst case. Nothing consumes its value, since Gehring's lemma gives sigma > 0 without knowing the exponent.

*Numerics, abelian, one step, under a confirmed lock, criteria written first:* `checks/hard-core--independent_lift_sup.py`, phi(L) the maximum of |f_p| over plaquettes by a linear program over coarse data with |F'| <= 1. First run, 5 plaquettes per pattern: **WEAK-SIGMA**, s := log(phi(3) / phi(7)) / log(7 / 3) = 0.948, 0.940, 1.018 for one block and two diagonal pairs, 1.34 to 1.47 for the rest. A jump in pattern "one" suggested that the selection of plaquettes missed corners at small L, and the follow-up, with 400 plaquettes and L up to 9, confirmed this: phi = 1.066, 0.665, 0.456, 0.333 (one), 1.090, 0.633, 0.428, 0.393 (diag2), 1.053, 0.652, 0.446, 0.326 (diag4), 1.015, 0.553, 0.327, 0.211 (full torus), with the worst plaquette at distance 1 from a removed block, at a vertex of a block. **Registered result: WEAK2-SIGMA,** s2 := log(phi(5) / phi(9)) / log(9 / 5) = 1.64 (full), 1.18 (one), 0.81 (diag2), 1.18 (diag4), against my threshold 1.2. The criterion did not fail (no s2 below 0.6), and the values fit the vertex exponent above, though L <= 9 does not reach an asymptotic regime. The last step of diag2, 0.428 to 0.393, is slow, an exponent 0.34 on one step, and I have no explanation beyond the sampling of plaquettes, which only gives lower bounds on phi. A third run, pattern diag2 only, 4000 plaquettes, criteria written first: phi = 0.4997, 0.3925, 0.3197 at L = 7, 9, 11, **PASS3**, s3 = 1.02, and s3' = 0.96 on the step before. So the slow step was a sampling artefact, as phi(7) moved up from 0.428. **phi L = 3.50, 3.53, 3.52 at L = 7, 9, 11**, and for pattern one phi L = 3.20, 3.32, 3.19, 3.00 at L = 3, 5, 7, 9 with the smaller scan. So on these patterns the data fit phi = 3.5 / L, that is sigma_true near 1, at L <= 11: lambda_1(Sigma) near 1, if the separation of variables above is the right reading. **These numbers support sigma > 0 and they do not determine it.** They do say that the loss in (4.3) is far above interior scaling: phi L^2 is about 39 at L = 11. Over all vertex patterns, see `checks/hard-core--independent_lift_scan.py` in the state of attack: exponent as low as 0.79 at L <= 9 on a removed snake of orthants.

**Proposition 4.5i (from patches to Om: one-shot locally, level by level globally) [DERIVED at the level of each step].** *Why not one global fine-lattice problem:* the covariant fine-lattice saddle on all of Om in a uniformly local critical norm has no global small gauge, transition functions of order one, and block averages of adjoint-valued gauge functions that are not covariant. I do not need it, as follows.

Let f_{k,x}(V) be the fine energy in cell x of the tropical minimiser. So f_{k+1,x'}(W) = sum_{x in B(x')} f_{k,x}(V^*(W)), which is exact, and sum_x f_{k,x} = F_{k,Om}. Let f^Omega_{k,x} be the same for a patch Omega containing B_r(x) n Om, and delta_k(r) the sup over the complex class of |f_{k,x} - f^Omega_{k,x}|. Claim: for r >= 1,

    delta_k(r) <= C_1 eps_0^2 exp(-c r),      C_1 a polynomial in L, c absolute, uniformly in k and in Om.                     (4.4)

*Step k -> k + 1.* Assume (4.4) at level k. Take a patch radius R_t = A L log L at level k, with A large. Theorem G' gives the Taylor bounds of f^Omega_{k,x} on a polydisc much larger than the class, and (4.4) with Cauchy adds at most C_1 exp(-c R_t) / c_2^2 to second derivatives on the class, which is small against L^{-2}. So H := Hess F_{k,Om}(V) is, in a frame at each site, exp-local with moments bounded by linear constants, and

    H = ||D_V .||^2 + Delta_q^V + O(C(L, R_t) eps_0),

where Delta_q := q_n - ||d_k .||^2 >= 0 by Lemma 7.1 is the abelian difference, covariantised along short paths. An IMS localisation at radius R_t, with positivity of Delta_q in a patch gauge, gives Delta_q^V >= - C R_t^{-2} ||B||^2, with C from the second moment of its kernel, which is small against L^{-2} for R_t of order L and above. ||D_V .||^2 itself is nearest-neighbour and needs no patch. On ker Q^V, ||B|| <= C L (||D_V B|| + ||D_V^* B||) by the bound in (CUT) item 1. **So the level-k saddle S_k is a bounded operator on l^2 of level-k links, invertible with norm polynomial in L, and its kernel decays at rate c / L by the conjugation of 7.3.** Near the free boundary of Om, a union of level-(k+1) cells, the constant in Poincare is C L^2 with C from the finite list of coarse patterns, by scale invariance. *Newton, written out in this round.* Fix W in the class, with its frames at block centres. Each patch solution of Theorem G' has one representative in the block-axial gauge of those frames, so **no transition functions are needed**: link by link, two patch solutions are compared directly, and they differ by at most poly(L) eps_0 exp(-c R_t / L) on an overlap, by the same comparison as below. Let V_app be, on each link, the value from the patch centred nearest to it. Its residual in the saddle equations is at most poly(L) eps_0 exp(-c R_t / L), so Newton with S_k, with second derivatives C(L, R_t), converges for eps_0 small, also for complex W, with correction u, |u| <= poly(L) eps_0 exp(-c R_t / L). In order that the correction not spoil the curvature bound, this must be below C L^{-sigma} eps_0, which asks R_t := A L log L, **with A large and absolute, and not R_t = C L as I had it**. That only enters nonlinear constants. So V^* stays inside the class, because the class contracts under the lift (Lemma 4.5h), for real and imaginary parts.

Then compare with the patch problem at level k + 1 and radius r, whose level-k form is a patch of radius r L. f_{k+1,x'} - f^{Omega'}_{k+1,x'} = sum_{x in B(x')} ( [f_{k,x} - f^Omega_{k,x}](V^*_glob) + [f^Omega_{k,x}(V^*_glob) - f^Omega_{k,x}(V^*_patch)] ). The first brackets sum to at most L^4 delta_k(r L). The second are at most L^4 sup |grad f| sup |V^*_glob - V^*_patch|, where V^*_glob - V^*_patch solves S_k with a source of size C eps_0 at the boundary of Omega, plus a smaller one from delta_k, so is at most poly(L) eps_0 exp(-c r). Thus

    delta_{k+1}(r) <= L^4 C_1 eps_0^2 exp(-c r L) + poly(L) eps_0^2 exp(-c r),

and for L^4 exp(-c (L - 1)) <= 1/2 this is at most C_1 eps_0^2 exp(-c r) with C_1 := 2 poly(L). **The recursion cannot compound, because the error from level k is exponentially smaller at the same physical distance.** The base case is delta_0 = 0: the Wilson density is strictly local. []

So one-shot is used only to get a priori bounds on small patches, where a small gauge exists and the critical norm works. Level-by-level is used only to pass to Om, where only the ratio L occurs and operators are bounded. **C_1 is polynomial in L with absolute constants** because the large constants C(L, R_t) of Theorem G' only ever appear multiplied by eps_0, so R_t = A L log L is consistent, and it is the choice of eps_0 that pays for it. Lambda is not fattened, so C_M is absolute, and the margin L^2 >= 8 C_OS O (1 + C_M) of Theorem 4.2 holds for L large against absolute constants. All local estimates are in the frames of unwrapped patches, and distances are intrinsic to Om.

*Hostile pass on this section.* (a) I looked for compounding in Lemma 4.5c: the recursion uses the same Y at each level, an l^2 contraction 1/L that is exact, and an l^4 bound of norm exactly one, so there is none. (b) Lemma 4.5b is soft, so C_Y(L) could be huge. That is harmless only because eps_0 comes after L. If a consumer needed an L-free nonlinear constant this route would not supply it. I checked the consumers: (FLOW) does not (Proposition 8.4). The contraction of polymers uses the class radius alpha / eps_0, not rho_* (4.6). (c) In Theorem G' the patch energy must be small, R^4 eps^2 << A_0(L)^2, in the patch gauge. So eps_0 is exceedingly small against 1/L. No lower bound on eps_0 is used anywhere: Theorems 4.2, 4.3 and 5.5 hold for smaller eps_0, as long as gamma comes last. (d) Library Lemma 7 is the only place where a pattern enters, and its constant is finite for each pattern and goes only into nonlinear constants, except at radius 3 in 4.5f, where the list is absolute. (e) Holonomy around a hole is large in general. Unwrapping handles it, since the density at x only sees an intrinsic neighbourhood, up to (4.4). The comparison (4.4) is between the global solution, lifted to the cover, and the patch solution, in the cover. (f) In 4.5i, a patch radius of order L is forced by the crude IMS commutator against the gap L^{-2}, and the logarithm by the Newton correction. An earlier version tried to keep R_t small, because a fattening of Lambda at scale R_t would have made C_M about L^4 and killed the margin of Theorem 4.2. Without fattening there is no such constraint. Lemmas 4.5f and 4.5g remain in use for the L-free bound sup |Pi| <= C sup |F'| in the excess. (g) I wrote this without reading the files `library/critical-class-and-excess.md`, `library/block-poincare-energy-package.md`, `library/convex-laplace-tree-decay.md`, which appeared in the shared directory and which I did not author; their names suggest they may overlap with this section. They may come from the sibling attempt, so I am leaving them unread as the charter requires. **The lead should compare them with this section.**

**The tree norm [written in this round, because a hostile reader showed that (EI) as it stood does not supply the cluster library. Nobody has read this part yet].** *What failed.* Cauchy in product form on the telescoped density Delta_{x,r} gives, for X inside the patch Lam(x, r), the bound exp(-c r) a^{|X|}, with a small, and with nothing that sees how X is spread over the patch: the distance from a cube of Lam to Lam is zero. Over all X in the patch this sums to exp(-c r + a (r / M L)^4), which diverges for r^3 above (M L)^4 / a, that is, at r of order g^{-1/3}. A hull does not help: a set of (8 L)^{4/3} cubes over a diameter D has bound exp(-c M L D) a^{|X|}, against a hull of size 8 L D. The one-difference bounds for far hole sets, and the far expansion of R polymers, have the same defect: decay in the farthest distance. **What is needed is decay in the length of a tree.** The first version of (EI) asserted that, by Cauchy, and I removed it because Cauchy does not give it. It needs its own induction on the level, and this is that induction. It uses no cancellation, for a reason that is the same as in (4.4): *a tree that is long at level k is exponentially longer than the same tree at level k + 1, and a tree that is short is controlled by Cauchy from the sup bound, which is one-shot.*

*Definitions.* tau_k(A; S): the least number of links off A of a subgraph of the region at level k, each component of which meets A, that contains the finite set S; tau_k(A; empty) = 0. [Links off A, and each component meeting A: a reader noted that with a connected subgraph the inequality below fails when A is a long polymer, since two subgraphs meeting A at different places are not connected. So moving the anchor from Y to a cube c of Y costs the links of Y, about M^4 per cube of side M, and this is why c_3 is below kappa_G by M^4.] A power series G(v) = sum_alpha G_alpha v^alpha in link variables v = (v_l^a), and in site variables below, anchored at A, has the norm, at radius rho_T and rate c_tau,

    ||G||_A := sum_alpha rho_T^{|alpha|} exp( c_tau tau_k(A; supp alpha) ) |G_alpha|.

Since tau_k(A; S_1 u S_2) <= tau_k(A; S_1) + tau_k(A; S_2), this is an algebra norm, ||G H||_A <= ||G||_A ||H||_A, and ||G||_{A'} <= ||G||_A for A in A'. *Site variables.* A **family** at level k is a set of sites U_i, at mutual distance at least L, each with two states (Lambda_i^{s_i}, V_i^{s_i}), s_i in {0, 1}, of a set of holes in U_i and a field on U_i regular off them. A function of s is a multilinear polynomial, G(s) = sum_Xi G^{[Xi]} s^Xi, with G^{[Xi]} the Mobius sum over Xi. Put the weight exp(c_tau tau_k(A; supp alpha, Xi)) on the coefficient of s^Xi v^alpha, with the subgraph meeting each U_i of Xi. Since s_i^2 = s_i only merges monomials, and the weight of a union is at most the product, this is still an algebra norm. [No radius above one is available for s, and none below one would keep the algebra, since the product of overlapping monomials gets a smaller weight. So the weight per site is 1, and the separation L is what makes the sums over sets of sites converge.] A site-free family is the empty one.

*Order of the constants.* c_tau <= min(delta_0, c) / 4, absolute, with delta_0 the rate of Lemma 4.5g and c the rate of the saddle kernel in level-(k + 1) units (4.5i). epsilon_1 := c / (C(L, R_t) L^2): Proposition 4.5i and Theorem G' hold on the class with this in place of eps_0, for the functional F_k only, with sup |f_{k,x}| <= C epsilon_1^2 and the same statements. rho_1 := c epsilon_1, so that a polydisc of radius rho_1 about a field of curvature at most epsilon_1 / 2 stays in that class. tau_0 := A_0 log L, N_0 := C tau_0^4 the number of link variables in a ball of radius tau_0, rho_T := L^{-A_T} epsilon_1, epsilon_2 := L^{-A_T'} rho_T, with A_0, A_T, A_T' absolute and large, and **eps_0 <= epsilon_2**. Note that the radius rho_T is far above every regular scale: eps_0, g p, and r_* are all small against it. That is the use of having chosen eps_0 after L, and it is what makes the states of far sites expandable.

**Lemma 4.5j (the adapted lift contracts amplitudes) [DERIVED, abelian, given Lemmas 4.5g, 4.5h and library Lemma 8].** Let a^{(c)} be the first-order lift of the bond data e_c at level k, abelian, trivial background, on any region with good local patterns, with Q_str a^{(c)} = e_c. There is a single-valued scalar lambda^{(c)}, in general not in G_y, such that a'^{(c)} := a^{(c)} + d lambda^{(c)} obeys, with d' the distance in level-(k + 1) units,

    | a'^{(c)}_l | <= C_P L^{-sigma} exp( - delta d'(l, c) ),      so      theta_L := sup_l sum_c | a'^{(c)}_l | exp( c_tau d'(l, c) ) <= C L^{-sigma},

with sigma <= 1/2 from Lemma 4.5h, and C_P, delta absolute. **Each bond has its own gauge, and that is allowed, since only the gauge-invariant functional is composed with this field.**

*Proof.* By Lemma 7.1 and Lemma 4.5h, the level-k curvature f := d_k a^{(c)} has sum over plaquettes in B(l, r) of |f_p|^2 at most C (r / L)^{2 sigma} exp(-2 delta d'(l, c)), for 1 <= r <= L, where the term eps^2 of 4.5h is the local sup of the multiplier, at most C exp(-delta d') by Lemma 4.5g. On the charts of the proof of 4.5f, at scale L, library Lemma 8 gives gauges e_j = a^{(c)} - d lambda_j with sup |e_j| <= C L^{-sigma} exp(-delta d'), and ||e_j||_{l^2} <= C L ||f||_{l^2(U_j)}. Let lambda^{(c)} := - sum_j h_j lambda_j. On chart i, a'^{(c)} = sum_j h_j e_j - sum_j (lambda_j - lambda_i) d h_j. By paths in a box, the non-constant part of lambda_j - lambda_i is at most C L sup |e|, so with |d h_j| <= C / L that part is at most C L^{-sigma} exp(-delta d'). The constants are controlled as in 4.5f, with the block averages at most C (1[c meets N(x)] + ||f||_{N(x)}) <= C exp(-delta d'), since |Q_str e| <= L^{-1} ||e||_{l^2} on a pair box, and they carry |d h_j| <= C / L. That is the claim. For Step 4 below: |lambda^{(c)}| <= C(L) exp(-delta d'), with C(L) polynomial, in the gauge of (CUT) item 1.  [] **On a bad pattern this Lemma is false** (3.2): the tiles are what it rests on.

The Feynman gauge of (CUT) item 1 is not adapted, and I do not need its amplitude. `checks/hard-core--independent_lift_l1.py`, under a confirmed lock, computes it anyway, P_L := sup_l sum_c |a^{(c)}_l| in that gauge, full torus and three patterns of removed blocks: 1.59, 1.37, 1.13, 0.94 (full), and 1.59, 1.40, 1.23, 1.09 (one), 1.59, 1.40, 1.23, 1.11 (diag2), 1.54, 1.37, 1.20, 1.11 (snake) at L = 3, 5, 7, 9. Registered **NEITHER-PL**: the ratio P_L(9) / P_L(5) is 0.69 to 0.81, against PASS at most 0.7 and FAIL at least 0.9. P_L L is 8.5 to 10 at L = 9 and still rising, with the worst link a crossing link. I wrote those criteria before finding that the requirement is only theta <= 1/2 and that the gauge may be adapted. So the numbers say that the condition is not delicate, and they do not test Lemma 4.5j.

**Lemma 4.5k (tree decay of the densities), (TREE) [DERIVED at the level of each step].** For a level-k region Om, a family as above, and base fields V_0[s], equal to V_i^{s_i} on U_i and to a fixed field off the sites, all of curvature at most epsilon_2 off the holes, let f_{k,x}^s(v) := f_{k,x}^{Om_s}(exp(i v) V_0[s]), and let T_k be the sup over all of these, and over x, of the norm ||.||_x of f_{k,x}^s(v) minus its value at v = 0, s = 0, so hole-only coefficients are included. Then T_k <= T_* := C L^4 N_0 epsilon_1 rho_T, uniformly in k, in Om and in the volume.

*Step 0: the base case.* f_{0,x} is the Wilson density of a cell, a finite sum of entire functions of boundedly many links, so T_0 <= C epsilon_1 rho_T.

*Step 1: composition.* Let W_0 have curvature at most epsilon_2, and V_0 := V^*(W_0), of curvature at most C L^{-sigma} epsilon_2. Suppose that for coarse variables w, and coarse site variables s, V^*[s](exp(i w) W_0[s]) is gauge equivalent to exp(i a(w, s)) V_0[s], with a(0, 0) = 0 and

    || a_l ||_{l^*} <= theta rho_T,    theta <= 1/2,                                                                                       (A)

in the norm of level k + 1, anchored at the coarse cell l^* of l. By gauge invariance, f_{k+1,x'}^s(exp(i w) W_0[s]) = sum_{x in B(x')} f_{k,x}^s(a(w, s)). A connected subgraph at level k with tau links meets at most 16 (1 + 2 tau / L) blocks, connected by bonds, so tau_{k+1} of its image is at most 15 + 32 tau / L. Substituting,

    T_{k+1} <= exp(15 c_tau) sum_{x in B(x')} sum_{(alpha, Xi) != 0} | f_{x, alpha, Xi} | exp( 32 c_tau tau / L ) (theta rho_T)^{|alpha|},       tau := tau_k(x; supp alpha, Xi).

*Step 2: short trees, by Cauchy.* For tau <= tau_0, supp alpha lies in a ball of radius tau_0 about x, and since tau_0 << L, at most one site meets that ball. So |f_{x, alpha, Xi}| <= 2 C epsilon_1^2 rho_1^{-|alpha|}, by Cauchy in those variables only, from the sup bound of (a), which is one-shot; for alpha = 0 it is at most 2 C epsilon_2^2. The sum over short terms of the right side is at most A_S := C epsilon_1^2 N_0 rho_T / rho_1 + C epsilon_2^2 <= C N_0 epsilon_1 rho_T, for N_0 rho_T / rho_1 <= 1/4.

*Step 3: long trees, by the change of scale.* For tau > tau_0, the term is at most rho_T^{|alpha|} exp(c_tau tau) |f_{x, alpha, Xi}| exp(-c_tau (1 - 32 / L) tau_0). So its sum is at most exp(-c_tau tau_0 / 2) T_k, and

    T_{k+1} <= exp(15 c_tau) L^4 ( A_S + exp(-c_tau tau_0 / 2) T_k ) <= C L^4 A_S + (1/2) T_k,

for A_0 large. **So T_k <= T_* for all k. This cannot compound, and it needs (A) with theta <= 1 only: no factor of L, and no cancellation, is asked of the lift.** A naive recursion for a coefficient of low degree on a short tree would read L^4 theta^m, which for m <= 4 is not below one, and for a quartic term is marginal, as it must be. That is why short trees are not in the recursion: they are bounded by Theorem G', through the sup bound.

*Step 4: (A), by Newton in the algebra.* Let lambda(w) := sum_c w_c lambda^{(c)} chi_c, with lambda^{(c)} of Lemma 4.5j in the frames of a patch at c of radius R_2 := A log L in coarse units, and chi_c a cutoff at that radius. Let u^{[1]}(w) := sum_c w_c a^{(c)}(V_0), the lift at V_0 in the gauge of (CUT) item 1, and V_1(w) := (exp(i u^{[1]}(w)) V_0)^{exp(i lambda(w))} =: exp(i a'(w)) V_0. In a patch gauge |v_0| <= C (L R_2)^2 epsilon_2, so by the vertex bound below the Hessian at V_0 differs from the abelian one by at most C(L) epsilon_2 / epsilon_1 on the patch; off it, the kernel of the saddle is below C(L) exp(-c R_2). So a'^{(c)}(V_0) obeys the bound of Lemma 4.5j up to L^{-A}, and the second-order part of a' is at most C(L) rho_T^2, with C(L) polynomial, since lambda^{(c)} and a^{(c)}(V_0) are. So || a' ||_{l^*} <= 2 theta_L rho_T + C(L) rho_T^2 <= (1/4) rho_T. Let v solve the saddle equations about V_1(w), in the gauge of (CUT) item 1. Their linear part is S(V_0) up to C(L) rho_T / epsilon_1, with the kernel bound of 4.5i: sum_{l'} |S^{-1}(l, l')| exp(c_tau d'(l, l')) <= C_S(L), polynomial in L. *There is no first-order source:* the gradient at a gauge rotation of a field is the rotation of the gradient, the multiplier rotates with it, and H a^{(c)} + Q^T mu^{(c)} = 0, grad F_k(V_0) + Q^T mu_0 = 0. The constraint is exact modulo a coarse gauge, up to a local second-order term, at most C_Y(L) rho_T^2, where C_Y(L) <= C L^4 is polynomial, by Cauchy on the polydisc of Lemma 4.5b. The nonlinear term of the gradient is controlled by the **vertex bound**: for ||u||_l <= rho_T / 2,

    || grad_l F_k(exp(i u) V_0) - grad_l F_k(V_0) - H u ||_l <= K ||u||^2,    K := C N_0^3 epsilon_1^2 rho_1^{-3} + C rho_T^{-3} exp(-c_tau tau_0 / 2) T_k,

from Cauchy on short trees, and T_k on long ones. With T_k <= T_* the second term is at most the first, for A_0 large against A_T, so K <= C N_0^3 / epsilon_1. The fixed point v = - S^{-1} N(v, w) contracts in the algebra if C_S(L) K rho_T <= 1/8, that is, if L^{-A_T} C_S(L) C N_0^3 <= 1/8, and this fixes A_T. It gives ||v|| <= (1/8) rho_T. By uniqueness in 4.5i the series is the true solution on the polydisc. So (A) holds with theta = 1/2, for rho_T small, in the field variables. The induction is on k, jointly with T_k <= T_*, and C_S, C_P, c_tau, tau_0, A_T do not depend on k.

*Step 5: site variables [written out in this round, after a first version that left the interface of a base field open].* Let the family at level k + 1 have sites U'_i, at mutual distance at least L in level-(k + 1) units, with states (Lambda'^{s}_i, W^{s}_i). Let V^0 be the solution with all s_i = 0, and V^i the solution with only site i in state 1. Both have curvature at most C L^{-sigma} epsilon_2 off their holes.

*(GRAD) The gradient at a base field is of the order of its curvature:* |d_l f_x^s(V_0)| <= C(L) epsilon_2 exp(-c_tau tau_k(x; l) / 2). For a patch B of radius tau_1 := A log L about x, radial transport gives V_0 = exp(i chi v_0) V_0^flat, with V_0^flat flat on the half patch and |v_0| <= C tau_1^2 epsilon_2, so d_l f_x(V_0) = d_l f_x(V_0^flat) + O(T_* rho_T^{-2} tau_1^2 epsilon_2), and T_* rho_T^{-2} is polynomial in L. The patch density at a flat field has zero gradient, being non-negative and zero there, and the difference from the patch density has gradient at most C_1 epsilon_1^2 exp(-c tau_1 / 2) / rho_1, by (4.4) and Cauchy, below epsilon_2. For tau_k(x; l) > tau_1, use T_*. A naive reading of T_* alone would give a source of order T_* / rho_T, about epsilon_1, far above rho_T, so this is needed.

*Comparison.* V^i and V^0 solve the same saddle off U'_i, with a source at most C(L) epsilon_2 at its boundary, by (GRAD). So zeta_i := log(V^i (V^0)^{-1}), in the gauge of (CUT) item 1, obeys |zeta_i(l)| <= C(L) epsilon_2 exp(-c d'(l, U'_i)) off the refinement of U'_i, by the comparison in 4.5i. *The family one level down.* Let U_i be the refinement of U'_i with a margin of A_5 log L in level-(k + 1) units, A_5 so large that |zeta_i| <= L^{-A_5'} epsilon_2 at the edge of the margin. The U_i are at distance at least L^2 / 2 at level k, so they form a family. The state s_i of U_i is the refined hole with V^{s_i} on U_i, with V^{0} read for s_i = 0 and V^i for s_i = 1, and V_0[s] := V^0 off the sites. At the edge of a site the mismatch is at most L^{-A_5'} epsilon_2, so V_0[s] has curvature at most epsilon_2 off its holes, **and the class of base fields is stable from level to level.**

*Saddle in s.* Let S[s] be the saddle operator at V_0[s] on Om_s, uniformly invertible, by (COER-G) on any union of whole blocks. Let S_i be S at V^i, and S_0 at V^0, Delta_i := S_i - S_0, G_0, G_i their inverses. Delta_i is near U_i, up to tails at the rate of zeta_i, and the coefficient of s^Xi in S[s] for |Xi| >= 2 is at most T_* rho_T^{-2} exp(-c_tau L / 2), by T_k. **The sites are at distance at least L in level-(k + 1) units,** so the T-matrices t_i := Delta_i (1 - G_i Delta_i) obey || t_i G_0 t_j || <= C(L) exp(-c L / 2) for i != j, in the kernel norm. Since (S_0 + sum_{i in I} Delta_i)^{-1} = G_0 - sum_i G_0 t_i G_0 + sum_{i != j} G_0 t_i G_0 t_j G_0 - ..., the coefficient of s^Xi is the sum over walks that visit exactly the sites of Xi. Each hop carries C(L) exp(-c d' / 2), so S[s]^{-1} has norm at most 2 C_S(L) in the algebra, with the sum over Xi convergent by the separation.

*Newton in s.* The residual of V_0[s] in the saddle equations is at the edges of the sites with s_i = 1, of size C(L) L^{-A_5'} epsilon_2, and for |Xi| >= 2 its coefficient is smaller by exp(-c_tau L / 2). The nonlinear part has the vertex bound of Step 4, with sites in it, and at most one site in a short ball. So a(0, s), with V^*[s] = exp(i a(0, s)) V_0[s], has norm at most C(L) L^{-A_5'} epsilon_2 <= rho_T / 8. *Jointly with w.* Step 4 runs at the base V_0[s], with u^{[1]} and lambda depending on s. The bound of Lemma 4.5j holds on each pattern, and for a bond c at distance d' from U'_i the two patterns have curvatures of the lift that differ by at most exp(-delta d') in energy, by 4.5g for the difference, and the gauge of Lemma 4.5j is linear in the curvature. So the coefficient of s_i in a'^{(c)}_l is at most C L^{-sigma} exp(-delta d'(l, U'_i) - delta d'(U'_i, c)), a path through the site, and the weight exp(c_tau tau') is covered. So (A) holds with s. Steps 1 to 3 go through, with one change: the image of a subgraph that meets U_i may be up to A_5 log L coarse links from U'_i, so there is a factor L^{c_tau A_5} per site of Xi, which for |Xi| = 1 multiplies T_*. This asks A_0 >= 2 A_5 + ... and A_5 (c - c_tau) >= 4 + A_T + A_5', and that is solvable since c_tau <= c / 4, in the order A_T, A_0, A_5, A_5', A_T', with no circle.  []

*Hostile pass.* (a) Is the sum over alpha in T_k finite at all? At k = 0, yes, and the recursion preserves it, by Step 3, given (A); (A) at level k uses only T_k. There is no loss of radius between levels, since theta <= 1 is all that composition asks. (b) Is the sup bound on the larger class honest? Proposition 4.5i and Theorem G' never use eps_0 except as a small parameter against C(L, R_t). The regular threshold of I(k) is a separate use of eps_0, and it is smaller. (c) Does the choice eps_0 <= epsilon_2 hurt anything? No lower bound on eps_0 is used anywhere (3.1). (d) Lemma 4.5j asks only sigma > 0, and that comes from Gehring. A worst pattern with a small sigma only means a larger L. (e) Is N_0 rho_T / rho_1 <= 1/4 compatible with tau_0? tau_0 is a multiple of log L, and rho_T / rho_1 is a large negative power of L, and A_0 is chosen against A_T, since only exp(-c_tau tau_0 / 2) has to beat L^{-2 A_T}: this needs A_0 at least 4 A_T / c_tau, and N_0 = C (A_0 log L)^4, so there is no circle. (f) tau_0 << L is what leaves one site per short ball, and it holds because all these exponents are absolute. That depended on C_Y(L) being polynomial, which it is, by Cauchy on a polydisc of radius of order L^{-2}.

**4.6 The analytic sector: the exterior polymer bound (EI) [DERIVED at the level of each step, given Lemma 4.5k, Theorem G' and the covariance decay of 7.3].** This section is about N_good of 4.7. Fix W regular, possibly complex in the class, and V^* = V^*(W).

*Coordinates.* On the fiber use the chart V(b) of (CUT) item 1, with the gauge device there, whose normalisation is an exact constant. The solution of the constraint has the structure of CMP 109 (2.9)-(2.13) as transcribed (LANE-READ), with a Feynman term for his exponential gauge, and with the second-order correction on crossing links. Then C := A^{-1} is coercive by Text 7, with kernel decaying at rate c / L in level-k units by 7.3, with a polynomial prefactor C(L). White noise: b = g (2^{-1/2} P xi + G_1 xi_1 + G_2 xi_2), as in (CUT) below, with all three product measures. In this section xi means all three. (A first version used maximal tree gauge here: see (CUT), item 1, for why that fails.)

*Expansion.* Psi(xi) := -g^{-2} R(b) + PP(b) + log JJ(b) + log FP(b), with b := b_hat(xi), where R is the remainder of F_k(V(b)) beyond second order in b, and PP the variation of EE_k.

*Local form.* F_{k,Om} = sum_x f_{k,x}. The integrand is gauge invariant, so f_{k,x}(V(b)) = f_{k,x}(exp(i u(b)) V^*), with u(b) = b on interior links and u(b) = b + (second order, on the two blocks) on crossing links, by the fiber flow of (CUT) item 1. Let R_x be this function of b minus its Taylor polynomial of order 2 at b = 0. The Taylor polynomials sum to F_{k+1}(W) + (1/2) <b, H b>, the linear term vanishing on X. So R = sum_x R_x, with R_x vanishing to third order at b = 0. By Lemma 4.5k, f_{k,x}(exp(i u) V^*) has tree norm at most T_*, at radius rho_T, and |b_hat| << rho_T on all of Dom. The polymers of EE_k carry the tree norm of I(k)(b), at radius rho_e := c alpha. log JJ is a sum over bonds, and log FP is the trace of the logarithm of a local operator with an inverse at rate c / L, so a Neumann series in the algebra gives it the same form.

*Localisation in xi: the tree norm.* Group xi in cubes of side M L in level-k units, that is, level-(k+1) cubes of side M, and define H(X; xi, W) as the Mobius sum in the support of xi over a set X of cubes. Resetting xi to 0 outside a set is invariant under the Ad rotation accompanying a gauge transformation of W, so H(X) is jointly gauge invariant. Put a multiplier t_c on xi_eff,c. By (CUT) item 1, b_hat_l is strictly local in xi_eff, |b_hat_l| <= g C(L) sum_{c near l} |t_c| |xi_eff,c|, and xi' enters only through the centre of the product measure, in stage (ii). A Mobius sum over X picks out the monomials in t whose cube support is exactly X. Put a_c := C_b(L) g |xi_eff,c| C(L) / rho_T, with C_b(L) := C exp(16 kappa_1), polynomial in L, which absorbs the weights at a corner of cubes, where tau_c(x; X) can still be 15; and tau_c the tree length in cubes. Substituting the series of b_hat in R_x, as in Step 1 of Lemma 4.5k, with c_tau M L >> kappa_G, and using that the series in t starts at degree 3 (a majorant series with non-negative coefficients bounded at radius 1 / (2 a) by 2 T_* is at most 2 T_* (2 a)^3 at radius 1 from degree 3 on),

    | H_x(X) | <= 16 T_* 2^{|X|} ( max_{c in X} a_c )^{max(3 - |X|, 0)} prod_{c in X} a_c exp( - kappa_1 tau_c(x; X) ),    kappa_1 := kappa_G / 2.

**This is the bound that was missing: the decay is in the length of a tree joining x and all of X, however many cubes it has.** Sum over x: there are (M L)^4 per cube, and the sum over the cube of x of exp(-kappa_1 (tau_c(x; X) - tau_c(X))) is at most C (1 + tau_c(X))^4, since all the cubes of a box can lie on minimal trees. For |xi_c| <= p, a_c <= a := C_b g p C(L) / rho_T, and g^{-2} a^3 T_* <= g p^3 C(L, epsilon_1). So for every set X,

    | H(X; xi, W) | <= H_0 exp( - (3/4) kappa_1 tau_c(X) ),     H_0 := C(L, M, epsilon_1) ( g p^3 + C_E g p ),

for g C(L, M) p <= 1, where C_E g p is from the terms that vanish only to first order: the old polymers and log FP, whose tree norm at radius rho_e gives g p / alpha per cube, at most 1. **Hull.** X need not be connected, and the cluster library asks for connected polymers. For each X choose one connected set of cubes Z(X) containing X, with |Z(X)| = tau_c(X) + 1. Put H~(Z) := sum over X with Z(X) = Z of H(X). It depends on xi in Z only, and sum_Z H~(Z) = sum_X H(X), and there are at most 2^{|Z|} sets X in Z, so

    | H~(Z; xi, W) | <= 2 H_0 exp( - kappa (|Z| - 1) ),   kappa := kappa_G / 4,                                                                  (EI)

for kappa_G large. **So kappa depends on M only, H_0 is small for g <= gamma, and the bound is uniform in k, W and the volume, and the library applies with no change.** *What failed here.* The version until two rounds ago asserted Taylor kernels at most C^m exp(-delta_0 tree) "by Theorem G' and Cauchy". That does not follow. The version of the last round replaced it by Cauchy in product form on Delta_{x,r}, which gives no tree (see "The tree norm" above), and it claimed a bound in |X| for sets X with no control of their spread. What holds is the kernel bound, in summed form, with its own induction.

*Output.* The variant of `library/ultralocal_cluster_expansion.md` in a Banach algebra, with the anchored norm in the coarse variable w at radius rho_e and rate c_3 per level-(k + 1) link, and b_0 = C c_3 M^4 <= kappa_G / 16, gives log int exp(sum_Z H~(Z)) dmu_cut = sum_Y H^#(Y; W), with ||H^#(Y; W)||_Y <= C H_0 exp(-r |Y|), where H~ is taken in the algebra at fixed xi, by composition with a(w) of Lemma 4.5k and with the kernels of the chart, which are analytic in w in the kernel norm, by Neumann series. **So far dependence of H^# on W is in tree form, and H^# is analytic in W and gauge invariant.** The polymers from the expansion are of size g p^3, and only the one-loop term is of order one.

*One-loop term.* By (8.1), L_k(V^*) - L_{k+1}(W). Differentiating in W gives a trace of C(W) against a local kernel, so it is local with the decay of 7.3, gauge invariant, analytic, and bounded by E_0(L, M). It feeds nothing back into itself, since the kernels above come from F_k and not from it.

*Closure.* e_{k+1} consists of the output above and the old polymers at B = 0, carried by 11.1 with xi = 1/L: contraction C M^{5 + a} L^{-a}, where the powers of M come from D^{2 + a} and c_2^{-2}, written (M / L)^a below for short. The recycled gas is not here: it is in the R sector of 4.7. So ||e_{k+1}|| <= C (M / L)^a ||e_k|| + E_0, and C_E <= 2 E_0 for L large: **no recursion in E_0 beyond this geometric sum.** [Derived given 11.1 and the items above.] **The tree norm of polymers closes as in Lemma 4.5k [DERIVED at the level of this paragraph].** Let T^e_k(Y) := ||e_k(Y)||_Y exp(kappa d(Y)), at radius rho_e and rate c_3. Compose the old polymer with a(w), with theta_L <= 1/2. A coefficient on a tree of length at most tau_3 := A (log L) / c_3 is bounded by Cauchy from sup |e_k(Y)| on the class, which is what carries the contraction of 11.1, at a cost in the number of variables that is paid by the room between rho_e and the radius of the class. A longer tree has exp(-c_3 tau_3 / 2), which beats the L^4 of positions. So T^e_{k+1} <= (1/2) T^e_k + C(L, M) (E_0 + H_0), with no compounding. Sites: tau_3 << L, so at most boundedly many far sites per cube of Y are within tau_3, and a site within (1 / c) log(C(L) / c_2) of Y moves the near data by more than rho_e, so it is held fixed, with a factor 2 per site, paid by kappa.

*The cut (CUT), for the R sector [ROUTE written step by step; the bound on z(Y) is Lemma 4.7b].* A chart cube has no rough plaquette, a sharp condition on b. But b = g ZZ xi, so with C^{1/2} of exponential tails this indicator is **not** a product in the white noise: it depends on all of xi, with a sharp edge. In Dimock II the analogous function is carried in the large-field characteristic functions, and his Rome lectures (p. 57 in the program's notes) call restoring its locality "a difficult technical problem", with no analyticity available. The first version of (EI) assumed a cut on xi alone and so missed this. An analytic cut (an erf of small width in b, then Cauchy in each cube) was my second attempt; it cannot serve alone, because the fluctuation chart ends at the edge of Dom, so the integrand is not defined where the analytic cut is only small and not zero.

Route.
1. *The chart: a strictly local white noise plus an exponentially local spectator [DERIVED at the level of each step; lines marked (=) are exact algebra].* **Conditioning and range both matter.** In maximal tree gauge the form has spectrum in [c L^{-4}, C], because a smooth mode of Landau amplitude 1 has tree-gauge amplitude L, so a polynomial needs degree L^2. Landau gauge in each block is not right either: any gauge discontinuous across block faces makes the crossing links larger by a factor L. A polynomial in a projected form has a range error: one step of a projection onto ker Q has range L, so degree L gives range L^2, which does not fit a cube of side M L when L >> M; and the same holds for the projection Pi_0 on zero block average that the gauge device needs. The construction that works is as follows. **The chart is fixed: it depends on W and on nothing else, not on the direct set.**

   *Coordinates on the fiber.* Let V^* := V^*(W; Om'). The fiber is invariant under G_y, the gauge functions with g(y) = 1 at block centres, and its tangent space is ker Q_root^{V^*}. Let E send a block function to a strictly local smooth function with avg_y E(a) = a_y = E(a)(y), and let h be the block functional with Q_root = Q_str + d_c h. Then T' := 1 + d E h is a strictly local bijection from ker Q_root onto X := ker Q_str, with T'^{-1} = 1 - d E h, and T' d lambda = d S lambda, where S := 1 - E(avg) is a strictly local bijection from Lie(G_y) onto Lie(G_0), the scalars with zero block average. Norms of T'^{+-1}, S^{+-1} are polynomial in L. This is why Q_str and G_0 are the right data, although the exact map is Q_root and G_y. A point of the fiber is V(b) := phi_g [ (exp(i b) V^*)^{theta(b)} ], with b in X, built in two steps. First the gauge rotation theta(b) := exp(-i E(h b)), a gauge function on sites, which is the nonlinear form of T'^{-1}: by covariance M_c((exp(i b) V^*)^theta) = theta(y) M_c(exp(i b) V^*) theta(y')^{-1}, and since Q_root b = d_c (h b) on X and E(a)(y) = a_y, the first order cancels. **The integrand is gauge invariant, under all level-k gauge functions, and Haar on direct links is invariant too, so it depends on b only through exp(i b) V^*, up to the second step, and the block functionals h of neighbouring blocks do not enter it.** Then the fiber flow of Lemma 2.1 on crossing links: on I(c), for a chart bond c, u -> u b_u g_c b_u^{-1}, with g_c := M_c(...)^{-1} W_c, of second order in b, so M_c = W_c exactly, with no implicit equation to solve. **The equations are decoupled:** the crossing links of c occur in the paths of no other bond (they are private, Text 1). So g_c is an analytic function of the links of the two blocks of c only; V(b) is a strictly local function of b; and the Jacobian is a product over bonds of local analytic functions. If the correction were spread by bumps, as I had it, neighbouring bonds would couple, the correction would have tails, and the indicators of the local system would not be strictly local at fixed xi'. Everything is covariant under coarse gauge rotations of W, with xi rotating by Ad.

That is what Lemma 4.7b uses. Tree gauge remains the coordinate for nothing in the chart; frozen and direct cubes use Haar.
2. *For fixed xi', the expansion needs no analyticity in xi.* Expand the product of local factors: (e^{Psi_X} 1_{no rough plaquette} - 1). Disjoint polymers are independent for the product measure, so the integrated weights form a hard-core gas with activities z(Y; xi'). Steps 3 to 5 of the cluster library proof use only a bound on |z(Y)|.
3. *Bound on z(Y), cubes of four kinds.* All in xi_eff. Let p_2 := c(L) eps_0 / g, with c(L) so small that |xi_eff| <= p_2 in the range of a cube forces every indicator of the cube to be 1. Small: all |xi_eff| <= p in the cube. Each factor is at most H_0. Mid: some |xi_eff| in (p, p_2]. The integrand is still analytic in b there, and it has no indicator, and g^{-2} |R(b)| <= theta ||xi_eff||^2, with theta small by the choice of eps_0: use the Gaussian integral with exp(theta ||xi_eff||^2), and such a cube costs at most (M L)^4 exp(-p^2 / 9) for |s| <= p / 2. Far: a chart cube with some |xi_eff| above p_2. The indicators keep b in Dom, so |b| < r_*, and the same bound holds with theta := C(L, R_t) r_*, with cost exp(-p_2^2 / 9). This is where the box is consumed. Direct: the cube has a rough plaquette or meets a strong block: next item. A far cube can change b_hat in its neighbours through P, whose range is below M L by the choice of M, and no further. So the bad set Bad is the union of the direct cubes, the cubes with some |xi_eff| above p_2, whether chart or direct, the neighbours of both of these, and the regions I(BB) of stage (ii); and Y_0 := Bad + collar. [Neighbours of direct cubes were missing until this round: a plaquette between a direct cube and a chart cube has an indicator that reads both states, so without them the indicators were not local to the sites.] Off Bad, b_hat depends only on variables at most p_2. **Inside Y_0 the only conditions on xi_eff are the ones that define Bad, and indicators on b_hat; outside Y_0 there are no indicators, only the product cuts of small cubes.** The expectation of cost above uses a real s; a cube with |xi'| above p_4 nearby is in BB.
4. *Large cubes.* See Lemma 4.7b. The conditional mean is real, and it is below g p only beyond r_2 = C log(1 / g). It need not be computed: in the core, integrate with the crude Gaussian from (4.3), and beyond r_2 the first-order term is below any power of g. An earlier version moved analytic polymers near Lambda into the gas. That is not needed: see I(k)(b), where the analytic sector is indexed by regions, and complex data never meet a hole, since holes occur only in the R sector, which is real in W.
5. *Decay in the hole set.* log det has Mobius decay, by `library/path_expansion_locality.md`; 4.7.
6. *Spectator xi'.* Stage (ii) of 4.7. **My first version asked for (amplitude of B') times p small, which fails as g -> 0 at fixed range. Only the imaginary direction matters.**
7. *No analyticity in W is needed in this sector.* The first version argued analyticity of z(Y; W) from a real contour independent of W. That does not cover polymers whose hole carries the constraint, and it is replaced by Lemma 4.7c.

So **there is no hard indicator in xi except through local functions, and no analyticity in xi is used.** The output is the hard-core gas of 4.4.

**4.7 One step in two sectors (GAS) [DERIVED at the level of each step, except where marked].** Text 4.4 and 4.6 give the costs. This section says how the sum over large sets is organised, and it replaces the earlier plan of exponentiating the recycled gas into analytic polymers. That plan had a real difficulty, found while writing it: a recycled polymer with a frozen hole is not analytic in W near the hole, since r_k is known only on fields real near Lambda, and a tube that is mostly frozen carries W only through frozen links. And without some regularity in W, the vacuum subtraction gains nothing, the activity is multiplied by L^4 per level, and LF-count diverges when c beta < 4, as it does here, because eps_0 is tiny.

*Two sectors.* Let A^{-1} = (1/2) P P^T + G_1 G_1^T + G_2 G_2^T be the chart of (CUT) item 1, with xi' := (xi_1, xi_2). Let **good** mean: no hole, and |xi_eff,c| <= p, |xi'_c| <= p_4 for every cube. On good cubes all indicators of the local system are 1, since |b| <= C(L) g p << r_*.
- **Analytic sector.** For a region Om' and its refinement Om, N_good[Om'](W) := the integral over good fluctuations of the cubes at distance at least L from the complement of Om, with the noise of the other cubes at xi_eff = 0, of exp(-g^{-2} F_{k,Om} + EE^an_{k,Om}), with no R-part, no G, and no hole. It is analytic in W on the class, since the cuts are on the product variables and the chart is analytic in W. Its logarithm is given by 4.6: (EI) with exponentially local kernels, in (xi, xi'), and the cluster library. So EE^an_{k+1} := log N_good(W) - log N_good(1) + g^{-2} F_{k+1}(W) - (normalisation) **depends only on F_k and EE^an_k: this sector closes on itself.** The flow of g is extracted from it, up to the tiny term below.
- **R sector.** G_{k+1}(W) := [N(W) / N_good(W)] / [N(1) / N_good(1)], for real W regular on the torus; for W with rough cells it carries rho_H as well, 4.8 item 10. Nothing in this sector is ever evaluated at complex W. Its elements are bounded functions, real at real w, with the bounds of Lemmas 4.7a to 4.7c.

*Statement I(k), revised.* For real V,

    r_k(V) = exp( -g_k^{-2} F_{k,Om}(V) + EE^an_k(V) + EE^R_k(V) ) * sum over families PP covering Lambda(V) of prod_{P in PP} z_k(P; V),

with PP a family of disjoint polymers, each containing a cluster of Lambda(V), EE^R_k = sum over Y disjoint from PP of (B_k(Y; V) + C_k(Y; V)), and
- |z_k(P; V)| <= exp( -c_F eps_0 Y_k(V; P) / g_k^2 - kappa |P \\ Col_k(P)| ), with Col_k(P) as in 3.3(c) ["core" and "collar" were used here without a definition until this round, and a term B_0 |core P| was carried that the margin absorbs, Theorem 4.3];
- |B_k(Y; V)| <= bb_k exp(-kappa |Y|): *born at the last step, bounded, no regularity in V is claimed*;
- |C_k(Y; V)| <= cc_k exp(-kappa |Y|) ( t_Y^2 + t_Y^3 ), t_Y := |F|_Y Vol(Y)^{1/2} / g_k, Vol(Y) := D(Y)^4, with |F|_Y the sup on the ball of radius D(Y) about Y, read with the weight of (TAIL): *carried, with no second moment left in the family, no regularity claimed beyond this bound, and no saturation*;
- bb_k, cc_k are tiny, summable along the flow (below).

The sum over PP is explicit, so at complex w nothing has to be divided by it; r_k^w may vanish at some real V near Lambda for complex w, and no logarithm of it is taken. There are no blocked tubes in this form (Lemma 4.4b).

*Sensitivity of the R polymers (TAIL) [statement changed in this round, after a hostile reader; DERIVED at the level of each step; not read by anyone yet].* The statement above, and Lemma 4.7c until two rounds ago, read as if an R polymer were a function of V on P or Y only. **That is false.** A polymer z(Y; W) born at this step depends on W far from Y through the centre V^*(W) and the operators of the chart, and the effect is real: the weight of a hole changes with the local curvature by a relative amount g^{-2} eps_0 |delta F^*|, which is not small until delta F^* is below g^2 / eps_0. Telescoping in patches, as in 4.5i, does not give strict supports with the decay the gas needs: a piece at patch radius r has size exp(-c r), on a support of order (r / M)^4 cubes, and the library's Dobrushin step pays exp(|Y|) for exclusion from Y. **The version of the last round expanded in far cubes by Cauchy in product form on a tube. That also fails:** the tube is capped at alpha, and beyond the cap the factor per far cube is g p C(L) / alpha, with no decay in the distance, so the sum over sets of far cubes diverges, in the volume. The same holds for its one-difference bound for far hole sets, whose decay is in the farthest distance only. What is true, and what the gas needs, is the tree form of Lemma 4.5k. Let N_1(P) := P + 1. For P, and for V real on N_1(P), expand in the field off N_1(P), and in the states of far sites, with the anchored norm at P with a **radius function**,

    ||G||_P := sum_{alpha, Xi} prod_l rho_P(l)^{alpha_l} exp( c_3 tau_k(P; supp alpha, Xi) ) | G_{alpha, Xi} |,      rho_P(l) := min( rho_e, c' g exp( c_1 d(l, P) ) ),

with c_1 = kappa_G / (2 M L), and rho_e = c alpha, c' small against C(L, M). [I had c_1 absolute. A reader showed that this fails: noise of a cube c lands on links l nearer to P, with a smaller radius, and with kernel rate below c_1 the series is not controlled there. The radius must grow no faster than the kernel decays.] The base field off N_1(P) is any field of curvature at most epsilon_2 off its holes, complex or real.

(TAIL) z_k(P; V), B_k(Y; V), C_k(Y; V) are defined at real V on N_1 of their rough sets (below), with no regularity there, and for such V they are power series in the far variables, whose norm ||.||_P obeys the bounds of the statement, up to a factor 2. Sites at distance below r_2 cubes from P are not variables: they are near data. In the bound of C_k, t_Y is read at the base field, with a weight: t_Y := sup_x exp(-c d(x, Y)) |F(V)_x| Vol(Y)^{1/2} / g, in level-k units, where Vol(Y) := D(Y)^4 and D(Y) is the diameter of Y. [A reader pointed out that |Y| in place of Vol(Y) is false for a thin loop: holonomy R^2 F costs F^2 R^4. Polynomial factors in |Y| are paid by exp(-kappa |Y|), since D(Y) <= M L |Y|.]

**[A gap found at the last stop, closed at this one by a strictly local centre; read by nobody yet.]** At birth there was a conflict between two kinds of sharp function. In the variable xi_eff the cuts are fixed, and the indicators of a chart cube that read the field, no rough plaquette, read V(b)[V^*(W)], which moves with far W, since V^*(W) is global. In the variable V the indicators are fixed, and the cuts on the noise move. So a sharp noise cut at a far cube, or at a cube of Y_0, was not analytic in far W in either variable. *Tries that did not do it, logged in the state of attack:* entire labels, one sharp function per term, Mobius into free sets.

*The repair: the chart need not be centred at the exact minimiser, so centre it at a field that depends on W only nearby.* Let R_c := A_c log(C + K - k) chart cubes, with A_c so large that exp(-c M R_c) <= g_k^{C} along the trajectories of Proposition 8.4, and R_c <= r_2 / 2. **It is a function of K - k and not of g_k,** since a lattice-valued function of g_k would make the chart, N_good and rho_j jump, against (T5) and item 12 of 4.8 [a reader]. For a block y let pi_y be the coordinatewise folding of an auxiliary torus of side 4 R_c cubes onto the ball of radius R_c about y; it sends a plaquette to a plaquette or to a degenerate one, and the region goes with it, and it is covariant. If T_{k+1} is smaller than that, as at the top levels, put V^# := V^*, and there is no far field to speak of. A pulled-back union of tiles has good patterns, since a tile and its mirror image are one box at the mirror, and the result is a facet times an interval.
