Let V^#(W) be, on the interior links of B(y), V^*(W o pi_y; Om' o pi_y), computed on the auxiliary torus, in the gauge of the block frames, and on a crossing bond the same with the map centred on that bond, corrected on its crossing links, which are private, so that M(V^#) = W exactly. So V^#(W) is in the fiber, it is regular on the refinement of Om', analytic on the class, with the constants of Proposition 4.5i and no dependence on R_c, and **V^# on a cube depends on W only within R_c of that cube.** By the comparison in 4.5i, in linear form, |V^# - V^*|_x <= C(L) exp(-c M R_c) sup_{x'} exp(-c d(x, x')) |F(W)_{x'}| in a patch gauge, so at most g^{C} eps_0, and **it vanishes at flat W, with the same weight as F^***, which is what a polymer with growth needs. Build (CUT) item 1 from A^#, whose entry at a pair (l, l') is the entry of Hess F_{k,Om} at V^*(W o pi; Om' o pi), pi centred at the pair. [A reader showed that the Hessian at V^# itself is not enough: F_k has tails, so H_1 would read far V^#, and P, b_hat and the sharp indicators with them.] So P^#, G_1^#, G_2^# are built from A^#, and **P^# is a strictly local function of W,** at range R_c + rho_P. b_hat = 2^{-1/2} g P^# xi_eff as before. What changes is a small part of Psi. F_k(V(b)[V^#]) has a linear part g^{-2} <J, b>, |J_x| <= C(L) exp(-c M R_c) times the same weighted sup of |F(W)|, which is g^{-1} <J, R xi>, at most g^{C - 1} |xi_c| per cube, far below H_0: it is a single-cube term of (EI), and it is zero at W = 1. Its quadratic form differs from A^# by a kernel of norm at most g^{C}. And there is a constant g^{-2} (F_k(V^#) - F_{k+1}(W)), of size at most g^{C}, which goes with the normalisation. In 4.4 the centres already differ, (d) there, and this adds g^{C} to delta. In 11.1, F_j(V_j) = F_{j+1}(W) holds exactly at V^*, and with V^# up to g^{2 C - 2}.

**So at fixed (xi_eff, xi'), V on a cube depends on W only within R_c of that cube, and every sharp function, the noise cuts and the field indicators alike, depends on W only within R_c of its cube.** Far W enters only through the centre s(W, xi') of the Gaussian of xi_eff, by the tails of G^#, and through far V in the tails of F_k, of EE^an_k and of old polymers, all analytic. A cube with a field indicator that is not constant is a direct cube, a far cube, or a neighbour of one, and it lies in Bad, at depth at least r_2 in Y_0. So W within R_c of it is inside the coarse image of Y, and is near data, real, as the statement of (TAIL) allows. On small and mid cubes off Bad the field indicators are identically 1, and there W can be complex. *What is rough, and the density step [rewritten after a reader. My first version charged a zone of radius R_c to every old polymer inside a new one, with a factor bb_k^{1/2}. That failed twice. A long polymer of n chart cubes has weight exp(-kappa n L) and a zone of about n R_c^3 cubes, with R_c^3 >> L. And a polymer whose zone contains a tube is not in Lemma 4.7c, has no second tiny factor, and in that version had radius rho' = rho / L + R_c, whose fixed point stops the shrinking, so that LF-count came back].* Give each R polymer a **rough set** Rg(Y) in Y. (TAIL) is read off Rg(Y): d(l, P) in the radius function means d(l, Rg(P)), and on Y minus N_1(Rg(Y)) the polymer is analytic. *At birth,* Rg(Y) is Bad in Y with a neighbourhood of radius R_c. Each direct or far cube has its own factor, exp(-c_3 / g^2) or exp(-p_2^2 / 3), shared with its neighbours in Bad, and a cube of I(BB) has exp(-c(L) p^2), all far below exp(-kappa C R_c^4), so the weight exp(-kappa |Y|) holds with Rg(Y) in Y, also for a long Bad. The tails of Y, small and mid cubes, are analytic. *For a term with an old polymer,* Z := Rg of that polymer, take any Z, with or without a tube. Exchange the product cuts on d(Z) for cuts on (v, xi^perp), cube by cube. A term where the two differ at one cube has a factor exp(-p^2 / 3), and it is rough only on Z and a ball of radius R_c about that cube: **that one ball is paid by that factor, since p_0 > 2.** What is left is the density step of Lemma 4.7c, with no Taylor expansion yet: Gamma(W) = int dv D(v; W) e(v; ...), with D the marginal density of V on Z, and all W dependence in D. In the variable v the centre enters only through D, so **D is smooth in W_b unless the whole tube of b lies in Z, and then D is supported on the surface M_b(v) = W_b, and W_b is real.** So Rg(Gamma) is the set of bonds whose tube lies in Z: its radius is rho / L, with no R_c. If that set is empty, the rest of Lemma 4.7c applies. If not, |Z| >= (L / M)^4 cubes, and the existing line for polymers containing a tube pays: |Y'| is about |Y| / L^4, against a weight exp(-kappa |Y|) and L^4 positions. **So a rough set shrinks by L per level, and it can grow back by R_c only at a cost exp(-p^2 / 3).** A zone of size log(1 / g) is harmless here, for the reason that I rejected it in an early version of Lemma 4.7c: that version gave it to every polymer, with nothing to pay for it.

*At birth, then.* Let W be real on the coarse neighbourhood of Y, and w the far coarse variables at radius rho_{Y'}(c). In the variable xi_eff the integrand of K(Y; zeta) is analytic in w: w moves V only on small and mid cubes off Bad, through V^#, by Step 4 of Lemma 4.5k, and since sum_c |a'^{(c)}_l| rho_{Y'}(c) exp(c_tau d'(l, c)) <= theta_L c' g exp(c_1 d'(l, Y')), by at most theta_L c' g C(L) in the norm of level k + 1; the lift of V^# is the lift of V^* on the pulled-back field, so the adapted bound holds, in the gauge per bond of Lemma 4.5j. The kernels of the chart move by the same amount in the kernel norm, by Neumann series in the algebra; (1 - Delta)^{1/2} is at rate s_P, which is why c_3 is below kappa_G / M. The centre s moves the Gaussian of xi_eff: a cost exp(C(L) (c' C(L))^2) per link, and the majorant series of a Gaussian density in the shift, integrated against the density, costs exp(C(L) c' C(L)) per link: so per cube a factor at most e, if c' is small against C(L, M), and this is paid by kappa. The action adds at most g^{-2} |J| |delta V|, at a direct cube, with |J| <= C eps_0, C c' eps_0 / g, far below c_3 / g^2, and at a mid cube, with |J| <= C g p, C c' p, far below p^2 / 3. Far sites: their effect on the near data is at most C(L) eps_0 exp(-c d') in the algebra, by Step 5 of Lemma 4.5k, below c' g at distance r_2.

*Closure.* For l at level k off N_1(Y), theta_L c' g_{k+1} exp(c_1 d'(l, Y')) <= rho_Y(l), for L large, since exp(c_1 L d') has all the room, and at the cap, theta_L rho_e <= rho_e, **and that is the only place where theta <= 1 is used for R polymers**. In term (2) and Lemma 4.7c the far field at fixed noise is the base field, so real noise is not expanded. Composition in the algebra at fixed noise, then the integral, gives the same bound, with a factor per cube of Y that kappa pays, and with the Holder gain of 4.7c unaffected, since the Taylor expansion of Gamma in the near curvature is Banach valued. *Consumption in stage (i).* At the next step, off N_1(P), put a multiplier t_c on the noise of cube c in the argument of the polymer only, not in the indicators. Then a_c := C(L) g |xi_c| / rho_P(c), and since the kernel decays at rate kappa_G per cube, and rho_P grows at half that rate, for every link l the noise of c on l, over rho_P(l), is at most a_c exp(-kappa_G d(c, l) / 2). So a_c <= 1/2 for |xi_c| <= p at distance above d_0 := (2 / kappa_G) log(2 C(L) p / c') cubes, and for a mid cube the same with p_2 in place of p. Nearer cubes are simply included in the polymer, a range in log p that stage (ii) already has. So z_k(P; V) = sum over sets X of far cubes and far direct components of z^{[X]}(P; V near, zeta_X), the Mobius terms, with

    | z^{[X]} | <= 2 sup |z_k| prod_{chart c in X} a_c exp( -(kappa_G / 2) tau_c(P; X) ).

**Each term has strict support, no logarithm of z_k is taken, and the sum over X converges uniformly in the volume, since it is over trees.** The set X attaches to P through its hull, as a connected part of a polymer. Sites at distance below r_2 are inside the collar of Y_0. So the structure of Lemma 4.7a is unchanged, with old polymers strictly local after this expansion.

**Lemma 4.7a (Mayer with partial sums, exact) [DERIVED].** Let lambda be a product measure over sites (cubes, or connected components of Bad, see below) on local states zeta_c, with a vacuum state, and f(zeta) a function on configurations, with f(vacuum) = 0. For a set Z of cubes, let f_Z(zeta) := f(zeta on Z, vacuum off Z), and HH(X) := sum_{Z in X} (-1)^{|X \\ Z|} f_Z, so sum_{X in Z} HH(X) = f_Z, by Mobius inversion. Then exp f = prod_X (1 + (e^{HH(X)} - 1)) = sum over disjoint families of prod K(Y_j), where K(Y) := the sum over connected families {X_i} with union Y of prod (e^{HH(X_i)} - 1), and disjoint Y_j involve independent variables. **If a site U is such that HH({U}) = f_U need not be small, keep exp f_U as a factor, unexpanded.** Then, at fixed Bad, with the sites of the gas being the connected components C_j of Bad + collar and the cubes off them,

    exp f = prod_j exp f_{C_j} prod_{X, not a single C_j} ( 1 + (e^{HH(X)} - 1) ),

and K(Y) is the product of exp f_{C_j} over the C_j in Y, times the sum over connected families of the X. **There is no partition.** [Until this round the sites near a hole were the components of Bad and the collar cubes, with the family inside Y_0 grouped as a signed sum over partitions, at most |Y_0|^{C |Y_0|} of them. A reader found that this has a factor exp(C n_0 log n_0) with n_0 about |Bad| r_2^4, which beats c_3 |Bad| / g^2 for a connected Bad of size above exp(c / (g^2 r_2^4)), so that the bound was not uniform in the volume; I had written log r_2 in place of log n_0. No partition is needed: the components of Y_0 are separated by a whole cube, and their Bad parts by 2 r_2 cubes, so what joins them is small.]

**So near a hole only upper bounds on partial exponentials are needed, and a partial exponential is the same integrand at a configuration with the other cubes reset to the vacuum.** No smallness of HH near the hole is used, and no logarithm is taken of an integrated quantity: f is the logarithm of an integrand, at fixed variables, and the z_k and G-terms are kept as factors, not in f, when they can vanish.

*The local system.* Fix xi', and fix W real regular. A configuration has a direct set eta, the cubes meeting Lambda(V) or S(V), as in Lemmas 4.4a, 4.4b. [It read: containing a rough plaquette or meeting a strong block. A reader noted that a tile reaches into a chart cube, and there the form q^R has no plaquette.] The state of a cube c is (eta_c, V_l for interior links in c, T_u for the crossing links of the non-chart bonds assigned to c, xi_l for links in c), with the product measure: Haar on V_l, the law of Lemma 1.1 at W_{c'} on (T_u)_{u in I(c')}, which does not depend on any interior link, and standard Gaussian on xi_l. **The state of a crossing link of a non-chart bond is T_u = a_u u b_u, and not u.** [A reader found that with u as the state, the conditional law of u depends on chart links through a_u, so the measure is not a product, and that resetting a chart block at fixed u gives M_c != W_c, out of the fiber, where (4.3') says nothing. With T_u fixed, u = a_u^{-1} T_u b_u^{-1} moves with the chart block, M_c = W_c holds in every partial configuration, and there is no Jacobian, since u -> T_u preserves Haar at fixed interior links.] On R(eta) the field is V(b_hat), with b_hat = 2^{-1/2} g P xi + B' from (CUT) item 1; on direct interior links it is V_l, and on crossing links of non-chart bonds it is a_u^{-1} T_u b_u^{-1}. Dummy variables integrate to one. **b_hat does not depend on eta**, and P is strictly local, so at fixed xi' **every indicator is a strictly local function of the states of a cube and its neighbours, so of one site of the gas**: for eta_c = 0, c meets neither Lambda(V) nor S(V), and b_hat is in Dom on c; both read within 2 P L + L level-k links of c, inside the neighbours of c, since M >= 8 P; for eta_c = 1, the reverse of the first two, read off V_l. My version of the last round let the chart depend on eta, with tails through its remainder, and an indicator of b would then have depended on far states, with no strict locality: that was a gap. The indicators are kept as factors, not in f, since they can vanish. The vacuum is: eta = 0, xi = 0, except on a component of I(BB), where |B'| can be large, and the vacuum of that site is b_hat = 0, the field V^*. The integrand relative to the product measure, and to its value at the vacuum at the same xi', is exp f, with

    f := -g^{-2} [ F_{k,Om}(V) - F_{k+1,Om'}(W) + (tau / 2) ||rho_R||^2 - (1/2) <b_hat_R, H^S_R b_hat_R> ] + EE^an_k(V) + log FP_R + log JJ + log c(eta) - (the same at the vacuum),

and the factors from the level-k gas and R-part are local bounded factors. With no direct cube, H^S_R = H_tau, the quadratic parts cancel, and this is Psi of 4.6.

*Sites of the gas, and what a partial sum evaluates [DERIVED; a gap found and fixed in the last round].* For fixed Bad the measure on the cubes off Bad has mass slightly below one, so normalise it: the overall factor is independent of W and cancels in the ratio, and the rest is a factor per cube of Bad, negligible. The factors z_k of old polymers stay outside f: at a true configuration |z_k(P; V)| is at most its bound, which reads Y_k(V; P) off direct links, so it is a product over the components of Bad and is independent of the chart variables that are being integrated. **A partial exponential evaluates the integrand at a configuration with some states reset to the vacuum, and that configuration must not be allowed a spurious gain.** If two adjacent direct cubes c, c' are in different blocks of a partition, then f_{c} has c' reset to the chart. In the direct cube c the links are Haar variables, with no gauge fixed, so the links of c near c' can be gauge-rotated against the chart by an amount of order one, at no cost in the true integrand, which is gauge invariant. In the partial configuration this interface is then rough on all of its (M L)^3 plaquettes, F drops them, and f_{c} can gain up to C_S eps_0^2 M^3 / g^2. The true cost of two barely rough plaquettes is only c_F eps_0^2 / g^2, so **the bound of Lemma 4.7a would be useless at adjacent direct cubes**. The repair has two parts. (i) Fix the set Bad, by the exact identity 1 = sum over Bad of 1[Bad(zeta) = Bad]. For fixed Bad the measure is still a product, over the **connected components of Bad, each taken as one site of Lemma 4.7a**, and over the other cubes, each with |xi_c| <= p_2 and eta_c = 0. Take as one site each component of Bad + collar (Lemma 4.7a). M L is larger than four times the range of P, and Bad parts of different sites are 2 r_2 cubes apart, so links next to one site do not feel the xi of another through P. So a partial sum never separates adjacent cubes of Bad, nor a collar from its core. (ii) Define f for all states with the hole set by direct links only. In a true configuration a rough plaquette has all its links in direct cubes, so Lambda and S are functions of the direct links, and Om_eta := Om(V) there. Off a component of Bad, |b_hat| <= c(L) eps_0, so resetting a cube next to a component changes interface plaquettes by at most c(L) eps_0, and the interface plaquettes stay inside the analytic domain of F_{k,Om_eta}. The function f so defined agrees with the integrand on true configurations, which is all that the exact Mobius identity needs, and Y_k of a partial configuration, read from its direct links, is additive over components. **So (4.3') applies to each partial configuration with the Y_k of its own components, and these sum to Y_k(V).**

*Quadratic bookkeeping (=).* Put xi_all := (xi, xi'), and R_all := (2^{-1/2} P, G_1, G_2), so R_all R_all^T = A^{-1}, b_hat = g R_all xi_all. Let R_R be R_all followed by the restriction to R. Then R_R^T H^S_R R_R is the orthogonal projection Pi_R on the range of R_R^T. So <b_hat_R, H^S_R b_hat_R> / g^2 = |Pi_R xi_all|^2 <= |xi_all|^2, for every direct set.

**Lemma 4.7b (partial-sum bound, integrated, joint in xi and xi') [DERIVED from (4.3'), Lemma 4.4b; two earlier versions were wrong].** There is an operator KK_eta >= 0 on xi', local to eta with tails at rate c / L, trace at most C(L) |N(eta)|, with ||KK_eta|| <= 1 - c_4, c_4 := c L^{-2}, such that for every Z, with H_Z the links of Lemma 4.4b in the direct part, and Mid_Z the cubes with some |xi_c| > p, and the gas and R factors included,

    int dlambda(zeta_Z) | exp f_Z | <= exp( -c_3 |H_Z| / g^2 ) (C / g)^{n |H_Z|} C(L, M)^{|Z|} exp( (1/2) <xi', KK_eta xi'> ),

with an extra exp(-(p^2 / 3) |Mid_Z|) when eta is empty there, an extra exp(-p_2^2 / 3) for each far cube in Z that is not next to a direct cube, and with a factor small with g per cube for good cubes at distance at least r_2 = C log(1 / g) from eta. This is what Lemma 4.7a asks: the exp f_{C_j} depend on disjoint variables, and the factors that join them are bounded pointwise in stage (i), item 3 (b).

*Proof.* The excess in its sharper form holds: with q_Far as in (4.2) and theta small,

    F_{k,Om(V)}(V) >= F_{k+1,Om'}(W) + (1/2) (1 - theta) q_Far(B) - (c_F / 2) eps_0 Y_k(V),                                     (4.3')

since (iii) of Lemma 4.4a loses only a relative C(L, R_t) eps_0 L^2, and the shift of centre delta between V^*(W; Om') and V^*(W; Om' \\ S) is charged to C_S: by Cauchy, with theta(x) up to 1/2 in the blocks near S, decaying exponentially in the block distance from S down to its small value, q(b - delta) >= (1 - theta) q(b) - (1 / theta) q(delta) block by block, and the sum of (1 / theta) q(delta) over blocks stays at most C eps_0^2 |S|, with C absolute. So theta in (4.3') depends on the position, with values up to 1/2. Add the gauge term. Let q^R be the minimum of q_Far + tau ||rho_R||^2 over the direct variables, at fixed b on R. With (=) above, on the chart variables the exponent, including the Gaussian of the product measure, is at most

    -(1/2) |xi_all|^2 + (1/2) |Pi_R xi_all|^2 - (1 - theta) <b_hat_R, q^R b_hat_R> / (2 g^2) + (c_F / 2) eps_0 Y_k / g^2.

Far from eta, H^S_R and q^R both equal H_tau, up to tails at rate c / L, so the positive part cancels there, up to theta. Near eta, q^R >= c L^{-2} by (COER-G), and H^S_R <= C, since it is at most ||A|| times the squared norm of an extension from R into X, and an element of X_R extends to X with norm at most C by putting the compensating weight on the direct part of each mixed tube, where the tube weight is at least comparable. So q^R >= c_4' H^S_R, and with K_R := H^S_R - (1 - theta) q^R, the exponent is -(1/2) <xi_all, (1 - theta' - KK) xi_all> + (c_F / 2) eps_0 Y_k / g^2, with KK := R_R^T (K_R)_{loc} R_R, local to eta, of norm at most 1 - c_4, and of trace at most C(L) |N(eta)| since the kernel of (K_R)_{loc} decays away from eta. Integrating xi_Z gives det(1 - KK_xx)^{-1/2}, at most C(L)^{|N(eta)|}, and the Schur complement in xi', which is the claim, with KK_eta := 1 - (the Schur complement of 1 - KK in xi'), of norm at most 1 - c_4. Direct links: a Gaussian on the regular ones as in Lemma 4.4b. The cost from z_k is exp(-c_F eps_0 Y_k / g^2), which leaves exp(-(c_F / 2) eps_0 Y_k / g^2) <= exp(-c_3 |H_Z| / g^2). For the difference from the vacuum, everywhere outside N(eta) the quadratic terms cancel exactly in f, and what is left is the mixed difference of (a), (b), (c) below.  []

**With xi' = 0 the bound is immediate from the factor 1/2 alone:** P^T A P <= 1 + e leaves -(1/4)(1 - e) |xi_Z|^2, and only F - F_{k+1} + gauge >= -(c_F / 2) eps_0 Y_k is used. What needs (COER-G) is the coefficient of |xi'|^2. For good xi', |xi'_c| <= p, the last factor is at most exp(C(L) p^2 |N(eta)|), and p^2 is polylog in 1 / g, far below c_3 / g^2. For bad xi' it is integrable, by ||KK_eta|| <= 1 - c_4, with a Gaussian of width L: exp(-c_4 |xi'_c|^2 / 2) is the activity. That width is harmless since L is fixed against g. An earlier version of this proof ignored B' altogether, and bounded the Gaussian as if b_hat were P xi alone: its bound was uniform in xi', which is false.

**A pointwise bound on f_Z is false near a direct cube.** There f has a term linear in b, g^{-2} <J(V_d), b>, with |J| of order eps_0 at the boundary of the direct region, since V_d is not close to V^* there, at least next to a strong block. In xi it is g^{-1} <P^T J, xi>, and completing the square gives exp(<J, C J> / (2 g^2)), with <J, C J> up to C L^2 (M L)^3 eps_0^2, much larger than the cost by itself. This is the conditional mean, and my first version dismissed it. It is harmless only because (4.3) is a bound on the sum, for all V, which already includes the relaxation of the exterior. At distance r_2 from eta, |J| <= eps_0 exp(-delta_0 r_2) <= g^C, so the conditional mean is below g p there, and outside the core the term is small pointwise. **So the core must be integrated with the crude Gaussian, and no fine expansion is available in it, with C(L, M) per cube; its size is polylog in 1 / g per direct cube, and this is paid by c_3 / g^2.**

*Crossing terms and (Z-BOUND) [DERIVED at the level of each step].* Let Y_0 := Bad + collar of radius r_2 cubes, with sites as in Lemma 4.7a. A set X of at least two sites is crossing. There are these cases. (a) X meets eta and a cube beyond the collar: by Lemma 4.5k with the components of eta as sites, |HH(X)| <= g^{-2} C(L, M) T_* 2^{|sites of X|} prod_{chart c in X} a_c exp(-kappa_1 tau_c(X)), and since tau_c(X) >= r_2 this is at most g^{C} exp(-(3/2) kappa tau_c(X)) (1 + |xi|), for r_2 a large multiple of (log 1 / g) / kappa. The same holds for X meeting several components of eta, with no chart cube. [This case had a one-difference bound, with decay in the farthest distance, until a reader showed that it does not sum over sets of far components beyond distance exp(c_6 / (3 g^2)).] (a') X meets a far cube of a chart, with no direct cube near, and a cube beyond its collar: as in (a), with the far cube a site of Lemma 4.5k, or in b: |b| < r_* << rho_T by the indicator, so a_c is replaced by r_* / rho_T there, however large the noise, and tau_c(X) >= r_2 gives the same g^C. [A reader noted that this case had no line.] (b) X meets a good cube of the collar and a good cube outside: this is the bound (EI). (c) X meets a mid cube: at most theta |xi|^2 exp(-(3/2) kappa tau_c(X)), from the cubic bound with theta small by the choice of eps_0, and it is absorbed in the Gaussian of that cube. (d) X meets eta and a far cube, through the Schur form. Exactly, with D := D(eta), for b_hat in X,

    <b_hat_R, H^S_R b_hat_R> = <b_hat, A b_hat> - <P_D A b_hat, (A|_D)^{-1} P_D A b_hat>,                                     (=)

since the harmonic extension of b_hat_R differs from b_hat by an element of D, with A-orthogonality. So the eta-dependence of the density is the last term. A P xi_c restricted to D decays at rate c / L away from c, so the mixed difference between eta and a cube c is at most C(L) |xi_near| |xi_c| exp(-c M d(c, eta)), and beyond the collar exp(-c M r_2) <= g^C; xi_near is unbounded inside Y_0, and the bound is absorbed by the Gaussian margin c_4 >> g^C there, and by the Gaussian of the cube outside. Between two far components of eta the same term has a Neumann series in the off-diagonal blocks of A|_D: since A|_D >= c L^{-2} and the off-diagonal blocks are at most C(L) exp(-c M) << L^{-2}, it converges, and the mixed difference decays with the distance. log det(A|_D) in c(eta) has the same Mobius decay, by `library/path_expansion_locality.md`. So the crossing bonds are small and they attach further cubes with the factor exp(-kappa |Y \\ Y_0|). With Lemma 4.7a and Lemma 4.7b on each site of Y_0 this gives the bound of stage (i).

*Stage (i), written out [DERIVED at the level of each step].* Fix xi'. Treat first all |xi'_c| <= p.

1. *Sum over Bad.* Insert 1 = sum over Bad of 1[Bad(zeta) = Bad]. For fixed Bad the measure is a product over the components C_j of Y_0 := Bad + collar and the other cubes, each with |xi_eff,c| <= p_2 and eta_c = 0. So two polymers of the gas, over the sum on Bad, are compatible only if their Y_0 are not adjacent: the exclusion is from Y + 1 layer of Y_0, at a cost per cube of Y_0 that c_6 / g^2 pays.
2. *Mayer.* exp f as in Lemma 4.7a, exactly, with f agreeing with the integrand on true configurations. Old polymers are kept as factors: the mandatory z_k are expanded by (TAIL), and each B_k + C_k is written 1 + (e^{B_k + C_k} - 1), a chosen factor being one more term of the Mayer expansion, with support N_1(Y) and with its far expansion by (TAIL). So f contains EE^an_k only, and it is analytic in b off the chosen N_1(Y). [A reader found that "the integrand is analytic in b" was false on small and mid cubes while B_k, C_k were in f, since they are only measurable on N_1. See stage (ii).]
3. *Activity with a bad cube, as one chain.* Let Y be a polymer with bad part Bad(Y), Y_0 := Y n (Bad(Y) + r_2), n_0 its number of cubes, so n_0 <= C |Bad(Y)| r_2^4. |Theta| <= 1 throughout.
   (a) By Lemma 4.7a, K(Y) is the product of exp f_{C_j} over the sites C_j of Y_0, times the sum over connected attachments of prod (e^{HH(X)} - 1), over the other X.
   (b) For X joining a site C_j to anything else, |HH(X)| <= exp(-(3/2) kappa tau_c(X)) sum_{c' in X} exp(-kappa d(c', X \\ C_j)) ( (H_0 + g^C) (1 + |xi_all,c'|^2) + theta 1[c' mid] |xi_all,c'|^2 ), by (a) to (d) above, where d(c', X \\ C_j) is the distance from a cube of the site to the rest of X, so a cube deep in a site does not count. [A reader found two errors in the first form: with a collar cube at mid noise the term g |xi|^3 is above H_0 |xi|^2, so theta is needed, as in (c); and with no distance weight the coefficient of |xi_c|^2 summed to H_0 times the boundary of the site, which beats the margin c_4 for a large site.] The term g^C is the effect of the bad part, across the collar. The term H_0 is that of a collar cube next to a cube outside, which is (EI). The same with H_0 for X outside Y_0; X is grouped by its hull as in (EI), which gives the factor exp(-kappa |Z|) for a connected Z. So |e^{HH} - 1| <= |HH| e^{|HH|}, and the exponentials together are at most exp(sum_c (C H_0 + theta) (1 + |xi_all,c|^2)), with the sum per cube bounded by the weights, and C H_0 + theta below the margin.
   (c) Integrate the cubes off Y_0, against a probability measure: each attachment gives its H_0, and the attached part gives exp(-kappa |Y \\ Y_0|). The term H_0 |xi_c|^2 is below the margin there, 1/2 - theta.
   (d) Integrate each C_j. (C H_0 + theta) |xi_c|^2 is below the margin c_4 = c L^{-2} of Lemma 4.7b, since theta is small by the choice of eps_0 after L and L is fixed against g. By Lemma 4.7b, with Z = C_j, the integral is at most exp(-c_3 |H_j| / g^2) (C / g)^{n |H_j|} C(L, M)^{|C_j|} exp((1/2) <xi', KK_{eta_j} xi'>) exp(-(p_2^2 / 3) |far(C_j)|).
   (e) For good xi', exp((1/2) <xi', KK xi'>) <= exp(C(L) p^2 n_0). So in all,

    |z(Y)| <= exp( -c_3 |H| / g^2 - (p_2^2 / 3) |far(Y)| + n |H| log(C / g) + C(L, M, p^2) n_0 ) exp(-kappa |Y \\ Y_0|).

   (f) Since |H| >= |direct(Y)| and |far(Y)| + |direct(Y)| >= c |Bad(Y)|, and p_2^2 is a fixed multiple of g^{-2}, the positive terms are at most C(L, M) |Bad(Y)| r_2^4 p^2 + C |H| log(1 / g), and these are below a quarter of the negative ones, since r_2^4 p^2 and log(1 / g) are polylogarithmic in 1 / g, **with nothing that grows with |Bad(Y)| per cube**. So **|z(Y)| <= exp(-c_6 |Bad(Y)| / g^2) exp(-kappa |Y \\ Y_0|) <= exp(-c_6 |Y_0| / (C g^2 r_2^4)) exp(-kappa |Y \\ Y_0|)**, with c_6 depending on L, M, eps_0: the rate per cube of Y_0 goes to infinity with 1 / g.
4. *Activity with no bad cube.* A cube with |xi_c| in (p, p_2] has an extra exp(-p^2 / 3), and cubes are joined by HH(X), at most (g p^3 C + theta |xi|^2) exp(-kappa |X|), by (EI) on small cubes. On a mid cube (EI) holds with a_c = C_b g |xi_c| C(L) / rho_T, at most C_b c(L) eps_0 C(L) / rho_T << 1, and for the polymers with g |xi_c| C(L) / rho_e <= c(L) C(L) / (c c_2), so c(L) is small against c_2 / C(L). For X = {c} the third-order vanishing gives g^{-2} T_* a_c^3 <= theta |xi_c|^2, with theta := a_c C_b^2 C(L)^2 T_* rho_T^{-2}, small since eps_0 <= L^{-A_T'} rho_T. So |z(Y)| <= H_0 exp(-kappa |Y| / 2), with H_0 := C(L, M, eps_0) g p^3.
5. *Gas.* In each case z(Y) fits the variant of `library/ultralocal_cluster_expansion.md`, so Steps 3 to 5 there give Xi(xi') != 0, also at complex w, where only upper bounds on activities were used, and log Xi(xi') = sum_Y H^#(Y; xi'), with |H^#(Y)| <= C H_0 exp(-r |Y|).
6. *Ratio to the good sector.* Put a parameter s in front of the activities of polymers with a non-small cube. Everything is analytic in s for |s| <= s_0 := min( exp(c_6 / (2 g^2 r_2^4)), exp(p^2 / 6) ), and at s = 0 this is the expansion of N_good. By the Schwarz lemma in s, H^#(Y) - H^#_good(Y) is at most C H_0 exp(-r |Y|) / s_0. **This is the bound bb^new_k of the Output, up to the constants, with no separate analysis of the ratio.**
7. *Mandatory polymers.* Polymers that contain a rough plaquette of W, or the frozen hull of Theorem 4.3, are mandatory. They are not exponentiated. The hard-core exclusion of the rest from them gives a factor exp(-sum_{P meets Y} e(P)), and a Mayer expansion of this factor attaches those P to Y, keeping the sum over disjoint families. That is the new family PP' at level k + 1.

*Stage (ii) [rewritten in this round, with the effective noise].* At fixed xi' every indicator and every cut is in xi_eff, so xi' enters only through the centre s of a product Gaussian. Put t_c as a multiplier of xi'_c in s. A polymer H^#(Y; t) sees s on Y only, and s on Y moves by C_s(L) |t_c| |xi'_c| exp(-kappa_G d(c, Y)). The density obeys |rho(x - s)| <= rho(x - Re s) exp(|Im s|^2 / 2) per variable. So H^#(Y; t) is analytic in t on the region where |Im s| <= c on Y, per cube, with **Re s unrestricted**, the bounds of stage (i) holding at real s, and there is **no shift of a contour and no cut in the way**: the old polymers chosen in the Mayer expansion, and the indicators, are functions of xi_eff, which is the integration variable. So on a polydisc for t, with radius rho_c = c exp(kappa_G d(c, Y) / 2) / (C_s(L) max(|xi'_c|, p)) for those c where this is above 1, the Mobius difference over a set X of cubes has a factor prod_{c in X} rho_c^{-1}: **decay in the sum of distances, by Cauchy, in a product form, which is right here since s is linear in xi' with a kernel that decays. A one-difference Lipschitz bound by the measure of a thin shell, which I tried first, gives only the farthest distance, and that does not sum over X.** So polymers are extended by a range (1 / kappa_G) log p, polylog in log 1/g. At complex W the same, with s(W). [Until this round this paragraph shifted xi near the inner cubes by a right inverse depending on Bad. Two readers broke that: old polymers made the integrand non-analytic in b, cuts on xi stood in the way of the shift, and the partition by Bad became circular. All three are gone.]

The same library then runs on xi', with cut p_4, a cube with |xi'_c| > p_4 counted as a bad cube, of influence radius (1 / kappa_G) log(|xi'_c| / p), mandatory in stage (i), and activity exp(-xi'^2 / 2) up to exp(theta |xi'_c|^2) away from a direct cube and up to exp((1/2) <xi', KK_eta xi'>) next to one, by Lemma 4.7b. The sum over those is a Gaussian integral with a margin c_4: the tails of KK_eta, exp(-kappa_G d), are below c_4 = c L^{-2}, since kappa_G >= 4 log L. *Several bad cubes of xi'.* Fix the set BB of cubes with |xi'_c| > p_4, and let I(BB) be the union of its influence regions. For fixed BB, cubes of I(BB) are put in Bad: each component is in one site of the gas, whose vacuum is b_hat = 0, since at xi = 0 the field would leave Dom through B' alone once |xi'_c| is above eps_0 / (g C(L)) [a reader's point]; off I(BB), |B'| <= C(L) g p, so the interface with such a vacuum stays in the analytic domain. There is no fine expansion in these cubes, the crude integral of Lemma 4.7b, and Lemma 4.7a with mandatory part Y_* containing I(BB). So at fixed xi' the stage (i) output is a sum over Y_* of K(Y_*; xi') exp(sum over Y disjoint from Y_* of H^#(Y; xi')), with no logarithm taken over a mandatory part, and with K and H^# analytic in xi' on the polydisc above for cubes c off I(BB). A connected Y_* carries the factor from each c in BB: exp(-(1/2 - theta) |xi'_c|^2), or exp(-c_4 |xi'_c|^2 / 2) next to a direct cube, against an entropy C(L, M)^{|I(c)|} with |I(c)| of the order of (log(|xi'_c| / p) / kappa_G)^4. Polylog against a square, so the sum over |xi'_c| > p converges, to at most C(L, M) exp(-c_4 p^2 / 4) per cube of BB. Overlaps of influence regions only reduce the entropy. So a union of bad cubes is a polymer of the same gas, and the sum over BB converges. [DERIVED at the level of this paragraph.]

*Output.* log(N / N_good)(W) = sum_Y e(Y; W), with polymers from the bad part. At W = 1 the same family gives the vacuum value. So EE^R_{k+1}(W) = sum_Y [ e(Y; W) - e(Y; 1) ], and it consists of: (1) new births, polymers containing a bad cube at this level, of size at most bb^new_k := C(L, M) ( exp(-c_6 / (2 g_k^2 r_2^4)) + exp(-p(g_k)^2 / 6) ), which is 1 / s_0 of stage (i), item 6; (2) terms linear in the old B_k + C_k, namely ebar(Y; W) - ebar(Y; 1), with ebar the expectation under the good measure; (3) products of old and new terms, smaller by a tiny factor. Term (2) is the one that could accumulate, and this is the point of the next lemma.

*Term (3), as an exact statement [DERIVED at the level of each step].* Let e_k := B_k + C_k, put a parameter u in front of it, and let nu_u be the normalised full measure at W, with all cube kinds and with u e_k in the exponent, and nu_good the normalised good measure with u = 0. Then

    log N(W; u = 1) - log N(W; u = 0) = int_0^1 du sum_Y E_{nu_u}[ e_k(Y; V) ],    and    term (2) := sum_Y E_{nu_good}[ e_k(Y; V) ].

So term (3) is sum_Y int du ( E_{nu_u} - E_{nu_good} )[ e_k(Y) ]. Both measures are given by convergent gases with Lemmas 4.7a, 4.7b and stage (ii), so the difference is at most sup |e_k(Y)| times the sum over clusters of a bad cube or an old polymer that reach Y, of its activity, with decay in the distance: at most C(L, M) sup |e_k(Y)| ( bb^new_k + bb_k + cc_k ) exp(-kappa d). **So in a term with a bad cube next to an old polymer there is nothing to separate: it is a born polymer, B_{k+1}, of bounded size, with no regularity claimed.** Only term (2), with the pure good measure, needs Lemma 4.7c. On V regular on Y, t_Y <= eps_0 M^2 / g, so cc_k (t^2 + t^3) is still below (cc_k)^{1/2}: the growth of C_k does no harm in these bounds. I had filed this as an assertion; the interpolation is the argument.

**Lemma 4.7c (irrelevance by Gaussian smoothing) [DERIVED at the level of each step].** Let e(Y; V) be a bounded function of V, measurable in V on N_1(Y), with the tail (TAIL) off it, invariant under gauge transformations, on a polymer Y of level-k cubes with |Y| links and diameter D. **No regularity of e on N_1(Y) is assumed.** In the proof Y means N_1(Y). *What failed.* I first made the zone with no regularity N_*(Y) := Y + (1 / c) log p, to match the expansion in stage (i). That would have made D grow with 1 / g, so that in the deep ultraviolet it exceeds L and the factor (D / L)^a is lost. The tube has to start at distance 1, and it does: at birth the contour shift works for any far W, and in this lemma far b_hat is real, so a complex W near Y moves the far field only inside the tube, whatever the size of the real noise there. Let ebar(W) := E_W[ e(Y; V) ] under the good measure at W. Let D be small against L, so that Y does not contain a whole tube. Then

    ebar(W) - ebar(1) = beta(e)(F_0^*, F_0^*) + Rem,     |Rem| <= C sup |e| ( t^3 + t^2 (D / L)^a ) + sup |e| exp(-p^2 / 3),   C absolute,

with F^* the curvature of V^*(W) on Y, F_0^* its value at a base site of Y, t := |F^*|_Y Vol(Y)^{1/2} / g, and beta(e) a quadratic form with |beta(e)(F_0^*, F_0^*)| <= C sup |e| t^2. The term t^2 (D / L)^a replaces F^*(x) F^*(x') by F_0^* F_0^* in the second-order term, using the Holder modulus of the lift, 9.4; I missed it in a first version. If |e| grows like (|F| / g)^3 the same holds with sup |e| replaced by its coefficient.

*Proof.* Take the radial gauge from a site of Y for V^*, so V^* = exp(i s) on Y, with s a smooth lift, single valued. Use the coordinate v, with V = exp(i v), so b = v - s + (commutators). Then ebar(W) = int dv D(v; W) e(exp(iv)) up to the removal of the cuts (the error exp(-p^2 / 3)), where D is the marginal density of v on Y. **All dependence on W is in D, which is smooth, and none is in e.** Under the good measure D(v; W) = rho(v - s) exp(small), with rho the Gaussian of covariance g^2 C_YY, and the smallness analytic. So D(v; W) / D(v; 0) = exp( <v, C_YY^{-1} s> / g^2 - <s, C_YY^{-1} s> / (2 g^2) ) up to small corrections. Here <s, C_YY^{-1} s> is the minimal energy of an extension of s off Y, by the Schur complement, so it is at most C |F^*|_Y^2 Vol(Y): a cutoff extension in the radial gauge on the ball of radius D has energy of that order, since |s| / D is of the order of |F^*|, and it fills the holes of Y, so Vol(Y) and the sup on the ball are right even for a thin Y with a loop, where the holonomy R^2 F costs F^2 R^4. Put t := |F^*|_Y Vol(Y)^{1/2} / g. If t >= 1 the claim is trivial, since sup |e| and beta(e) are at most C t^3 sup |e|, and this is the case |s| >> g, in which the supports of D(.; W) and D(.; 1) are far apart. If t <= 1, then |s| <= t g <= g. *The non-Gaussian part (MOM) in the two-noise chart [DERIVED at the level of each step].* Consider Gamma(W) := int dv D(v; W) e(v) as a function of W near Y, with W far from Y held fixed; here Y has side M, so it contains no tube, and restrictions of X to Y are all vectors on Y. **Use the strictly local noise to absorb the coordinate.** Let d := Y + range of P, inside which only xi moves. Change variables from xi_d to (b_hat_Y, xi_d^perp), with xi_d^perp in the kernel of P_{Y,d}. Replace the product cuts on d by the cuts |v| <= g p_3 on the real coordinate v and |xi_d^perp| <= p_3, with p_3 := C(L) p (error C(L, M) exp(-p^2 / 3), both events having probability that close to one). **The cuts cannot simply be removed: the chart ends at Dom, and Psi is defined only on small b. A cut on v, which does not involve W, is compatible with analyticity in W.** Then b_hat_Y = 2^{-1/2} g P_{Y,d} xi_d + B'_Y has, at fixed xi' and fixed xi off d, a Gaussian density with covariance (g^2 / 2) (P P^T)_{YY}, centred at B'_Y. The coordinate on Y is v = s(W) + b_hat_Y + NL, with NL of second order and local at block scale; after a gauge transformation on Y, the linear term d E h b, which is pure gauge on Y, is gone. At fixed xi', xi off d and xi_d^perp, the map from b_hat_Y to v is a near-identity diffeomorphism, with an analytic Jacobian, by the inverse function theorem, since |b_hat| <= C(L) g p. So

    D(v; W) = E_{xi', xi off d}[ cuts off d ; rho_d( b_Y(v, ...; W) - B'_Y(W) ) * J * exp(Psi) ] / N_good(W),

with rho_d that Gaussian, and **every factor is analytic in W for real v**, with no conditioning on a non-product event: the cuts off d are in real variables, unaffected by v. For complex W with t <= c, put y := Im( b_Y - B'_Y ). Then |rho_d(x + i y)| <= rho_d(x) exp( <y, ((1/2) P P^T)_{YY}^{-1} y> / (2 g^2) ). The form here is that of the massive compression: by P P^T = C_1^{1/2} (1 + Delta) C_1^{1/2} it is at most 2 (1 + 2 e) times the minimum over extensions in X of <b, (A + m^2) b>. For y from Im s, a cutoff extension in radial gauge gives at most C |Im F^*|_Y^2 Vol(Y) (1 + (D / L)^2), with C absolute, since m^2 = L^{-2} and D <= L. The imaginary part of B'(W) is smaller by a factor C(L) g p. So the Gaussian factor is at most e^{C c^2}, and |exp Psi| and J are at most 1 + small on the good variables. So |Gamma(W)| <= C sup |e| on that complex polydisc, **with C absolute, because t already contains |Y|**, and no separate moment of Psi is needed. The polydisc: W_zeta := exp(i zeta w) near Y in the radial gauge at its base site, joined by a cutoff to the fixed far field, |zeta| <= c / t. At the edge |zeta F'| <= c g L^2 / Vol(Y)^{1/2} << alpha, since gamma comes last, so W_zeta is in the class. Dependence on far curvature enters through F^* near Y, and t is read with the weight of (TAIL), in level-k units, as everywhere: t := sup_x exp(-c d(x, Y)) |F^*(x)| Vol(Y)^{1/2} / g. [Line had c / L here and c in (TAIL), a clash a reader noted: the far coarse curvature is in F^* on Y already, through the kernel K of the recursion below.] At zeta = 0 the field near Y is flat up to those tails, whose linear effect vanishes by colour. Cauchy gives the Taylor expansion of Gamma in the curvature at scale t. The zeroth order is ebar(1). The first order vanishes, since Gamma is invariant under gauge rotations of W and the only invariant linear term in the curvature would be a colour vector. The second order is a quadratic form in F^* on Y, at most C sup |e| t^2, and replacing F^* by F_0^* costs the Holder modulus. The third-order remainder is at most C sup |e| t^3.  []

*With growth [DERIVED].* Let |e(V)| <= cc (t_Y(V)^2 + t_Y(V)^3), with t_Y from (TAIL), and write t_* := t_Y(V^*(W)). Then

    | ebar(W) - ebar(1) - beta(e)(F_0^*, F_0^*) | <= C cc P(M) ( t_*^3 + t_*^2 (D / L)^a ),    P(M) := Vol(Y)^{3/2},

with beta(e) bounded by C cc P(M) t_*^2. *Proof.* F(V)_x = F^*_x + (D b_hat)_x + nonlinear, and the variance of (D b_hat)_x / g is at most ||D A^{-1} D^*|| <= C, so under the Gaussian, T := Vol(Y)^{1/2} sup_x exp(-c d(x, Y)) |(D b_hat)_x| / g has E[T^m] <= C_m Vol(Y)^{m / 2}, since the weights make the sup over x a sum over a bounded volume. So t_Y(V) <= C (t_* + T). The proof above used sup |e| only to bound |Gamma| on the polydisc. There |rho_d(x + i y)| <= e^{C c^2} rho_d(x), so |Gamma| <= C cc E[ (t_R + T)^2 + (t_R + T)^3 ], with t_R <= c the real part on the polydisc: at most C cc P(M). For t_* <= c, Cauchy gives the claim as before. For t_* > c, |ebar(W) - ebar(1)| <= C cc (t_*^2 + t_*^3 + P(M)) <= C cc P(M) t_*^3 / c^3, which is in the claim, and beta(e) is not extracted there beyond its bound. The vacuum value ebar(1), which is about cc P(M) and is not small relative to cc, cancels exactly in the ratio.  []

The condition that Y does not contain a tube matters: if it did, the marginal of v on Y would have a support depending on W. Such polymers have |Y| >= (L / M)^4 cubes, so they carry exp(-kappa (L / M)^4), which beats the factor L^4 with no regularity at all.

*The recursion, with the weighted t [written out in this round].* In the interior, F^*(x) = L^{-2} sum_{x'} K(x, x') F'_{x'}, with K of rate c_K > c in level-(k+1) units, Holder in x with exponent a, the same tails, and modulus (|x - x_0| / L)^a (9.4). So with t_Y and t'_{Y'} the weighted norms of (TAIL), and |Y| comparable to |Y'|, both of order M^4,

    t_Y <= C L^{-2} t'_{Y'} (g_{k+1} / g_k),

**far coarse curvature is inside t'_{Y'}, and it also carries the Holder factor,** since it enters F^* through the same kernel. So the risk I named, of far curvature with no factor (D / L)^a, does not occur. The tail of e adds to Gamma a second-order form sum_{x, x''} exp(-c d(x, Y) - c d(x'', Y)) F^*_x F^*_{x''} / (c' g)^2, by Cauchy on the tube, of the same order as t_Y^2 and with the same replacement of F^*_x by F_0^*, at the cost of an effective D of order 1 / c; it goes into beta(e). Evaluating on V^*(W) in a level-(k+1) cube of side M: |F^*| <= C_s L^{-2} sup |F'| with the weight, in the interior, and there are L^4 level-k cubes per coarse cube. *Marginal part.* By covariance, the sum over Y of beta(e)(F_0^*, F_0^*) is beta~ (1/2) sum_x |F^*_x|^2, up to the Holder modulus. **F^* is smooth at the level-k scale whatever W is, so by 11.1, Step 6, this is beta~ F_k(V^*(W)) = beta~ F_{k+1}(W), with a relative error L^{-a}.** [The upper chain notes that beta(e) depends on the position of Y in its block, through C_YY, so the covariance here is under T_{k+1} only. Accepted. beta~ is taken from the average over positions in a block. The rest is a form of mean zero over the block, against a field smooth at scale L, so it gains the same L^{-a}, and it goes into C_{k+1}. Derived at this level.] So beta~ goes into the coupling, exactly as a multiple of the classical functional, for any W, rough at its own scale or not: rho_k gains at most C bb_k / g^2, far below the bound of Proposition 8.4. So

    bb_{k+1} <= bb^new_k + C(L, M) ( exp(-p^2 / 3) + exp(-kappa (L / M)) L^4 ) (bb_k + cc_k) + (bb_k + cc_k)^2 C(L, M),
    cc_{k+1} <= C M^{10 + a} L^{-a} (bb_k + cc_k) (g_{k+1} / g_k)^3 + (from new births, one step later).

Here a < 1 is the Holder exponent of the lift in the interior of Om; an R polymer within distance L of a hole is attached to the mandatory part PP, so it needs no such gain. For a function that grows like t^2 + t^3 the bound is the one above, so the factor is C M^{10 + a} L^{-a} on t'^2 and C M^{10} L^{-2} on t'^3, with sum over the L^4 positions and t_Y <= C L^{-2} t'. Polymers containing a tube, or of diameter above L, have their extra factor exp(-kappa (L / M)) or smaller, and go into bb_{k+1}. With these factors at most 1/4, which holds since L >> M, this closes: bb_k + cc_k <= 2 sum_{j <= k} 4^{-(k - j)} bb^new_j, which is tiny, and summable along the flow by (T4). **So LF-count needs no analyticity near holes. The vacuum part cancels in the ratio, the part linear in the curvature vanishes by colour, the part quadratic in the curvature goes into the coupling, and the rest contracts by L^{-a} per level, only because the next fluctuation integral smooths at scale g whatever it integrates.** Note that this uses p-cut errors and tiny products, not any condition c beta > 4.

Why this is consistent at curvature much above g: then t^3 cc_k is large against cc_k, but cc_k is smaller than any power of g, and on regular fields |F^*| <= eps_0, so the term is small against F^2 / g^2.

*Hostile pass on 4.7.* (a) I tried to break Lemma 4.7c by a tube: handled by the size of such a Y. (b) A gauge-non-invariant e would have a first-order term: all R-polymers are invariant, jointly with the rotation of xi, by the argument of 11.4(b), since the hole indicator and the cuts are in invariant variables at fixed gauge average. I checked this only for the rotation of xi and for the hole indicator; the Feynman average over G_0 is invariant by construction. (c) Stage (ii) is at the level of each step, but its partition of cubes is the least checked item. (d) Lemma 4.7c assumes C_YY^{-1} bounded with the Schur complement form, at real W, and the small corrections in D are analytic only on good cubes; a polymer of old e overlapping a new bad cube is in term (3). (e) Complex w: the Gaussian has stiffness b_k complex, with |1 - w g^2| close to 1, so the moments in the proof change by a factor 1 + O(g^2), and all polymer bounds are modulus bounds on one level. The first-order term vanishes by colour at any w. Lemma 4.7a at complex w has complex f, and it uses only upper bounds on |exp f_Z|. *The a^{-4} check for term (2) [DERIVED at this level].* ebar^w(Y; W) is a ratio of integrals at the same w that differ only near Y, so by the convergent gas it is bounded by sup |e| times a factor per variable of Y, not of the volume. Directly: the marginal of b_hat on Y has stiffness b_k complex, |rho^w(v)| / rho^0(v) = |b g^2|^{dim / 2} exp(Re w <v, C_YY^{-1} v> / 2), which is 1 + O(rho g^2 (1 + |xi|^2)) per variable. No a^{-4} arises, as the far part of the Gaussian is normalised at the same w. (f) Complex data occur only in N_good[Om'], which has no holes, and in which every field is regular on Om. So the question of r_k at a complex field near a rough plaquette never arises. R-polymers near Lambda are attached to PP, so they need no contraction near corners. (g) N_good(W) uses only cuts in xi and xi'. Its complement contains configurations with no rough plaquette, and they have the mid or far activity. The chart domain Dom is sharp, but its edge is reached only by the gauge part of b, far above the good region.

**4.8 The step I(k) -> I(k+1) in order [an order-of-operations check; each item is at the label of the place it cites, and the new estimates are marked].** Fix real W, and let Lambda' := the union of the tiles of TT_{k+1} containing a cell that meets a rough plaquette of W, Om' its complement, H the refinement of Lambda', T the torus. **"Meeting", not "based in":** H must contain the hull of each rough plaquette, for the reason in item 6. Nothing below uses an item before it is available.

1. *Centre.* V^* := V^*(W; Om'), real, regular on the refinement of Om', with phi_* <= C L^{-sigma} eps_0. [Theorem G', 4.5i, 4.5h.] Same for a complex W in the class, for the analytic sector only.
2. *Chart on the refinement of Om'.* A, P, G_1, G_2, Dom, FP, built at the local centre V^# of 4.7, from the pulled-back Hessian. [(CUT) item 1, (COER-G), (WOOD), (STAB).] They depend on W and Om', and on nothing else.
3. *Partition.* Direct cubes: those meeting H, Lambda(V) or S(V). Insert the gauge device on the chart region, exactly, with N_0 constant. [(REL).]
4. *Sites.* Fix Bad. Components of Bad are sites. Define f by the hole read from direct links. [Sites of the gas, 4.7.]
5. *Old polymers.* B_k, C_k are Mayer factors. z_k are expanded in far cubes and far sites, with strict supports and in tree form. [(TAIL).]
6. *Excess, on the exterior of H [the budget, written out in this round].* By superadditivity, F_{k,Om(V)}(V) >= F_{k,Om(V) n H}(V) + F_{k,Om(V) \\ H}(V). In the first, F_{k,Om(V) n H}(V) >= c_A E_k(V; H). In the second the region is the refinement of Om' minus holes, with constraints on exactly the bonds of Om', and (4.3') applies with no loss from H. Y_k(V; H) is the sum over rough plaquettes meeting H, and Y_k(V; off H) the rest. **If H were only the cells based at rough plaquettes, part of a hull would lie outside H; its energy would be needed both for (4.1) and for F_{k+1,Om'}, and it cannot be used twice. A strong block outside H can be strong because of rough plaquettes inside H, and then (4.3') spends (c_F / 8) eps_0 Y_k of the part in H, once.** So the budget is: from z_k, c_F eps_0 Y_k(V); to the exterior, at most (c_F / 2) eps_0 Y_k(V; off H) + (c_F / 8) eps_0 Y_k(V). What is left on H is at least (7/8) c_F eps_0 Y_k(V; H), and with c_A E_k(V; H), by (4.1), it is at least (7/4) c_F eps_0 Y_{k+1}(W). What is left off H is at least (3/8 - C C_M L^{-2}) c_F eps_0 Y_k(V; off H), with the second term from (4.1), for rough plaquettes within D_T cells of H that a tile charges there, and this is what pays c_3 |H_Z| in Lemma 4.7b. Entropy, per cube of H and collar, takes at most (1/4) c_F eps_0 Y_{k+1}(W), by the count of Theorem 4.3. **So the exponent is -(3/2) c_F eps_0 Y_{k+1}(W) / g_k^2, and (T2) has g_{k+1}^{-2} <= (1 + C gamma^2) g_k^{-2}.** So the margin 3/2 against 1 is what is left for a complex w, in 6.1 item 7.
7. *Prefactors and integral.* c(eta) by Jacobi's minor, and the joint Gaussian with KK_eta. [Lemmas 4.4b, 4.7b.]
8. *Gas.* Stage (i), with crossing bonds (a) to (d), and with region dependence of F_{k,Om} and of EE^an_{k,Om}, in tree form, by Lemma 4.5k and I(k)(b). Stage (ii), with bad cubes of xi' as polymers. Output: N(W) / N_good[Om'](W) = sum over families PP' covering Lambda' of prod z(P'; W), times exp sum over Y disjoint from PP' of e(Y; W). The ratio at W = 1 has no PP'.
9. *Analytic sector.* N_good[Om'](W) for every region and for W in the class. (EI), one-loop term, Proposition 11.1, with the marginal part extracted: that defines beta_{k+1} and EE^an_{k+1,Om'}. Polymers wrapping a loop are paid by scale. [4.6, 11.1, 11.4(e), 8.3.]
10. *Assembly.* r_{k+1}(W) = N(W) / N(1) = [ N_good[Om'](W) / N_good[Om'](1) ] * [ N_good[Om'](1) / N_good[T](1) ] * [ (N / N_good[Om'])(W) / (N / N_good[T])(1) ]. The first factor is exp(-g_{k+1}^{-2} F_{k+1,Om'}(W) + EE^an_{k+1,Om'}(W)). The second is rho_H, with the comparison of Haar and Gaussian on H. The third is the gas, with e(Y; 1) in the denominator. For Y disjoint from PP' it pairs with e(Y; W). For Y meeting PP' it stays alone, and exp(-sum e(Y; 1)) has size at most exp(C (bb_k + cc_k) P(M) |PP'|), and it goes into z_{k+1}, with all factors of rho_H.
11. *R polymers of the next level.* Term (1), births, is bb^new. Term (2) is Lemma 4.7c; its marginal part goes into rho_k; its remainder is C_{k+1}. Term (3) is by interpolation, and goes into B_{k+1}. Polymers containing a tube go into B_{k+1}. [(4.7).]
12. *Flow.* (8.2), Proposition 8.4, with rho_k continuous in the couplings. [8.8(d).] This is the only place where the induction on k uses g_{k+1}.
13. *Check of I(k+1).* (a) Theorem G', with F_{k+1,Om'} the tropical minimum: (4.3) shows the slice minimum is the minimum over the fiber, among fields regular off rough cells. (b) item 9. (c) items 6, 8, 10, 11, with (TAIL) at birth, and Col_{k+1} := rough cells of P' + r_2 cubes. (d) by covariance at each step.

*The base case.* I(0): F_{0,Om} = A on Om, EE = 0, no B, C. r_0(V) = exp(-A / g_0^2), and G_0(V) = prod over clusters of Lambda(V) of z_0, with z_0(P; V) := exp(-A_P(V) / g_0^2), where A_P is the action of the plaquettes of the cell union P that are not in Om(V). Since 1 - (1/2) tr U = f^2 / 2, A_P >= (1/2) sum over rough p of f_p^2 >= (1/2) eps_0 Y_0(V; P), and c_F <= 1/2. Strict support, so (TAIL) is trivial.

*Hostile pass on this list.* I wrote it to look for something used before it exists, and I found three things, now fixed: H had to be the cells meeting a rough plaquette, not based at one (item 6); the region dependence of EE^an was used in crossing bonds but not stated in I(k)(b) (item 8); a strong block next to H spends the part of Y_k that lies in H, which the budget had not counted (item 6). The margin went from 7/4 to 3/2. I looked for a circularity in beta: polymers at level k use g_0, ..., g_k only, and g_{k+1} appears only in the next step's chart, so there is none. I did not find a use of the complex class outside the analytic sector.

**4.9 What real w gives: a probe, requested by the target lead [the result is negative for the induction; each step below is derived at this level].** Under UV-OS nothing consumes complex w. My failed-route list said that real-only arguments for the R sector, positivity, Peierls and Dobrushin, took one line at real w, since Xi(Z) / Xi(Z \\ c) lies in [1, 1 + z]. I tested that line, and two larger versions of it. **It does not shorten the gas, (TAIL), or the large-field sector at a rough exterior, and the line in that list was naive. For term (2) alone a real version is untested: see Test 2.**

*Test 1: the conditional activity.* At w = 0, N(W) is a sum over families of sites of N[B; W] >= 0, the integral with Bad = B. The line needs N[B u {C}; W] <= a(C) N[B; W] uniformly in B. Numerator and denominator then share the far sites of B, so the bound is on G_k(V_in, V_far) / G_k(V'_in, V_far), with V_far rough. I(k)(c) gives G_k at a rough field as a signed sum over families, from above only. **A ratio of two such sums at a common rough exterior is not bounded by anything I have.** By exact positivity it reduces, sup over the exterior at each level, to ratios of integrals with frozen fine data on a boundary, at all levels below. That is an induction with frozen faces, the label D that the upper chain asks about. It is not cheaper than the gas, and the gas does not need it: the gas never divides by the sum. *What positivity does give at once:* N(W) >= N_g(W) and the lower bound by single sites, and the identity that the far part of the bound on a ratio is exact when the far part is regular everywhere. For W regular everywhere, sizes follow from I(k), with no new argument.

*Test 2: a carried class with no polymers.* Carry q_k := log G_k on regular V as one global function. Since it is positive and smoothed, the hope is to control E_W[q_k] - E_1[q_k] by real derivatives or by differences, with no expansion in far variables, so no tree and no labels. Its local pieces could come from telescoping -log P_W(Good) over cubes, in any order, so also symmetrically, and all of this is real. (a) *Sup norm:* the density grows by L^4 per level, against activities exp(-c k log L) or exp(-(log k)^{2 p_0}), which is LF-count, and it loses. (b) *First differences of q_k, cube by cube:* a coupling of E_W and E_W' has disagreement probability min(1, |s_c|) at a cube, with |s_c| of order L^{-2} t', so the output at a coarse cube is of order L^2 t' y against the t'^2 y needed, for 1 < t' < L^2. (c) *Taylor in the white noise, up to third order, with no regularity* [DERIVED; a reader checked it]: for a product Gaussian, the third derivative in a shift s of E[f(xi + tau s)] is a sum over three cubes of prod |s_{c_i}| times a bounded Hermite moment times the mixed difference of f, with lower-order differences when cubes repeat. So with y_1, y_2, y_3 the sums of mixed differences of orders 1, 2, 3, and s uniform, the output has marginal part y_2 t'^2 and remainder L^{-2} y_3 t'^3. **This cannot be the whole class, since a change of W is not a shift of the noise:** the variation of the lift at a bond is a tangent vector plus a part of mean one on the crossing links of the bond, which no noise can realise. *The tube function.* Let f(V) := y g(W(dP)(V)), with W(dP) the holonomy of a coarse plaquette, built from the means over four tubes, so gauge invariant, and g of unit size and width of order g_k L. On the fiber it is constant, so its white-noise differences vanish identically, while E_W[f] = y g(W(dP)), with no smoothing at all. Read in field moves, a link of a tube has weight up to L^{-3}, and differences of every bounded order are of order y L^{-4}, up to powers of M, so differences of bounded order miss the output by a power of L. **But a first-order clause with distance sees a tube** [a reader]: if q = sum_c q_c with |delta_c' q_c| <= y' exp(-kappa d(c, c')), then second differences decay with distance, and a tube has amplitude at most y' L^6 exp(-kappa L / (2 M)), up to powers of M. And by I(k)(c) a true tube term is at most bb_k exp(-kappa (L / M)^4). So the function is not relevant at a comparable size, and what it shows is only that such a clause cannot be dropped. **So real differences with a distance weight are a candidate for term (2) and Lemma 4.7c, in place of Cauchy there. That is untested. They do not replace the gas, because of Test 1, and they give no shortening there.**

*Test 3: large field by a surgery map.* Compare N(W) with N(W'), for W rough on U' and W' regular, by a Peierls map on clusters with a good ring. The bound sup over the exterior of the ratio of inner integrals is too weak at an atypical exterior, by classical terms at the boundary, and the map with a ring repairs that. It needs the relative form of Test 1 at the inner field. So it gives what Theorem 4.3 gives, at the price of that form, and no more unless that form is had.

*What survives.* (i) Text 6, I^w and the phase remarks are optional under UV-OS. (ii) On the cut, the good law is uniformly log-concave in xi, by the Schur test on (EI), so at W regular everywhere covariance decay holds by Helffer and Sjostrand with no cluster expansion: a second proof of what the library gives, not a new input. (iii) **Complex W is used in this file in three places: N_good, the marginal density of Lemma 4.7c, and (TAIL). In none is r_k evaluated at a complex field near a rough plaquette, and none needs complex w.** (TAIL) is Cauchy in far variables outside the analytic sector, so the upper chain's description of complex W as confined to the analytic sector is not exact.

*The one statement that would change this, and is open:* a relative large-field bound at arbitrary far data, r_k(V) <= r_k(V') exp( -g^{-2} [ F(V) - F(V') ] - c_F eps_0 Y_k(V; U) / g^2 + small |U| ), for V, V' equal off U, V' regular on U and near it, the far field arbitrary. It would make G_k positive in the bookkeeping, remove the sum over families at rough fields, and give (SCR_j), (DEC_j), (RAR_j) at rough tops by the telescoping in outward order. It is an induction with frozen faces. It is not needed for UV-CT.

### 5. The field inequality (OS) [DERIVED except where marked]

**5.1 A change in the average: waypoints.** The prefix of 1.2 averages x over line(u), which depends on the direction mu. Termwise that leaves a star through y
whose cancellation is only approximate at large field. Replace it, keeping every other line of Section 1:

    Psi_y^mu(z) = Mean over x in B(y), and over axial orders, of [ path(y -> x) * R_mu(x, z) ],     a_u = Psi_y^mu(u^-),     b_u = Psi_{y'}^mu(u^+)^{-1},

with R_mu(x, z) first moving transversely from x to (z_perp, x_mu), and then straight along mu to z (reversed for the suffix). Everything is interior, so each crossing link is still private, so
Lemma 1.1, Corollary 1.2 and Lemma 2.1 hold unchanged. The mean over x and orders is one flat mean, with no inner normalisation, and the x set is all of B(y) for every direction.

*Linearisation.* QA(c') = h(y) - h(y') + avg_u [ avg_x A(R_mu(x, u^-)) + A(u) + avg_{x'} A(R_mu(u^+, x')) ]. The transverse legs cancel in the mean: x_perp and u_perp are independent and identically distributed, and swapping them reverses the path while the reversed order is also in the family, so the expected signed use of a transverse link is exactly zero.
A longitudinal link at (xi_mu, xi_perp) in B(y) is used when u_perp = xi_perp and x_mu <= xi_mu, with probability L^{-3} (xi_mu + o + 1) / L, and likewise in B(y'), which is the triangular tube weight of the paired-contour average. **So Q is exactly the Q of (R4), and RENEW's (R5), (R7) with their sharp constants apply.** [Derived.] In particular constant curvature is reproduced exactly with no factor per level. The check script as first written tests the earlier line-averaged map. **Obligation:** rerun the check on this map.

**5.2 Exact identity.** For unit quaternion families with means m_i = |m_i| M_i, and independent indices,

    |m_1| |m_2| |m_3| |m_4| * M_1 M_2 M_3^{-1} M_4^{-1} = avg_{u_1, u_2, u_3, u_4} T^{(1)}_{u_1} T^{(2)}_{u_2} (T^{(3)}_{u_3})^{-1} (T^{(4)}_{u_4})^{-1},          (5.1)

because quaternion multiplication is multilinear and (avg T)^* = avg T^*. **For the map of 5.1 and 5.4 the T_u contain the normalised Psi, so the terms on the right are not holonomies, and expanding Psi puts 1 / |m_z| inside the average.** So (5.2) below is for the line-averaged map of an earlier version only, and it is kept as a record; Steps 1 to 5 of Theorem 5.5 do not use it. [A reader noted this. I had it listed as the input of (5.5).] On a family where each term is the holonomy Gamma of a closed loop, since the left side is a positive multiple of a unit quaternion,

    |W(dP) - 1| <= (2 / prod_i |m_i|) avg |Gamma - 1|.                                                                                     (5.2)

(If Re W >= 0 then |W - 1| <= sqrt 2 |Im W|, and Im W is the average of Im Gamma over the product of the |m_i|. If Re W < 0 then avg Re Gamma < 0, so avg |Gamma - 1| >= 1.) This holds at every field with all m_i != 0.

**5.3 Two elementary facts.** (i) For a flat mean with reference h a unit quaternion: |m - h| <= D := avg |g_i - h|, so 1 - |m| <= D and |Mean - h| <= 2 D, **with no lower bound on |m|**, whenever m != 0. (ii) For nonzero a, b in R^4: |a/|a| - b/|b|| <= 2 |a - b| / |b|.

**5.4 The map, final form (D3).** Call a plaquette of a block *rough* if f_p > eps_0. For a site x_0 in B(y) and a plaquette p in the (alpha, beta) plane at site q, with gamma, delta the other two axes, put W_0(p; x_0) := 1[q_gamma = x_{0,gamma}, q_delta = x_{0,delta}] + L^{-1} (1[q_gamma = x_{0,gamma}] + 1[q_delta = x_{0,delta}]), and s(x_0) := sum over rough p in B(y) of f_p W_0(p; x_0). **The weight f_p is necessary, and W_0 is the true weight of a star of axial paths.** Put

    Psi_y^mu(z) = path(y -> x_0) * Mean over x in B(y), and over axial orders, of [ R(x_0, x) R_mu(x, z) ],

with x_0 = y if s(y) <= s_rule, an absolute constant, and otherwise x_0 a minimiser of s; ties and the axial order of path(y -> x_0) are decided by uniform choice in the kernel, a finite mixture of hard constraints, so the kernel is exactly covariant under the lattice symmetries. All of this depends on interior links only, so the crossing links stay private and Lemma 1.1, Corollary 1.2, Lemma 2.1 hold for each element of the mixture. On fields with no rough plaquette this is the map of 5.1, with linearisation (R4). A regular field has f_p <= eps_0, so s = 0 and x_0 = y, and the rule does nothing there. The rule reads the rough plaquettes of the block, whatever they are, so analyticity is used only on the chart.

*Why the rule.* A defect of fixed size next to y can make the mean over stars small. Rotating the first links out of y in directions +-e_0, +-e_1 by -1 changes the sign of half of all paths from y. Numerics, TUNED2 in `checks/hard-core--independent_field_ineq.py`: with x_0 = y fixed, min |m| is 0.001 to 0.012, and with the rule it stays near 0.56 to 0.73. *First version of the rule was wrong:* it used a separate absolute threshold eps_rule >> eps_0. Then about 1 / eps_rule plaquettes just below eps_rule, next to x_0, make the star disperse with l^1 weight of order one, which L^{-2} ||f||_1 does not see. So the threshold must be eps_0. *Second version also wrong, found on this pass:* s was an unweighted count of rough plaquettes. Then many plaquettes barely above eps_0 elsewhere in the block can make the minimiser of the count sit next to a large defect, where the star disperses, although the total l^1 weight is small; and the line in Step 5, that the minimum is at most the average C L^{-2} sum f_p, is false for a count, by a factor 1 / eps_0. *Third error, same pass:* I had written the star weight as min(1, dist(p, x_0)^{-2}), as for straight rays. Axial paths from x_0 run in coordinate planes through x_0, so a plaquette in such a plane has weight of order 1 at any distance: the true weight is W_0. A sum of W_0 over p is C L^2, and its square sum is of order L^2, not log L. With W_0 and f_p, Step 5 holds, more simply than before; see there. The earlier numerics tested an unweighted isotropic rule at threshold 0.5, so they have not tested this form.

**Theorem 5.5 (field inequality) [DERIVED; constants not computed].** For every real V, every coarse plaquette P, and every split of the hull plaquettes into irr, reg such that irr contains the rough ones,

    |W(dP) - 1| <= C_OS ( L^{-2} ||f||_{l^1(irr)} + ||f||_{l^2(reg)} ),    C_OS absolute.                                                       (5.5)

*Proof.* Step 1 (common factor). Fix a block y, the x_0 of the rule, and a second site x' used only in the analysis. Let omega index x with the axial orders of its legs and an axial path pi_omega from x to x', with an independent uniform order. Put Xi_omega = R_omega(x_0, x) pi_omega, rho_omega(z) = pi_omega^{-1} R_{omega,mu}(x, z), both unit quaternions. (An earlier wording used a flat mean over orders for R(x, x') and its inverse. That inverse is not a conjugate. Averaging the identity over the path pi fixes this.) Then R_omega(x_0, x) R_{omega,mu}(x, z) = Xi_omega rho_omega(z), with **Xi_omega independent of z and of mu**. With rho(z) := rho_{omega_1}(z) for a reference omega_1, and d(z) := avg_omega |rho_omega(z) - rho(z)|, we have m_z = Xi_bar rho(z) + e, |e| <= d(z), so by (ii), |Psi(z) - star Xi_hat rho(z)| <= 2 d(z) / |Xi_bar|, where star = path(y -> x_0) and Xi_hat = Xi_bar / |Xi_bar|. So if |Xi_bar| >= 1/4 the error is at most 8 d(z).

Step 2 (coarse link). T_u = Psi_y(u^-) u Psi_{y'}(u^+)^{-1} = (star Xi_hat)_y tau_u (star Xi_hat)_{y'}^{-1} up to at most 8 (d_y(u^-) + d_{y'}(u^+)), where tau_u = rho_y(u^-) u rho_{y'}(u^+)^{-1}. By covariance of the mean and (i), with M_tilde = conjugate of M by the common factors: |M_tilde - tau_{u_1}| <= 16 avg_u (d_y + d_{y'}) + 2 avg_u |tau_u - tau_{u_1}|, for every u_1. **The common factors cancel exactly from W(dP).**

Step 3 (loops). |W(dP) - 1| <= |tau_{u_1} tau_{u_2} tau_{u_3}^{-1} tau_{u_4}^{-1} - 1| + sum_i |M_tilde_i - tau_{u_i}|. Each term on the right is the distance from 1 of a holonomy of a closed polygon, directly or after averaging, and non-abelian Stokes gives |holonomy - 1| <= sum_{p in surface} f_p for any spanning surface. So |W(dP) - 1| <= C sum_p w_p f_p, where w_p is the probability, over the random vertices, that p is in the surface.

Step 4 (weights, (5.4)). Vertices of the polygons: x, x_1, x' (uniform on blocks), and z = u^-, whose transverse coordinates are uniform. z is reached and left along mu, on one line, so those two segments merge and z_mu is not a vertex coordinate. So every vertex coordinate that remains is a uniform variable, indexed by (vertex, axis), and independent for different indices. Fill an axial polygon with a bounded number C_d of axis-parallel rectangles, each of side at most 2 L, with each of the two perpendicular coordinates equal to one such variable. They have different axes, hence are independent. So a fixed plaquette is in a given rectangle with probability at most L^{-2}. Averaging over x' uniform gives

    w_p <= C_w L^{-2},   sum_p w_p <= C_w L^2,  C_w depending on d only,                                                                       (5.4)

and Cauchy-Schwarz on reg gives sum_p w_p f_p <= C_w L^{-2} ||f||_{l^1(irr)} + C_w ||f||_{l^2(reg)}. The average over x' may be restricted to the x' with |Xi_bar| >= 1/4; if those are at least half of each of the two blocks, w_p grows by at most a factor 4.

Step 5 (when |Xi_bar| is small). For a block, |Xi_bar| >= 1 - avg_{omega, omega_2} |Xi_omega - Xi_{omega_2}| >= 1 - sum_p w^*_p f_p, with w^*_p the weight of the polygon x_0, x, x', x_2. Rectangles of its filling have perpendicular coordinates among those of x_0, x', which are fixed, and x, x_2, which are uniform. So w^*_p <= C ( W_0(p; x_0) + W_0(p; x') + 1[q_gamma = x_{0,gamma}, q_delta = x'_delta] + L^{-2} ). Averaged over x', the mixed term is L^{-1} 1[q_gamma = x_{0,gamma}], which is a term of W_0(p; x_0), and everything else except W_0(p; x_0) is at most C L^{-2}. So if the x' with |Xi_bar| < 1/4 are more than half of the block, then

    (3/4)(1/2) <= C s(x_0) + C sum over p with f_p <= eps_0 of W_0(p; x_0) f_p + C L^{-2} sum_p f_p.

The middle term is at most C eps_0 sum_p W_0(p; x_0) <= C eps_0 L^2 = C c_1, **which is small because eps_0 L^2 = c_1 is small, with no use of the l^2 norm, so no logarithm appears.** By the rule, either s(x_0) <= s_rule, or s(x_0) = min s, at most the average of s over x_0, which is at most 3 L^{-2} sum over rough p of f_p, since the average of W_0(p; x_0) over x_0 is at most 3 L^{-2}. So for c_1, s_rule small against the left side, L^{-2} ||f||_{l^1} >= c. Since the hull has at most C L^4 plaquettes, L^{-2} ||f||_{l^1(reg)} <= C ||f||_{l^2(reg)}. So the right side of (5.5), with C_OS a large absolute constant, is at least the trivial bound |W(dP) - 1| <= 2.  []

*Status.* All five steps are derived here. The rectangle filling in Step 4 is by swaps [written out in this round]: in a closed polygon of axial paths with m vertices, swap adjacent segments along different axes until the segments along each axis between consecutive vertices are adjacent and merge. A swap of segments along alpha and beta sweeps one rectangle in the (alpha, beta) plane, whose two perpendicular coordinates are coordinates of vertices. The number of swaps is bounded by C(m, d). At the end the polygon is a path that retraces itself. No explicit value of C_OS is available, but it is absolute. Theorem 5.5 needs L^2 >> C_OS O (1 + C_M) to feed Theorem 4.2, which only asks for L large. *Hostile:* Step 5 needs eps_0 L^2 small, so this inequality is a statement for the chosen eps_0. And its first form had C_OS = C log L from an l^2 estimate on the star that was wrong; the first form of the conclusion was harmless, but its proof was not valid.

*Numerics, under a confirmed lock.* On the earlier rule, L = 3, 5: PASS-55, with max Ratio 1.000, 0.999, in the SHEET family. CHARGE is exact to 2e-16. On the final rule (weighted, W_0, eps_0 = 0.05), L = 3: PASS-55, max Ratio 1.000 in SHEET, CHARGE exact; TUNED2 min |m| 0.56 with the rule against 0.005 to 0.012 fixed. Adversarial hill climbing, L = 3, 300 moves per start: PASS-SEARCH, best Ratio 1.000, from the sheet start, never improved; from flat 0.041, from tuned about 0.02. **That search is weak**: single-link moves, accepted only if Ratio grows, and it found nothing above the sheet. COUNTTRAP, L = 5, three seeds, criteria stated first: TRAP-CONFIRMED. The old count rule puts x_0 on the defect in all three seeds, with min |m| 0.023, 0.069, 0.038, against 0.676, 0.675, 0.666 with the weighted rule. However, |W - 1| differs only by factors 1.9, 1.2, 1.1, and Ratio is about 0.001 for both, since noise inflates the right side. So this confirms that the old rule picks a bad base point and that |m| becomes small. It does not exhibit a violation of (5.5) by the old rule. At eps_0 = 0.01, L = 3: PASS-55, max Ratio 1.000, PASS-SEARCH, best Ratio 1.000. L = 5, eps_0 = 0.05: PASS-55, max Ratio 0.999 against 15.22, CHARGE exact to 2e-16. TUNED2 at L = 5: min |m| 0.73 with the rule against 0.001 to 0.009 fixed. SEARCH2, L = 3, eps_0 = 0.01, coordinated moves on sheets, lines and patches, 4 restarts of 500 moves: from random starts best 0.028, far below the sheet, so it says little; from the sheet of Ratio 1 at low temperature, best exactly 1.000, no move raised it. (The summary line printed by that run reports the wrong best, because a start value was not recorded; the per-restart lines are correct, and the script is fixed.) So max Ratio observed is 1 on every family and search. **This is evidence only: small L, and the map is not differentiated or optimised by gradient.** It does suggest that C_OS is not large on fields of this kind.

### 6. What this gives the interface UV-CT

The lead replaced UV-EXT by UV-CT (`parts/upper-chain.md`, Text 7, read for its statement only, as the request directs). The reason: a constant real shift cannot feed the chain. I take the statement as given and do not read anything behind its wording.

**6.1 Induction with a local source: one step [DERIVED term by term; each term is at the level of the corresponding small-field estimate].** Let the bare coupling be beta - w(p), with w complex, |w| <= rho, Lipschitz at the physical scale a_*, constant Lam. All levels k <= K have a_k <= a_K, a fixed fraction of a_*, so Lam a_k <= 1 and is summable in k. Density recursion: f_{0,p} = s_p, f_{k+1,x'}(W) := sum_{x in B(x')} f_{k,x}(V^*(W)), with V^* the source-free lift, so sum_x f_{k,x} = F_k, and these densities are bounded by Theorem G'.

Hypothesis I^w(k), on Om = Om(V):

    r_k^w(V) = exp( -g_k^{-2} F_{k,Om}(V) + sum_x w_k(x) f_{k,x}(V) + EE_k^w(V) ) G_k^w(V),

with G_k^w in the gas form of 4.7, which is not a logarithm: it may vanish at complex w, and no logarithm of it is taken.

Set V = exp(iB) V^*(W). Then, term by term:

1. *B = 0.* -g_k^{-2} F_{k+1}(W) + sum_{x'} w_k(x') f_{k+1,x'}(W) + sum_{x'} sum_{x in B(x')} (w_k(x) - w_k(x')) f_{k,x}(V^*). The last sum is a polymer of size C Lam a_{k+1} per coarse cell times the local action: an irrelevant term with gain a_{k+1}, summable in k.
2. *First order in B.* -g_k^{-2} dF_k . Z vanishes on the slice. And sum_x w_k(x) df_{k,x} . Z: for constant w it is w dF_k . Z, which also vanishes. So only (w_k(x) - wbar) df_{k,x} . Z survives, of size Lam a_k (local |F|) |Z|, with Z of order g p: **a source does not move the background**, up to terms that are small here.
3. *Second order in B.* -(1/2)(g_k^{-2} - wbar) <Z, H Z> + (gradient term). Writing it as a Gaussian at w = 0 plus a perturbation of size rho g^2 p^2 per polymer, H_0 in (EI) gains a term C rho g^2 p^2, which is small.
4. *One-loop with source.* For constant w the Gaussian with stiffness b = g^{-2} - w has determinant b^{-dim/2} (det H_L)^{-1/2}. **So the W-dependence of the one-loop term is the same as at w = 0, for constant w.** In perturbation: the term of order g^2 w is (1/2) g^2 w Tr[C(W) H_L(W)], and Tr[C H_L] = dim, independent of W. This settles a point I had left open: there is no term of order g^2 w in the marginal part. The W-independent part, n log(1 - w g_k^2) per variable, is the term of E at this level. For Lipschitz w the remainder has a gain Lam a_k.
5. *Higher orders.* H^# of 4.6, as a function of b. Analytic in b on a disc of radius 1 / (2 g^2) around g^{-2}, since g enters only through b^{-1/2}. By Cauchy, d/dw of a quantity of size S is at most C g^2 S. The marginal part of H^# has size at most C(L, M) g p^3, by size alone (Proposition 11.1), so its derivative in w is at most C g^3 p^3. Parity would improve this to g^4, and it is not needed.
6. *Flow.* Extract the marginal part of e_{k+1} by 11.1, polymer by polymer, at each x'. Then b_{k+1}(x') = b_k(x') - beta_{k+1}(b_k(x')) + (gradient, gain a_k), with beta_{k+1}(b) = b_* log L + (d_k - d_{k+1}) + rho_k(b), b_* the coefficient of 8.2, where the first two terms do not depend on b, by item 4. So |w_{k+1}(x) - w_k(x)| <= C g_k^3 p^c |w_k(x)| + C Lam a_k. By (T4), sum_k g_k^3 p(g_k)^c is finite and small with gamma, so **|w_k| <= 2 rho for all k, uniformly in K**, for gamma small and a_K small. Lipschitz constant: the same recursion.
7. *Large field.* Stiffness Re b_k >= (1 - 2 rho gamma^2) / g_k^2, and (4.1) is about real V. So Theorem 4.3 holds with the factor 3/2 replaced by (3/2)(1 - 2 rho gamma^2), still above 5/4. (The first version wrote 1 / (2 g^2) here, which would have used up the whole margin; the stiffness is much better than that.)

*Two sectors with a source (4.7).* The analytic sector is N_good with stiffness field b_k, analytic in w on the polydisc; the far part of the complex Gaussian is never put under a modulus, since the chart is real at w = 0 and the term (1/2) w g^2 |xi|^2 is in Psi, so it is expanded, and its mean is in E(w) exactly. The R sector at complex w uses Lemma 4.7a, which only needs upper bounds on |exp f_Z|, so a modulus is taken only on the cubes of a polymer. Its polymers at W = 1 are in E(w) and are analytic in w, with non-vanishing by the library. So E(w) contains the vacuum part of LF-count, and it may be large, of order a^{-4}; only its presence in CT1 matters, and in CT3 a polymer that meets two supports still has the factor exp(-kappa' eta / a_k). In Lemma 4.7c at complex w, the marginal density is complex with modulus at most the real density times 1 + O(g^2), per variable of Y, by the same local cancellation.

*Complex w and phases.* The modulus must never be taken through more than one level: |rho^w(1)| is smaller than rho^{Re w}(1) by a factor exp(c a^{-4} (Im w)^2 g^4), so a modulus bound at the bare level would lose an amount of order a^{-4}. In the step k -> k+1 the modulus is taken only over level-k variables of frozen cubes, and comparing Haar with the Gaussian at complex b_k costs |1 - w g_k^2|^{-n/2} per cube, which is 1 + O(g^2). Finer levels in that cube are already integrated, analytically in w, inside r_k^w. So there is **no loss of order a^{-4}**: this is the phase cancellation the gate asks about, and it follows from the level-by-level structure.

**6.2 The counterterm.** E(w) := log rho_K^w(1) - log rho_K^0(1), the source-dependent constrained partition function with the coarse field trivial, which is the named subtraction of (1.2). It is a convergent sum over levels of polymers at W = 1, so it is analytic and non-zero on the polydisc. Averaging over the lattice symmetries of Tor, E_sym(w) := avg_g E(w o g).
